x\in\operatorname{{Spa}}(A/\pi)
and let
\operatorname{{Spa}}(k(x),k(x)^{+})\to\operatorname{{Spa}}(A/\pi)
the affinoid residue field map.
Let
\mathfrak{p}_{x}\in{\operatorname{Spec}}(A/\pi)
denote the support ideal of
x
.
Since
A
is integrally closed in
A[\pi^{-1}]
, the pair
(A[\pi^{-1}],A)
is a complete Tate Huber pair and we have a surjective specialization map
\operatorname{sp}_{A}\colon\operatorname{{Spa}}(A[\pi^{-1}],A)\to{\operatorname{Spec}}(A/\pi)
by
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.2]
,
[
Bha17
, Remark 7.4.12]
.
Let
y\in\operatorname{{Spa}}(A[\pi^{-1}],A)
with
\operatorname{sp}_{A}(y)=\mathfrak{p}_{x}
.
We obtain a map
\operatorname{{Spa}}(R[p^{-1}],R)\to\operatorname{{Spa}}(A[\pi^{-1}],A)
with
R:=k(y)^{+}
. The residue field of
R
is
k(x)
and we can consider
R^{+}\subset R
defined as
R\times_{k(x)}k(x)^{+}
.
This promotes to a map
\operatorname{{Spa}}(R^{+})\to\operatorname{{Spa}}(A)
.
As
\operatorname{{Spd}}(B[\pi^{-1}],B)\to\operatorname{{Spd}}(A[\pi^{-1}],A)
is a v-cover we can find a v-cover of
\operatorname{{Spa}}(C,C^{+})\to\operatorname{{Spa}}(R[\pi^{-1}],R^{+})
with
(C,C^{+})
a perfectoid field and a commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{Spa}}(C^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{Spa}}(R^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{Spa}}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{Spa}}(A).}

The map
\operatorname{{Spa}}(C^{+})\to\operatorname{{Spa}}(R^{+})
is easily seen to be surjective since it is an extension of valuation rings.
So
x
lies in the image of
\operatorname{{Spa}}(B/\pi)
as we needed to show.

That
\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)
is a kimberlite for
A
as above follows from
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.23]
. Now, we may always find a cover by an affinoid perfectoid space
\operatorname{{Spd}}(P,P^{+})\to\operatorname{{Spd}}(A[p^{-1}],A)
by
[
Sch17
, Lemma 15.3]
. What we have shown so far implies that
\operatorname{{Spd}}(P^{+})\to\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)
is also a v-cover. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 2.32
.

If
A
is the
\pi
-adic completion of a flat and finite type algebra over
O
, then
\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)
is a flat
\pi
-adic kimberlite.

Proof.

We may assume that
A
is reduced as passing to the absolute weak normalization does not change
\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)
by
Theorem

2.16
and
Proposition

2.18
. As
A
is noetherian and quasi-excellent, the integral closure of
A
in its total ring of fractions is therefore a finite
A
-module. In particular, the integral closure
A^{\prime}
of
A
in
A[p^{-1}]
is finite over
A
. Thus, we can conclude that
\operatorname{{Spd}}(A^{\prime})
(with
A^{\prime}
given the
\pi
-adic topology) is flat by
Proposition

2.31
and the map
\operatorname{{Spd}}(A^{\prime})\to\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)
is a v-cover since it is isomorphism over
\operatorname{{Spd}}(F)
(this uses that the
\pi
-adic topology on
A^{\prime}
agrees with the subspace topology on
A[\pi^{-1}]
) and the map
{\operatorname{Spec}}(A^{\prime}/\pi)\to{\operatorname{Spec}}(A/\pi)
is proper and surjective (here we use again
[
Gle20
, Lemma 1.3.5, Proposition 1.3.8]
as in
Proposition

2.31
).
∎

Remark 2.33
.

A careful inspection of the proof of
Proposition

2.31
above allows us to conclude that a
\pi
-adic formal Huber pair
(A,A)
will give rise to a flat
\pi
-adic kimberlite
\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)
if and only if the specialization map

(2.22)

\operatorname{sp}_{A}\colon\{x\in\operatorname{{Spa}}(A)\mid|\pi|_{x}\neq 0\}\subset\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)\to{\operatorname{Spec}}(A/\pi)

is surjective.
The hypothesis taken in
Proposition

2.31
are easy to verify assumptions that ensure this happens.
Without assuming flatness of
A
, this might not hold since for a discrete and perfect
O
-algebra
A
in characteristic
p
, the v-sheaf
\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)
is a
\pi
-adic kimberlite that is not flat.

We can relate flatness for
\pi
-adic kimberlites to surjectivity of the specialization map.

Proposition 2.34
.

Let
X
be a
\pi
-adic kimberlite over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
.

(1)

If
X
is flat, then the specialization map
\operatorname{sp}\colon|X_{\eta}|\to|X^{\operatorname{red}}|
is surjective.

(2)

Conversely, if
X\to\operatorname{{Spd}}O
is proper and
\operatorname{sp}\colon|X_{\eta}|\to|X^{\operatorname{red}}|
surjective, then
X
is flat over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
.

Proof.

The first statement reduces to the case
X=\operatorname{{Spd}}(R_{i}^{\sharp+})
for
(R_{i}^{\sharp},R_{i}^{\sharp+})
a perfectoid Huber pair over
F
, where it follows from
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.2]
. Let us prove the second.
It follows from the hypothesis that
X_{\eta}
is quasi-compact over
\operatorname{{Spd}}F
, and thus we may find a v-cover
\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+})\to X_{\eta}
by affinoid perfectoid.
Refining the cover if necessary we may assume this map factors through a map
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})\to X
because
X
is v-formalizing.
Since
X
is quasi-separated over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
and
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})
is quasi-compact over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
(see
[
Gle20
, Lemma 1.2.26]
), we may conclude that
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})
is quasi-compact over
X
.
To prove it is a v-cover, it is therefore enough to prove that the map of topological spaces is surjective.
On the generic fiber this is clear.
Using
[
Gle20
, Lemma 1.3.5, Proposition 1.3.8]
, we need to show
{\operatorname{Spec}}((R^{+}/\pi)^{\operatorname{perf}})\to X^{\operatorname{red}}
is a scheme-theoretic v-cover, or equivalently that the map of the associated adic spectra induced by the morphism of schemes is surjective.

The proof now follows a similar argument to the one given in
Proposition

2.31
.
Given a point
x\in|(X^{\operatorname{red}})^{\operatorname{ad}}|
in the adic spectrum of
X
with affinoid residue field
\operatorname{{Spa}}(k(x),k(x)^{+})
we consider the point in
\mathfrak{p}_{x}\in|X^{\operatorname{red}}|
corresponding to the support of
x
.
By surjectivity of the specialization map there is a point
y\in|X_{\eta}|
with
\operatorname{sp}_{X}(y)=\mathfrak{p}_{x}
.
Represent
y
by a map
\operatorname{{Spa}}(C,C^{+})\to X_{\eta}
with
(C,C^{+})
a perfectoid affinoid field over
F
.
Replacing
\operatorname{{Spd}}(C,C^{+})
by a v-cover we may assume this map factors over a map
\operatorname{{Spd}}(C^{+},C^{+})\to X
.
In particular, it promotes to a map
\operatorname{{Spd}}(C^{+})\to X
.
The closed point of
\operatorname{{Spd}}(C,C^{+})
specializes to a point with the same support as
x
.
Let
\kappa(y)
be the residue field of
C^{+}
.
Then
\kappa(y)
is a field extension of
k(x)
, and we can find a valuation ring
\kappa(y)^{+}\subset\kappa(y)
making
\kappa(y)^{+}/k(x)^{+}
an extension of valuation rings.
By pullback along the surjection
C^{+}\twoheadrightarrow\kappa(y)
we may construct from
\kappa(y)^{+}
an open and bounded valuation
C_{1}^{+}\subset C^{+}
.
Since
X_{\eta}
is partially proper we may extend
\operatorname{{Spd}}(C,C^{+})
to a map
\operatorname{{Spd}}(C^{+},C_{1}^{+})\to X_{\eta}
.
After possibly replacing
\operatorname{{Spa}}(C,C^{+}_{1})
by a v-cover, we may assume it factors through
\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+})
.
Then the map extends to
\operatorname{{Spd}}(C_{1}^{+})\to\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})\to X
. The map of adic spectra
{\operatorname{Spec}}((C_{1}^{+}/\pi)^{\operatorname{perf}})^{\operatorname{ad}}=\operatorname{{Spa}}((C_{1}^{+}/\pi)^{\operatorname{perf}},(C_{1}^{+}/\pi)^{\operatorname{perf}})\to(X^{\operatorname{red}})^{\operatorname{ad}}
has
x
in its image as we wanted to show.
∎

We now discuss some ad hoc hypothesis on
\pi
-adic kimberlites that allow us to recover them from their specialization triple.

Definition 2.35
.

We let
{\mathcal{K}}
denote the category of flat
\pi
-adic kimberlites
X
that are quasi-compact and separated over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
and satisfy the following properties:

(1)

The
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
-valued points of
X
define a dense subset of
|X_{C}|
.

(2)

The reduction
X^{\operatorname{red}}
is a perfect
k
-scheme perfectly of finite type.

(3)

Every section
\operatorname{{Spd}}C\to X_{C}
formalizes to a map
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}\to X_{O_{C}}
.

Our main theorem about the category
{\mathcal{K}}
is the following.

Theorem 2.36
.

When restricted to the category
{\mathcal{K}}
of
Definition

2.35
, the functor sending a
\pi
-adic kimberlite to its generic fiber is faithful and the functor that sends it to its specialization triple

(2.23)

X\mapsto(X_{\eta},X^{\mathrm{red}},\mathrm{sp}_{X})

is fully faithful.

Proof.

Let us prove faithfulness.
Let
f,g\colon X\to Y
be two maps such that
f_{\eta}=g_{\eta}
.
Since
X
is flat and quasi-compact we may replace it by a cover of the form
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})
.
Since
Y
is separated and
\pi
-adic the map
\Delta\colon Y\to Y\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O}Y
is formally adic and a closed immersion.
The pullback of
\Delta
by
(f,g)
is closed and formally adic subsheaf of
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})
with the same generic fiber. We may finish by arguing as in the proof of
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.10]
.

Let us prove the map is full. Fix a map
f:=(f_{\eta},f^{\operatorname{red}})
of triples

(2.24)

f\colon(X_{\eta},X^{\operatorname{red}},\mathrm{sp}_{X})\to(Y_{\eta},Y^{\operatorname{red}},\mathrm{sp}_{Y})

and let
W=X\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O}Y
.
Let
g\colon\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+})\to X_{\eta}
be a formalizable v-cover which extends to a surjection
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})\to X
and for which
f\circ g
is also formalizable (this is possible using
Proposition

2.31
).
Let
(g,f\circ g)\colon\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})\to W
be the induced map and define
Z
as the sheaf-theoretic image of
(g,f\circ g)
in
W
. We have a projection map
Z\to X
and we wish to prove that it is an isomorphism.
Observe that the graph morphism
(\operatorname{id},f_{\eta})\colon X_{\eta}\to W_{\eta}
already identifies
X_{\eta}
with
Z_{\eta}
. In particular,
Z(C)
is dense inside
|Z_{C}|
by our assumption on
X
.

By construction
Z
is v-locally formal since
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+},R^{+})
surjects onto it. Moreover, since
Z\subset W
and
W
is separated over
O
, we see that
Z
is also separated over
O
.
Let us prove that
Z
if formally
\pi
-adic and that
Z^{\operatorname{red}}
is isomorphic to
X^{\operatorname{red}}
.

We claim that
Z_{s}\subset W_{s}=(W^{\operatorname{red}})^{\diamond}
factors through the graph of
(f^{\operatorname{red}})^{\diamond}
.
Indeed, since
X_{O_{C}}
and
Y_{O_{C}}
formalize
C
-sections, for any map
q\colon\operatorname{{Spd}}C\to Z_{\eta}
we obtain maps
q^{\operatorname{red}}_{x}\colon\mathrm{Spec}(\bar{k})\to X^{\operatorname{red}}
and
q^{\operatorname{red}}_{y}\colon\mathrm{Spec}(\bar{k})\to Y^{\operatorname{red}}
intertwinned under
f^{\operatorname{red}}
(because
|f^{\operatorname{red}}|\circ\mathrm{sp}_{X}=\mathrm{sp}_{Y}\circ|f_{\eta}|
by assumption), in other words
\operatorname{sp}_{W}(q)\in\Gamma(f^{\operatorname{red}})
. In particular,
\operatorname{sp}_{W}(|Z_{\eta}|)\subset\overline{\operatorname{sp}_{W}(Z(C))}\subset|W^{\operatorname{red}}|
is contained in
\Gamma(f^{\operatorname{red}})
.
By
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.2]
, we know that the specialization map
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R,R^{+})\to{\operatorname{Spec}}((R^{+}/\pi)^{\operatorname{perf}})
is surjective.
Because
(g,f_{\eta}\circ g)\colon\operatorname{{Spd}}(R,R^{+})\to W
has image
|Z_{\eta}|
(on topological spaces), this implies by naturality of the specialization map that the morphism
g^{\operatorname{red}}\colon\mathrm{Spec}(R^{+}/\pi)^{\operatorname{perf}}\to W^{\operatorname{red}}
factors through
\Gamma(f^{\operatorname{red}})
as well.
Consequently,
Z_{s}\to W_{s}
factors through
\Gamma(f^{\operatorname{red}})^{\diamond}
.
On the other hand, since
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})\to X
is surjective the projection map

(2.25)

(\mathrm{Spec}(R^{+}/p)^{\operatorname{perf}})^{\diamond}\to(X^{\operatorname{red}})^{\diamond}

is a surjection.
This implies that the morphism
{\operatorname{Spec}}((R^{+}/\pi)^{\operatorname{perf}})\to W_{s}
surjects onto
\Gamma(f^{\operatorname{red}})
, and this in turn implies that
Z_{s}\to\Gamma(f^{\operatorname{red}})^{\diamond}
is an isomorphism, as it is a monomorphism and surjective.
In particular, we get that
Z_{s}\cong(Z^{\mathrm{red}})^{\diamond}
, that is,
Z
is formally
\pi
-adic.

As we have seen the map
Z\to X
is an isomorphism on the generic fiber and on the special fiber. Since
\operatorname{{Spd}}(R^{+})\to Z
is surjective
Z
is quasi-compact over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
, which is enough to conclude
Z\to X
is an isomorphism (by
[
Sch17
, Lemma 12.5]
, note that
Z\to X
is quasi-compact, as
X
is qcqs over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
). ∎

It is also relevant to relate this to a notion of topological flatness that appears in
[
PR21
]
.

Lemma 2.37
.

Let
X
be a proper
p
-adic kimberlite over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
satisfying conditions (1)-(3) of
Definition

2.35
. If
\lvert X_{\eta}\rvert
is a dense open
10
10

10

The converse, however, fails. Indeed, let
O\langle t\rangle\subset V
be a higher rank valuation ring endowed with its
\pi
-adic topology. Then
\operatorname{{Spd}}(V)
is a flat
\pi
-adic kimberlite. Using the olivine spectrum
[
Gle20
, Definition 1.2.1, Proposition 1.2.17]
, one can prove that the locus
N_{t\ll 1}
where
t
is topologically nilpotent is an open of
|\operatorname{{Spd}}(V)|
that does not meet the generic fiber.
of
\lvert X\rvert
, then
X
is flat, thus lies in
{\mathcal{K}}
.

Proof.

By
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.20]
, the specialization map is a spectral map of spectral spaces, and by
[
Gle20
, Lemma 1.4.43]
, the map is specializing.
In particular, it sends closed subsets to closed subsets. Since
X^{\operatorname{red}}
is perfectly of finite type, it suffices to prove surjectivity of the map
X(\operatorname{{Spd}}C)\to X^{\operatorname{red}}(\bar{k})
induced by
\mathrm{sp}
.

For this, take the associated tubular neighborhood
\widehat{X}_{/x}
over a closed point
x
of the reduction, which can be represented by
{\operatorname{Spec}}\bar{k}\to X^{\mathrm{red}}
uniquely up to Galois automorphisms. It is a non-empty open by
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.29]
. Hence, it must have topologically dense generic fiber, which is in particular non-empty. By hypothesis, we can find a
C
-valued point mapping to
x
.
∎

The following statement gives a v-sheaf theoretic criterion to determine when a weakly normal scheme is already normal.

Proposition 2.38
.

Let
A
be a flat, weakly normal and topologically of finite type
\pi
-adically complete domain over
O
.
Suppose that
A[\pi^{-1}]
is normal and that, for every closed point
x\in{\operatorname{Spec}}(A/\pi)
, the diamond
(\widehat{\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)}_{/x})_{\eta}
is connected
11
11

11

Instead of tubular neighborhoods, one could use formal completions at closed points to get a more classical formulation.
.
Then
A
is normal.

Proof.

Let
B
denote the integral closure of
A
in
A[\pi^{-1}]
.
Since
A[\pi^{-1}]
is normal,
B
is also normal and
B
is a finite
A
-algebra.
We claim that
f\colon\operatorname{{Spd}}(B)\to\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)
is an isomorphism, so that
A=B
by
Theorem

2.16
.
By quasi-compactness, it is enough to check this on the generic and special fibers.
The generic case follows from the definition of
B
.
We need to prove
{\operatorname{Spec}}(B/\pi)^{\mathrm{perf}}\cong{\operatorname{Spec}}(A/\pi)^{\mathrm{perf}}
which amounts to proving that the fibers at closed points consists of singletons.
Let
x\in{\operatorname{Spec}}(A/\pi)
denote a closed point.
By
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.26]
, we have an identification

(2.26)

(\widehat{\operatorname{{Spd}}(B)}_{/f^{-1}(x)})_{\eta}\cong(\widehat{\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)}_{/x})_{\eta}

In turn we also have
\coprod_{y\in f^{-1}(x)}(\widehat{\operatorname{{Spd}}(B)}_{/y})_{\eta}\cong(\widehat{\operatorname{{Spd}}(B)}_{/f^{-1}(x)})_{\eta}
.
By
Proposition

2.32
and
Proposition

2.34
for all
y\in f^{-1}(x)
the tubular neighborhood
(\widehat{\operatorname{{Spd}}(B)}_{/y})_{\eta}
is a non-empty open subset of
(\widehat{\operatorname{{Spd}}(A)}_{/x})_{\eta}
.
Since we assumed this to be connected we can conclude
f^{-1}(x)
contains a unique element.
∎

3.
The affine flag variety

In this section, we discuss some relevant material on perfect schemes and Witt vector affine flag varieties.
Namely, we review the calculation of the Picard group by He–Zhou
[
HZ20
]
, the definition of canonical finite type deperfections of Schubert perfect schemes and apply a Stein factorization argument to construct a comparison isomorphism between the
p
-adic canonical deperfections of depth
0
Schubert perfect schemes with the corresponding weakly normal Schubert schemes in equicharacteristic.
In particular, we prove
[
Zhu17b
, Conjecture III]
on their singularities in this case.

3.1.
Perfect schemes

Here, we present some facts on perfect schemes that we will need later.
Let
p
be a prime number.
All our schemes in this subsection will be assumed to lie over
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
.

The basic theory of perfect schemes is discussed in
[
Zhu17a
, A.]
and
[
BS17
, Section 3]
. In particular, we will use the notions of a perfectly finitely presented map between qcqs perfect schemes
[
BS17
, Proposition 3.11]
of a perfectly proper morphism
[
BS17
, Defintion 3.14]
,
[
Zhu17a
, Appendix A.18]
and, if
k
is a perfect field, we occasionally call a separated, perfectly finitely presented scheme
X
over
k
a perfect
k
-variety
[
Zhu17a
, Remark A.14]
.

A morphism
Y\to X
of perfect schemes is called perfectly smooth if, étale locally on
Y
, there exists étale morphisms to the perfection of some relative affine space over
X
, see
[
Zhu17a
, Definition A.25]
.

Given any normal finite type
k
-scheme
Y
, its perfection
Y_{\mathrm{perf}}
is normal as it is a filtered colimit of normal schemes along affine transition maps.
Conversely, if
X
is a qcqs normal perfect scheme perfectly of finite type, then using
[
Sta21
, Tag 01ZA]
(and finiteness for integral closures of schemes of finite type over a field), then we can write
X
as the filtered colimit of perfections of normal schemes
Y_{i},i\in I
, which are of finite type over
k
.

The following result gives a topological criterion for normality of perfect schemes.
We stress that perfectness is crucial as one sees, for example, by looking at the normalization morphisms of the cuspidal curve.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a surjective, perfectly proper morphism between qcqs integral perfect schemes.
Assume that
Y
is normal and
f
birational.
Then
X
is normal if and only if the geometric fibers of
f
are connected.

Proof.

If all geometric fibers of
f
are connected, then the natural map
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to f_{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
is an isomorphism, see
[
BS17
, Proposition 6.1]
,
[
Zhu17a
, Lemma A.21]
.
Thus,

\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}(f^{-1}(U)),

for any open affine
U\subset X
.
As
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
is a normal ring for any open subset
V\subset Y
, the claim follows (here we use that
Y
is integral
[
Sta21
, Tag 0358]
).

Conversely, we can write
f
as the perfection of a proper, finitely presented morphism
f_{0}\colon Y_{0}\to X
by
[
BS17
, Proposition 3.13, Corollary 3.15]
. Let
g_{0}\colon Y_{0}\to Z_{0}=\underline{{\operatorname{Spec}}}((f_{0})_{\ast}(\mathcal{O}_{Y_{0}}))
be the Stein factorization of
f_{0}
, see
[
Sta21
, Tag 03H2]
.
Perfecting again, we get a factorization
f=h\circ g
with
g\colon Y\to Z:=(Z_{0})_{\mathrm{perf}}
having connected geometric fibers, and
h\colon Z\to X
an integral, dominant morphism of integral schemes inducing an isomorphism at generic points (because
f
is birational).
As
X
is normal we obtain that
X\cong Z
, which implies the claim.
∎

We now turn to Picard groups of perfect schemes. Given any qcqs perfect
k
-scheme
X
, we have
\operatorname{Pic}(X)\cong\operatorname{Pic}(X_{0})[1/p]
for any preferred choice of finite type deperfection
X_{0}
, cf.
[
BS17
, Lemma 3.5]
. In particular, the Picard groups of perfect schemes are always uniquely
p
-divisible.

If
X
is perfectly finitely presented over some perfect field
k
and
X_{0}\to{\operatorname{Spec}}(k)
a finitely presented model for
X
, then the localized Weil divisor class group
\operatorname{Cl}(X_{0})[1/p]
only depends on
X
and not on
X_{0}
, and we set

(3.1)

\operatorname{Cl}(X):=\operatorname{Cl}(X_{0})[1/p].

If
X
is normal, then by
[
Sta21
, 0BE8]
(and passage to the limit over Frobenius for some normal model) there exists a natural, injective map

(3.2)

\mathrm{Pic}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Cl}(X).

Let us recall that a line bundle on a (qcqs) scheme is semi-ample if some positive power of it is globally generated.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
X
be a perfectly proper perfect
k
-scheme and
{\mathcal{L}}
be a semi-ample line bundle on
X
. There is a unique perfectly proper surjection
X\to Y
of perfect
k
-schemes with connected geometric fibers such that all sufficiently divisible powers of
{\mathcal{L}}
descend uniquely to ample line bundles on
Y
.

Proof.

By semi-ampleness of
\mathcal{L}
, we can take
X_{0}
to be a finite type deperfection of
X
over
k
, and let
{\mathcal{L}}_{0}
be a base point free line bundle on
X_{0}
whose pullback to
X
is a power of
{\mathcal{L}}
.
Let
Y_{0}
be the Stein factorization of the canonical morphism

(3.3)

X_{0}\to Z_{0}\subset{\mathbb{P}}(\Gamma(X_{0},{\mathcal{L}}_{0})),

where
Z_{0}
is the (scheme-theoretic) image of
X_{0}
.
Clearly,
{\mathcal{L}}_{0}
descends by construction to an ample line bundle on
Y_{0}
, pulling back
{\mathcal{O}}(1)
on the right side of (
3.3
).
After taking perfections, we get
X\to Y
with the desired properties (see
[
BS17
, Proposition 6.1]
for unique descent of line bundles).

In order to prove uniqueness of
Y
, we proceed as in
[
BS17
, Proof of Theorem 8.3]
. The morphism
X\to Y
is a v-cover (by properness), hence
Y
is determined by the closed subscheme
X\times_{Y}X\subset X\times_{{\operatorname{Spec}}(k)}X
. To identify this closed (and necessarily reduced) subscheme it suffices to identify the geometric fibers of the map
X\to Y
in terms of
{\mathcal{L}}
, and we only have to argue on
k
-valued points as these are dense inside
X\times_{Y}X
. We claim that two
k
-rational points of
X
lie in the same fiber over
Y
if and only if both points can be linked by a chain of closed integral perfect
k
-curves
C
, such that the restriction
\mathcal{L}|_{C}
is torsion in
{\operatorname{Pic}}(C)
.

By connectedness of the fibers and the definition of
X\to Y
, every two points in it can be linked by such a chain of integral perfect
k
-curves
C
on which
{\mathcal{L}}
is torsion.
Conversely, given an integral perfect
k
-curve
C\subset X
whose image in
Y
is not a point, all sufficiently large powers of
{\mathcal{L}}
restrict to an ample line bundle on
C
.
Indeed, after passing to a finitely presented deperfection of
C
over
k
the morphism
C_{0}\to Y
is finite and pullback of ample line bundles along affine morphisms are ample.
∎

Next, we discuss finite type deperfections. Let
k
be a perfect field and let
X
be a qcqs perfect
k
-scheme of perfectly finite presentation.
For each of the finitely many generic points
\eta\in X
, fix a subfield
k(\eta_{0})\subset k(\eta)
, which is finitely generated over
k
and has perfection
k(\eta)
.
Then there exists a unique (up to unique isomorphism) weakly normal
12
12

12

A finite type reduced
k
-scheme
Y
is called weakly normal if every finite birational universal homeomorphism
Z\to Y
with
Z
reduced is an isomorphism.
finite type
k
-scheme
X_{0}
such that
(X_{0})_{\operatorname{perf}}\cong X
and for each generic point
\eta_{0}\in|X_{0}|\cong|X|
the function field of
X_{0}
at
\eta_{0}
identifies with
k(\eta_{0})
, see
[
Zhu17a
, Proposition A.15]
.
Note that
X_{0}
also reflects normality of
X
, that is,
X
normal implies
X_{0}
normal.

For group actions we can draw the following consequence.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
G
be an affine perfect
k
-group of perfectly finite presentation and
X
a qcqs perfect
k
-scheme of perfectly finite presentation equipped with a
G
-action with finitely many orbits.

(1)

Any reduced deperfection
G_{0}
of
G
is a smooth affine
k
-group.

(2)

For such
G_{0}
, there are unique weakly normal deperfections
X_{0}
with
G_{0}
-action, whose generic fixers are also smooth.

Proof.

The first item is
[
Zhu17a
, Lemma A.26]
. For the second item, we notice that
X
has a dense open subset
U
consisting of the disjoint union of its maximal orbits, cf.
[
Zhu17a
, Proposition A.32]
. Having constructed the unique deperfection
U_{0}
with the desired properties, it has a unique extension to a deperfection
X_{0}
of
X
by
[
Zhu17a
, Lemma A.15]
. Furthermore, the action map
G\times X\to X
also deperfects, because it does so over a dense open (and
X_{0}
is weakly normal).

Therefore, we may and do assume that
X=G/H
is a single orbit around a certain
k
-valued point
x
. But then taking
H_{0}\subset G_{0}
to be the unique reduced closed subscheme whose perfection recovers
H\subset G
, we get a
G_{0}
-orbit
X_{0}=G_{0}/H_{0}
deperfecting
X
with smooth fixers. Uniqueness is clear.
∎

Proposition

3.3
will be useful for constructing finite type deperfections for Schubert varieties in Witt vector affine Grassmannians, see
Section

3.3
.

3.2.
Affine flag varieties

We now study the geometry of Witt vector affine flag varieties.
Assume that
k
is a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
and that
F
is a complete discretely valued field with residue field
k
and ring of integers
O
. Exceptionally, we allow
F\cong k(\hskip-1.99168pt({\pi})\hskip-1.99168pt)
to be a Laurent series field, since it is needed in
Section

3.3
.

We denote by
\mathrm{Alg}^{\operatorname{perf}}_{k}
the category of perfect
k
-algebras.
For
R\in\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{k}
, we denote by
W_{O}(R)
the associated ring of
O
-Witt vectors, see
[
FF
, Section 1.2.1]
: if
O
is
p
-adic, then
W_{O}(R)=W(R)\otimes_{W(k)}O
; if
O\cong k[\hskip-1.42262pt[{\pi}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
, then
W_{O}(R)=R\widehat{\otimes}_{k}O\cong R[\hskip-1.42262pt[{\pi}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
.

Moreover, we fix a (connected) reductive
F
-group
G
and a parahoric
O
-model
{\mathcal{G}}
in the sense of Bruhat–Tits.
We note that, over the completion
\breve{F}
of the maximal unramified extension of
F
, the group
G_{\breve{F}}
is automatically quasi-split by Steinberg’s theorem, see
[
Ser94
, Chapitre III.2.3]
.
We let
{\mathcal{G}}_{k}={\mathcal{G}}\otimes_{O}k
be the special fiber of
{\mathcal{G}}
.

Recall the definition of the Witt vector affine flag variety associated to
{\mathcal{G}}
.

Definition 3.4
.

(1)

The loop group of
G
is the functor

(3.4)

L_{k}G\colon\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{k}\to(\mathrm{Sets}),\ R\mapsto G(W_{O}(R)\otimes_{O}F).

(2)

The positive loop group of
{\mathcal{G}}
is the functor

(3.5)

L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}\colon\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{k}\to(\mathrm{Sets}),\ R\mapsto{\mathcal{G}}(W_{O}(R))

(3)

The affine flag variety for
{\mathcal{G}}
is the quotient (for the étale topology)

(3.6)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}:=L_{k}G/L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}.

Because any
{\mathcal{G}}
-torsor on
W_{O}(R)
can be trivialized over
W_{O}(R^{\prime})
for some with
R\to R^{\prime}
étale, the affine flag variety
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
is equivalently the functor on perfect
k
-algebras
R
that classifies
{\mathcal{G}}
-torsors
\mathcal{P}
on
{\operatorname{Spec}}(W_{O}(R))
together with a trivialization over
{\operatorname{Spec}}(W_{O}(R)\otimes_{O}F)
.

We have the following crucial representability result, see
[
BS17
, Corollary 9.6]
.

Theorem 3.5

(Bhatt-Scholze)
.

The functor
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
is representable by an ind-(perfectly projective) ind-(perfect
k
-scheme).

Representability as an ind-(perfect algebraic space) was previously proved by Zhu,
[
Zhu17a
]
, but is not sufficient for our purpose.

Fix an auxiliary maximal split
F
-torus
A
, a maximal
\breve{F}
-split
F
-torus
A\subset S\subset G
whose connected Néron
O
-model
{\mathcal{S}}
is contained in
{\mathcal{G}}
, see
[
BT84
, Proposition 5.1.10]
.
Let
T\subset G
be the centralizer of
S
, and let
{\mathcal{T}}
be the connected Néron
O
-model of
T
.
This yields the Iwahori–Weyl group

(3.7)

\tilde{W}:=N_{G}(T)(\breve{F})/{\mathcal{T}}(\breve{O})

associated with
S
, see
[
HR08
, Definition 7]
.
By
[
HR08
, Lemma 14]
, there exists a short exact sequence

(3.8)

1\to W_{\mathrm{af}}\to\tilde{W}\to\pi_{1}(G)_{I}\to 1

with
W_{\mathrm{af}}\subset\tilde{W}
the affine Weyl group,
I
the absolute Galois group of
\breve{F}
, and
\pi_{1}(G)
Borovoi’s algebraic fundamental group of
G
. The choice of an alcove in the apartment for
S
yields a splitting
W_{\mathrm{af}}\rtimes\pi_{1}(G)_{I}
of the sequence. By declaring the elements of
\pi_{1}(G)_{I}
to have length
0
and to be pairwise incomparable, we can further extend the length function and the Bruhat partial order on the Coxeter group
W_{\mathrm{af}}
to
\tilde{W}
.

By the Cartan decomposition, we may identify the double coclasses

(3.9)

{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{O})\backslash G(\breve{F})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{O})\cong W_{\mathcal{G}}\backslash\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}

where
W_{{\mathcal{G}}}:=(N_{G}(T)(\breve{F})\cap{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{O}))/{\mathcal{T}}(\breve{O})
is the Weyl
O
-group of
{\mathcal{G}}
relative to its maximal
O
-torus
{\mathcal{S}}
, see also
[
HR08
, Proposition 8]
.
This double coset carries a natural action of the Galois group
{\operatorname{Gal}}(\breve{F}/F)
.

Definition 3.6
.

Given a finite subset
W\subset W_{\mathcal{G}}\backslash\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
with reflex field
13
13

13

Concretely, the residue field defined by the
{\operatorname{Gal}}(\breve{F}/F)
-stabilizer of
W
.

k_{W}
, we define the associated Schubert perfect
k_{W}
-scheme
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
as the closure of the Schubert perfect orbit
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}^{\circ}
, the étale descent to
k_{W}
of the union of the
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
-orbits of the maximal elements
w\in W
.

If
W=\{w\}
, then these are perfect
k_{w}
-varieties denoted by
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}
, respectively
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}^{\circ}
, which are usually called the Schubert perfect variety, respectively Schubert perfect orbit associated with
w
.
More generally, if we fix an Iwahori
{\mathcal{I}}
dilating
{\mathcal{G}}
and containing
{\mathcal{S}}
, then its
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}
-orbits are enumerated by
\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
.
Given some finite subset
W\subset\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
, we can define in the same manner

(3.10)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),W}^{\circ}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),W}

the finite disjoint union of orbits and their closure inside
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
.
The latter is called an Iwahori–Schubert perfect scheme.
We observe that Schubert perfect schemes are always Iwahori–Schubert (but the converse is false).
Indeed, given
w\in W_{\mathcal{G}}\backslash\tilde{W}/W_{{\mathcal{G}}}
with lift
\dot{w}\in\tilde{W}/W_{{\mathcal{G}}}
of maximal length, we have

(3.11)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\dot{w}}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}.

Here we recall that the length function and Bruhat partial order on
\tilde{W}
induces one on the cosets
W_{\mathcal{G}}\backslash\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
, respectively
\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
compatibly with the dimensions and closure relations of Schubert varieties, respectively Iwahori–Schubert varieties, see
[
Ric13
, Section 1, Proposition 2.8]
for details and proofs in equicharacteristic (the arguments translate literally).

Proposition 3.7
.

For each
w\in W_{\mathcal{G}}\backslash\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
, the Schubert perfect variety
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}
is normal and
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}^{\circ}
is a perfectly smooth dense open with connected fixers.

Proof.

Let
{\mathscr{B}}(G,F)
be the Bruhat-Tits building of
G
, and let
{\mathbf{f}}\subset{\mathscr{B}}(G,F)
be the facet associated to
{\mathcal{G}}
, see
[
BT84
]
.
Given
w\in\tilde{W}/W_{{\mathcal{G}}}
, the stabilizer of
wL^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}\in{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}
is
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}\cap wL^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}w^{-1}
,
which is the positive loop group associated to the parahoric group scheme, which is the connected fixer of
{\mathbf{f}}\cup w({\mathbf{f}})
. In particular, this stabilizer is pro-(perfectly smooth and connected). We deduce that
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}
is perfectly smooth.

Fix an auxiliary Iwahori
{\mathcal{I}}
dilating
{\mathcal{G}}
and containing
{\mathcal{S}}
.
This yields the subgroup functor
L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{I}}\subset L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
and, as explained before, we know that
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),w^{\mathbf{f}}}
where
w^{\mathbf{f}}
is the maximal lift of
w
to
\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
. Let
{}_{\mathbf{f}}w^{\mathbf{f}}
be the minimal lift to
\tilde{W}
, write it as
w_{\operatorname{af}}\tau
with
w_{\operatorname{af}}\in W_{\operatorname{af}},\tau\in\pi_{1}(G)_{I}
, and fix some reduced word
\dot{w}
in simple reflections (along the alcove defined by
{\mathcal{I}}
) for
w_{\operatorname{af}}
.

After all those combinatorics, we may consider the Demazure variety

(3.12)

{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}:=L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{P}}_{i_{1}}\times^{L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}}\dots\times^{L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}}L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{P}}_{i_{n}}/{L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}},

where
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}\subset L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{P}}_{i_{j}}
are the minimal parahoric overgroups attached to the simple reflections. It follows easily by induction that the geometric fibers of the birational resolution (induced by multiplication)

(3.13)

\pi_{\dot{w}}\colon{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}\rightarrow{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}

are perfected projective line fibrations, hence connected.
As
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}
is perfectly smooth over
\bar{k}
, normality becomes a consequence of
Lemma

3.1
.
∎

The Picard group of Schubert perfect schemes over
\bar{k}
can be explicitly determined, see
[
HZ20
, Theorem 3.1]
for the case when
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{I}}
is Iwahori and
W=\{w\}
.

Theorem 3.8

(He–Zhou)
.

The homomorphism

(3.14)

\operatorname{Pic}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W})\rightarrow\operatorname{Pic}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},{\mathbb{S}}_{W}})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}[p^{-1}]^{\lvert{\mathbb{S}}_{W}\rvert}

is a bijection where
{\mathbb{S}}_{W}
is the set of all length
1
elements in
W\subset\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
.
(Note that
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k}{\mathbb{S}}_{W}}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1,\mathrm{perf}}_{\bar{k}}
if
{\mathbb{S}}_{W}
is a singleton.)

Proof.

To reduce the question to Iwahori–Schubert perfect varieties, we contemplate the Mayer–Vietoris sequence

(3.15)

1\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W_{0}}}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W_{1}}}^{\times}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W_{2}}}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W_{3}}}^{\times}\rightarrow 1

where the subsets
W_{i}
are closed for the Bruhat order

W_{0}=W_{1}\cup W_{2}
and
W_{3}=W_{1}\cap W_{2}
.
Since we may and do assume all these Schubert perfect schemes to be contained in a single connected component of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
(which implies
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}^{\times})\cong\bar{k}^{\times}
by perfectly properness), we get a natural isomorphism

(3.16)

\text{Pic}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W_{0}})\cong\text{Pic}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W_{1}})\times_{\text{Pic}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W_{3}})}\text{Pic}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W_{2}}).

By definition
S_{0}=S_{1}\cup_{S_{3}}S_{2}
, which implies that it suffices to show the claim for
X={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},w}
an Iwahori–Schubert perfect variety.

Injectivity can be reduced to Demazure varieties, see
[
BS17
, Theorem 6.1]
. The Demazure varieties
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}
are
{\mathbb{P}}_{\bar{k}}^{1,\operatorname{perf}}
-fibrations and can be handled directly, see
[
HZ20
, Proposition 3.4]
.
To treat surjectivity, it suffices to descend certain line bundles on
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}
back to the Iwahori–Schubert varieties. By
[
BS17
, Theorem 6.13]
, it remains to check that restriction of
{\mathcal{L}}
to geometric fibers is trivial. For this, see
[
HZ20
, Proposition 3.9]
.
∎

The choice of a
{\mathbb{Z}}[p^{-1}]
-basis in
\mathrm{Pic}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W})
seems arbitrary, due to
p
-divisibility. However, using the deperfection
\mathcal{I}\otimes_{O}\bar{k}
for the quotient
R\in\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\bar{k}}\mapsto\mathcal{I}(R)
of
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}
, the perfect curve
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},{\mathbb{S}}_{W}}
has a canonical equivariant deperfection, see
Proposition

3.3
, yielding a natural
{\mathbb{Z}}
-lattice.

Remark 3.9
.

During the proof, we have also determined the Picard group of the Demazure varieties
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}
, or more generally those of the convolutions

(3.17)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},W_{1}}\tilde{\times}\dots\tilde{\times}{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},W_{n-1}}\tilde{\times}{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W_{n}}

of Iwahori–Schubert perfect schemes, where at most the last one is not at full level.

Together with
Proposition

3.2
, this tells us how to recover, for instance, the perfect Schubert variety
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w}
just from its Demazure resolution and the sub-
\mathbb{Z}[p^{-1}]
-module
{\operatorname{Pic}}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w})\subset{\operatorname{Pic}}({\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}})
: take any
\mathcal{L}
on
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}
which is the pullback of a line bundle on
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w}
whose restriction to
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},s}
has positive degree for each
s\in\mathbb{S}_{W}
.

We now turn to equivariant automorphisms of (connected) Schubert schemes.

Proposition 3.10
.

The group of
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
-equivariant automorphisms of a connected Schubert perfect scheme
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W}
is trivial.
In particular, the stabilizers are self-normalizing
14
14

14

This is false for general Bruhat–Tits subgroups, for example, consider
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{T}}\cdot R_{u}(L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}})
where
R_{u}({\operatorname{-}})
denotes the pro-unipotent radical.
subgroups of
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
.

Proof.

We prove the more general statement for Iwahori–Schubert perfect schemes. Consider the disjoint irreducible components in the dense open
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W}^{\circ}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W}
.
These will be permuted under any equivariant automorphism
\sigma
.
Moreover,
\sigma
preserves the
\bar{k}
-valued points of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W}
fixed under
{\mathcal{S}}(\breve{O})
. For the entire flag variety, we claim that the
{\mathcal{S}}(\breve{O})
-fixed points in
G(\breve{F})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{O})
lie in the image of
N(\breve{F})
. Indeed, let
[g]\in G(\breve{F})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{O})
be a fixed point.
Then
g{\mathbf{f}}
is a
{\mathcal{S}}(\breve{O})
-stable facet (with
{\mathbf{f}}
the facet determined by
{\mathcal{G}}
), hence contained in
{\mathscr{A}}(G,S,\breve{F})
by
[
BT84
, Proposition 5.1.37]
.
Multiplying on the left by a suitable element of
N(\breve{F})
, we can trivialize
[g]
, that is,
[g]\in N(\breve{F}){\mathcal{G}}(\breve{O})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{O})
.

Now, observe that the
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}
-fixer of some
w\in\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
equals
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}\cap wL^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}w^{-1}
, and it suffices to recover
w
from this subgroup alone.
Indeed, then
\sigma
must preserve
w
, and then
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W}
pointwise by
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}
-equivariance as
w\in\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W}
was arbitrary.
If
{\mathbf{a}}
is the alcove fixed by
{\mathcal{I}}(\breve{O})
and
\tilde{w}\in\tilde{W}
the minimal lift of
w
, then birationality of the Demazure resolution
\pi_{\dot{w}}
implies

(3.18)

L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}\cap wL^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}w^{-1}=L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}\cap\tilde{w}L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}{\tilde{w}}^{-1}.

Note that the right side is the Bruhat–Tits group attached to
{\mathbf{a}}\cup\tilde{w}({\mathbf{a}})
.
We need to recover
\tilde{w}
. Moreover, because
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W}
was assumed to be connected, all
\tilde{w}
considered here project to the same constant
\tau\in\pi_{1}(G)
, so it is enough to get
w_{\operatorname{af}}\in W_{\mathrm{af}}
if
\tilde{w}=w_{\mathrm{af}}\tau
.

By
[
BT84
, Corollaire 5.1.39]
, the fixed point set of
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}\cap\tilde{w}L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}{\tilde{w}}^{-1}
inside
{\mathscr{B}}(G,F)
equals the closed convex hull of
{\mathbf{a}}\cup\tilde{w}({\mathbf{a}})
. In turn, every alcove inside this closed convex hull lies in some minimal gallery connecting
{\mathbf{a}}
to
\tilde{w}({\mathbf{a}})
by
[
BT72
, Lemme 2.4.4]
. Since a minimal gallery describes a unique word of simple reflections necessary to move from one alcove to another, this gives back the affine transformation
w_{\operatorname{af}}
.
∎

Among Schubert schemes, we are especially interested in the
\mu
-admissible locus.
Recall that
C
is a completed algebraic closure of
F
and that
I
denotes the inertia group of
F
.
Moreover, let
B\subset G_{\breve{F}}
be a Borel containing
T_{\breve{F}}
.
Recall that the inverse of the Kottwitz morphism
[
Kot97
, Equation (7.2.1)]
induces an isomorphism of the coinvariants

(3.19)

X_{\ast}(T)_{I}\cong T(\breve{F})/{\mathcal{T}}(\breve{O}),\ \nu_{I}\mapsto\nu_{I}(\pi),

not depending on the choice of uniformizer
\pi\in O
, under which we may regard the former as the subgroup of
\tilde{W}
acting by translation on the standard apartment, see also
[
HR08
, Proposition 13]
.

Definition 3.11
.

Let
\mu
be a geometric conjugacy class of cocharacters with reflex field
E
.
The
\mu
-admissible locus is the Schubert perfect
k_{E}
-scheme

(3.20)

{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\{{\lambda}_{I}(\pi)\}},

where
{\lambda}\in X_{*}(T)
runs over all representatives of
\mu
and
\lambda_{I}\in X_{\ast}(T)_{I}
denotes the associated coinvariant under
I
.

Note that
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is geometrically connected because the finite Weyl group acts trivially on
\pi_{1}(G)_{I}
.
It does not depend on the choice of
T
.
By a result of Haines
[
Hai18
, Theorem 4.2]
, we know that
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\circ}:={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\{{\lambda}_{I}(\pi)\}}^{\circ}
, where
{\lambda}
now runs over the rational conjugates of
\mu
, that is, all those which are contained in a closed Weyl chamber attached to
w_{0}Bw_{0}^{-1}
for some
w_{0}\in W_{0}
, the finite Weyl group of
G_{\breve{F}}
with respect to
S_{\breve{F}}
.

It will turn out that
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\mu}
is functorial in
({\mathcal{G}},\mu)
, as soon as we develop a theory of local models
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
, see
Definition

4.11
, and calculate their special fibers, confer
Theorem

6.16
.
The admissible locus also admits the following representation-theoretic interpretation in terms of representations of the Langlands dual group
\widehat{G}
(here, taken over any algebraically closed field) with dual torus
\widehat{T}
:

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
\widehat{\lambda}^{I}\in X^{\ast}(\widehat{T}^{I})\cong X_{\ast}(T)_{I}
be running through the set of restrictions of all weights
\widehat{\lambda}
for
\widehat{T}
occurring in a finite dimensional algebraic representation of
\widehat{G}
with fixed highest weight
\widehat{\mu}=\mu
.
Then
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\{\widehat{\lambda}^{I}(\pi)\}}
.

Proof.

Being a
\widehat{G}
-representation,
V
contains all the weights
\widehat{\lambda}
conjugate to
\widehat{\mu}
under the absolute Weyl group with the same non-zero multiplicity.
Under
X^{\ast}(\widehat{T})\cong X_{\ast}(T)
, these correspond to the conjugates of
\mu
compatibly with the projection to
X^{\ast}(\widehat{T}^{I})\cong X_{\ast}(T)_{I}
.
Hence, the lemma follows from the definition of the admissible locus.
∎

Example 3.13
.

The basic example of the admissible locus occurs for
G={\operatorname{GL}}_{2}
,
\mu=(1,0)
and
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{I}}
an Iwahori.
In this case,
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is the union of two copies of
{\mathbb{P}}^{1,\operatorname{perf}}_{k}
intersecting transversally at a point.
More generally, one can enumerate the Iwahori–Schubert orbits of the translated to the neutral component admissible locus
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}
in terms of alcoves in the standard apartment
{\mathscr{A}}(G,S,F)
.
For pictures in the case of unitary groups of split rank
2
, the reader is referred to the introduction of
[
PR09
]
.
For further examples, see the survey
[
PRS13
]
.

3.3.
Canonical deperfections

Now, we wish to introduce equivariant deperfections of the Schubert perfect schemes
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}
following
Proposition

3.3
and discuss their geometry, at least for certain
W
. We are especially interested in admissible loci
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
for
\mu
minuscule.

First, recall that the congruence quotient
L^{\leq n}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
of
L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
has a deperfection
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}{\mathcal{G}}
, given by
(n+1)
-truncated Witt vectors and which is called the Greenberg realization.
We denote by
L^{>n}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
the kernel of
L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}\to L^{\leq n}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
.

Definition 3.14
.

Let
n
be the smallest nonnegative integer such that
L^{>n}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
acts trivially on
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W}
and call it the associated depth.
The canonical deperfection
15
15

15

In equicharacteristic, one recovers the weak normalization of classical Schubert schemes, which turn out to be the classical ones under
1.9
and also
p\nmid\lvert\pi_{1}(G_{\operatorname{der}})\rvert
, see
[
FHLR22
, Section 3.1]
and
[
HLR18
]
.

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}^{\operatorname{can}}
of the perfect Schubert scheme
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}
is the finite type
k_{W}
-scheme with
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}{\mathcal{G}}
-action determined by
Proposition

3.3
.

Assume the
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
-action on
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W}
factors through
L^{0}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}^{\mathrm{perf}}
.
For
V\leq W
, we get a deperfection

(3.21)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},V}^{\operatorname{can}}\to{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W}^{\operatorname{can}}

of the closed immersion of perfect Schubert schemes, because the image is a finite type deperfection with smaller function fields, as it carries a
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}
-action.

However, it is not clear that the finite type morphism is a closed immersion. To know more about the geometry of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}^{\operatorname{can}}
, we exploit the picture in equicharacteristic.

Assume
G
is adjoint, and also
1.9
for
G
over
\breve{F}
, that is, if
p=2
, then
G
has no odd unitary factors over
\breve{F}
.
Then, for every parahoric
\breve{O}
-group
{\mathcal{G}}
attached to a facet in
{\mathscr{A}}(G,S,\breve{F})
, we find smooth, affine, fiberwise connected
\breve{O}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
-lifts
\underline{{\mathcal{G}}}
in the sense of
[
FHLR22
, Proposition 2.8]
. Note that the
\bar{k}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
-reductions
{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}
are parahoric models of some adjoint connected reductive
\bar{k}(\hskip-1.99168pt({t})\hskip-1.99168pt)
-group
G^{\prime}
attached to a facet in some appartment
{\mathscr{A}}(G^{\prime},S^{\prime},\bar{k}(\hskip-1.99168pt({t})\hskip-1.99168pt))\cong{\mathscr{A}}(G,S,F)
, see
[
FHLR22
, Lemma 2.7]
.

In particular, these come with isomorphisms

(3.22)

{\mathcal{G}}\otimes_{\breve{O}}\bar{k}\cong{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}\otimes_{\bar{k}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]}\bar{k},

that are functorial as we vary
{\mathcal{G}}
among parahoric models attached to a facet in
{\mathscr{A}}(G,S,\breve{F})
, and
which we now exploit to compare their Schubert schemes.
Let us note that the loop groups
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
and its equicharacteristic cousin
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}({\mathcal{G}}^{\prime})
admit natural surjections on
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}\cong{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime,\mathrm{perf}}_{\bar{k}}.

Below, we use subscripts
({\operatorname{-}})^{\prime}
to denote the perfections of the equicharacteristic loop groups and Schubert varieties for
{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}
.

Lemma 3.15
.

Under the above constraints, there are unique equivariant isomorphisms

(3.23)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\operatorname{can}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W}\cong{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\operatorname{can}}_{{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime},\bar{k},W^{\prime}}

for all connected
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W}
of depth
0
, that is, whose
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
-action factors through
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
.

Proof.

As
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}\cong{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}^{\prime}
, it suffices by
Proposition

3.3
to produce equivariant isomorphism
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W}\cong{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime},\bar{k},W^{\prime}}
of the perfect Schubert schemes.
During the proof, we fix an auxiliary Iwahori
{\mathcal{I}}
dilated from
{\mathcal{G}}
and consider the corresponding Iwahori–Schubert perfect scheme
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},W}
.

First assume that
W=\{w\}
.
The perfect variety
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},w}
can be resolved via a Demazure variety
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}
.
If
s
is the first letter of the word
\dot{w}
and
\dot{v}
is the word obtained from deleting the first letter, we get

(3.24)

{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},s}\tilde{\times}{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{v}}

where
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}\subset L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{P}}
is the minimal parahoric corresponding to
s
. We claim that the action of
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}
on
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{v}}
is trivial when restricted to the normal subgroup
L^{\geq 1}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{P}}
.
Otherwise, let
\alpha
be the negative simple affine root corresponding to
s
and observe that
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha+1}
acts non-trivially on
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{v}}
.
But conjugating by
s
yields that
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{U}}_{-\alpha+1}\subset L^{\geq 1}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{I}}
does not act trivially on
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}
.

Arguing inductively on
\dot{w}
, and exploting the above claim, we reach at an
{\mathcal{I}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-equivariant identification of the Demazure perfect varieties

(3.25)

{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}\cong{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime},\bar{k},\dot{w}^{\prime}}

bounded by
\dot{w}
resp.
\dot{w}^{\prime}
and attached to
{\mathcal{I}}
, respectively the
\bar{k}(\hskip-1.99168pt({t})\hskip-1.99168pt)
-reduction
{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime}
of the Iwahori
\breve{O}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
-lift. In the case
l(\dot{w})=1
, then we get the unique equivariant identification of one-dimensional Iwahori–Schubert perfect varieties, which are perfected projective lines.

As the Picard group of the Demazure varieties have already been determined, see
Theorem

3.8
, respectively
[
HZ20
, Section 3.2]
for the equicharacteristic case, the previous isomorphism descends uniquely by
Proposition

3.2
to an
{\mathcal{I}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-equivariant identification

(3.26)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\bar{k},w}\cong{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}),\bar{k},w^{\prime}}

of the perfect Schubert varieties, see
Remark

3.9
.
If the left side is stable under
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}
, then we need to show the map is not only
{\mathcal{I}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-equivariant, but furthermore
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-equivariant.

Let
\bar{Q}\subset{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}
denote the image of
{\mathcal{I}}_{\bar{k}}
. By assumption, the
{\mathcal{I}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-action on both sides factors through the perfection of
\bar{Q}
. Using the convolution product

(3.27)

{\mathcal{G}}_{V}^{\operatorname{perf}}\times^{\bar{Q}^{\operatorname{perf}}}{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w},

we get a perfect
\bar{k}
-variety mapping
{\mathcal{I}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-equivariantly to
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w}
and that can be identified with its equicharacteristic analogue in a
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-equivariant fashion. Since
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}/{\bar{Q}^{\operatorname{perf}}}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k}}
is a Schubert perfect variety at Iwahori level, we know its Picard group by
Theorem

3.8
and
Remark

3.9
. Applying again
Proposition

3.2
, we not only recover the original isomorphism, by
Proposition

3.10
, but also conclude it is
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-equivariant and the unique such map.

For a general
W
as in the statement, we now can glue the above isomorphism to non-irreducible Schubert perfect schemes

(3.28)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W}\cong{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime},\bar{k},W^{\prime}}

appealing again to
Proposition

3.10
.
∎

From now on
G
is no longer assumed to be adjoint.
We approach the canonical admissible locus
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}}
for minuscule
\mu
, that is, the canonical perfection of the admissible locus, with our comparison result, describing its singularities (thereby confirming
[
Zhu17a
, Conjecture III]
for Schubert varieties in the admissible locus) and computing its coherent cohomology.

Theorem 3.16
.

Let
\mu
be minuscule and assume
1.9
.
Then,
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}}
is Cohen–Macaulay and Frobenius split compatibly with its
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}
-stable reduced
\bar{k}
-subschemes.

Moreover, for every ample line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}
on
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
that descends to
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}}
, there is an equality

(3.29)

\dim_{\bar{k}}\operatorname{H}^{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}},{\mathcal{L}})=\dim_{C}\operatorname{H}^{0}({\mathcal{F}}_{G,C,\mu},{\mathcal{O}}(c_{\mathcal{L}})).

Here,
{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu}=G_{E}/P_{\mu}^{-}
is the classical flag variety attached to
\mu
, the central charge
c_{\mathcal{L}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{rk}\Psi_{G}-\operatorname{rk}\Phi_{G}}
is given by Kac–Moody coefficients, see
[
PR08
, Section 10]
and
[
BS17
, Section 10]
, and the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}(c_{\mathcal{L}})
is the corresponding power of the ample generator of
\operatorname{Pic}({\mathcal{F}}_{G,C,\mu})
.

Proof.

We want to apply
Lemma

3.15
, in order to reduce the statements to equicharacteristic, where we refer to
[
FHLR22
, Theorem 3.1, Theorem 4.1]
.

In order to do this, we first notice that there is an equivariant isomorphism
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\mu}^{\mathrm{can}}\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad}},\bar{k},\mu_{\operatorname{ad}}}^{\mathrm{can}}
via the natural map. Here,
\mu_{{\operatorname{ad}}}
denotes the composition of
\mu
with
G_{C}\to{G_{\operatorname{ad}}}_{C}
.
Indeed, this can be checked on perfections and then at the level of geometric points, where it follows from the assertion that
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}\to{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ad}},\bar{k}}
is an open and closed immersion.

We still have to show that
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\mu}^{\mathrm{can}}
has minimal depth, that is,
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
acts via
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bar{k}}
.
Since
L^{\geq 1}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
is a normal subgroup, it suffices to check that it fixes each of the sections
{\lambda}
defining the admissible locus.
By the combinatorial dictionary, see our proof of
Proposition

3.10
, it suffices to show that
\lvert a({\lambda}_{I})\rvert\leq 1
, that is, the translation
{\lambda}_{I}
moves every affine root to a parallel one at distance at most one.
By definition, one has

(3.30)

a({\lambda}_{I})=[K:\breve{F}]^{-1}\sum_{{\sigma}\in{\operatorname{Gal}}(K/\breve{F})}{\sigma}\tilde{a}({\lambda}),

where
K
is a finite Galois extension of
\breve{F}
splitting
G_{\breve{F}}
, and
\tilde{a}
is an absolute root restricting to
a
, so its absolute value is at most
1
, since
{\lambda}
is minuscule.
∎

4.
Affine Grassmannians and local models

In this section, we start by gathering several basic facts on the
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-affine Grassmannian over
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
.
Most of them were established in
[
SW20
, Lecture XIX]
and
[
FS21
, Chapters VI.2, VI.5]
, but our approach is sufficiently different and relevant to later sections that it merits some elaboration.

Then, we introduce the main objects of study of this article, to wit the local models

(4.1)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{E}}

defined for every
\mu\in X_{\ast}(T)
via v-closures of Schubert diamonds in a Beilinson–Drinfeld Grassmannian. We dedicate the rest of the section to showing that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is an
L^{+}_{O_{E}}{\mathcal{G}}
-stable flat, proper
p
-adic kimberlite with good finiteness properties.
In particular, its special fiber will be shown to be representable by some connected Schubert perfect scheme
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}
.

4.1.
The
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-affine Grassmannian

In this section, we fix a complete discretely valued field
F/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with perfect residue field
k
, ring of integers
O
and uniformizer
\pi
, a complete algebraic closure
C/F
with ring of integers
O_{C}
and residue field
\bar{k}=k_{C}
.
We denote by
\breve{F}\subset C
the maximal unramified complete subextension with ring of integers
\breve{O}
and the same residue field
\bar{k}=k_{\breve{F}}
.
Further, we fix a (connected) reductive
F
-group
G
and a maximal
\breve{F}
-split
F
-torus
S\subset G
containing a maximal
F
-split torus, see
[
BT84
, Proposition 5.1.10]
.
As
G
is quasi-split over
\breve{F}
by Steinberg’s theorem, the centralizer
T
of
S
is a maximal torus.
Also, we fix a Borel subgroup
B\subset G_{\breve{F}}
containing
T_{\breve{F}}
.

For any affinoid perfectoid space
\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+})
in characteristic
p
equipped with a map to
\operatorname{{Spd}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, let
B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}^{+}(R^{\sharp})
, respectively
B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(R^{\sharp})
, be the rings of de Rham periods formed using
O
-Witt vectors.
For convenience, we set
B^{+}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}:=B^{+}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(C)
and
B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}:=B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(C)
.
The
B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-loop group of
G
is the group functor over
\operatorname{{Spd}}F
given by

(4.2)

LG\colon(R,R^{+})\mapsto G(B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(R^{\sharp})),

and the positive loop group is the subgroup functor

(4.3)

L^{+}G\colon(R,R^{+})\mapsto G(B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}^{+}(R^{\sharp})).

Their v-sheaf quotient

(4.4)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}:=LG/L^{+}G

is called the
B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-affine Grassmannian.
Similarly to
Section

3.2
,
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}(R,R^{+})
parametrizes
G
-torsors on the spectrum
{\operatorname{Spec}}(B^{+}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(R^{\sharp}))
with a trivialization over
{\operatorname{Spec}}(B_{\mathrm{dR}}(R^{\sharp}))
.
Here, we are primarily interested in the geometry and work therefore over
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
.
The base changes are denoted by
L_{C}G
,
L_{C}^{+}G
and
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C}
, for convenience.

As an auxiliary first step, we study the affine flag variety and then translate the results to the affine Grassmannian.
For this, the Iwahori group
B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-model
\mathcal{I}
is given as the dilatation of
G\otimes_{F}B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
along the subscheme
B_{C}\subset G_{C}
of its special fiber.
Define

(4.5)

L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{I}\colon(R,R^{+})\mapsto\mathcal{I}(B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}^{+}(R^{\sharp}))

which is a subgroup v-sheaf of
L^{+}G
.
It gives rise to the
B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-affine flag variety

(4.6)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{I},C}:=L_{C}G/L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{I},

viewed as a v-sheaf over
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
.

We recall that
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C}\to\operatorname{{Spd}}C
is an increasing union of proper, spatial diamonds by
[
SW20
, Lecture XIX]
.
The same holds for
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}\to\operatorname{{Spd}}C
, as the projection

(4.7)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C}

is a proper, cohomologically smooth
(G_{C}/B_{C})^{\diamondsuit}
-fibration.
The following discussion is parallel to parts of
Section

3.2
but simplified by the fact that we consider
G\otimes_{F}B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
which is a (split) reductive group over
B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
(and not some parahoric group scheme).
The geometry of the affine flag variety
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
or, better, the v-stack quotient

(4.8)

{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}:=L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{I}\backslash{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}=L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{I}\backslash L_{C}G/L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{I}

is reflected in the Iwahori-Weyl group

(4.9)

\tilde{W}:=N(B_{\mathrm{dR}})/T(B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}),

where
N
denotes the normalizer of
T
in
G
.
There is a canonical map
\underline{\tilde{W}}\rightarrow{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
because
T(B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}})\subset\mathcal{I}(B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}})
.

Lemma 4.1
.

The map
\underline{\tilde{W}}\to{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
induces a bijection

(4.10)

\tilde{W}\cong|{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}|.

Proof.

Every point of
|{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}|
is represented by a map
\operatorname{{Spa}}(K,K^{+})\to L_{C}G
with
K
algebraically closed perfectoid.
Two
K
-valued points have the same underlying element in
|{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}|
if, v-locally, they lie in the same double coset

(4.11)

\mathcal{I}(B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(K^{\sharp}))\backslash G(B_{\mathrm{dR}}(K^{\sharp}))/\mathcal{I}(B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(K^{\sharp})).

The identification now follows from the Bruhat decomposition which is independent of
K^{\sharp}
.
∎

Let
{\mathbf{a}}\subset{\mathscr{A}}(G,S,F)
be the alcove defined by
\mathcal{I}
in the appartment fixed by
S
of the Bruhat–Tits building.
Let
\mathbb{S}\subset\tilde{W}
be the set of simple reflections along the walls bounding
{\mathbf{a}}
.
The affine Weyl group
W_{{\operatorname{af}}}\subset\tilde{W}
is the subgroup generated by the elements in
\mathbb{S}
.
Then
W_{{\operatorname{af}}}
is a Coxeter group, and depends only on the Bruhat–Tits building of
G
.
There exists a canonical short exact sequence

(4.12)

1\to W_{{\operatorname{af}}}\to\tilde{W}\to\pi_{1}(G)\to 1,

which is naturally split by taking the stabilizer
\Omega_{{\mathbf{a}}}\subset\tilde{W}
of the alcove
{\mathbf{a}}
.
Thus, we can write each
w\in\tilde{W}
uniquely as
w=w_{{\operatorname{af}}}\tau
with
\tau\in\Omega_{\mathbf{a}}
and
w_{\operatorname{af}}\in W_{\operatorname{af}}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Equip
\pi_{1}(G)
with the discrete topology.
The morphism

(4.13)

|{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}|\to\pi_{1}(G)

is locally constant, thus underlies a morphism
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}\to\underline{\pi_{1}(G)}
of small v-stacks.

Proof.

Here, we follow the argument behind the proof of
[
PR08
, Theorem 5.1]
.
If
G=G_{\operatorname{sc}}
is simply connected, then we see that, for every algebraically closed perfectoid field
K/C
, the group
L_{C}G(K)
is generated by its affine root subgroups
L_{C}U_{a}(K)
.

But, since
L_{C}U_{a}
is connected (choose a pinning), we conclude that
L_{C}G
, hence also
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
and
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
are connected.
If
G=T
is a torus, then we see easily that
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,C}
equals the v-sheaf
\underline{X_{\ast}(T)}
compatibly with the map above.

Now, suppose that
G_{\operatorname{der}}=G_{\operatorname{sc}}
. Then,
\pi_{1}(G)
identifies with the fundamental group of the abelian quotient
G/G_{\operatorname{der}}
, so the claim is clear. Finally, for a general group
G
, consider the z-extension

(4.14)

1\to T_{\operatorname{sc}}\to\tilde{G}\to G\to 1,

where
\tilde{G}
is given by the pushout of
(G_{\operatorname{sc}}\rtimes T)
along the morphism
\mathrm{ker}(T_{{\operatorname{sc}}}\to T)\to T_{{\operatorname{sc}}}
, where
T_{{\operatorname{sc}}}
is the preimage of the maximal torus
T\subset G
under the map
G_{{\operatorname{sc}}}\to G
. Using the fact that
T_{\operatorname{sc}}
is an induced torus, we see that the Hecke stacks and the
\pi_{1}
’s lie in a similar exact sequence, which yields the claim.
∎

For
\tau\in\pi_{1}(G)
, we denote by
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}^{\tau}
the fiber over
\tau
of the morphism
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}\to\underline{\pi_{1}(G)}
.
We note that right translation by a representative of
\tau
in
L_{C}G
induces an isomorphism

(4.15)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}^{1}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}^{\tau}.

Moreover,
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}^{1}
is canonically isomorphic to the affine flag variety
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}}_{\operatorname{sc}},C}
of the simply connected cover
G_{{\operatorname{sc}}}
.
Namely, the transition morphism
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}}_{\operatorname{sc}},C}\rightarrow{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}^{1}
is bijective by checking on geometric points (
[
Sch17
, Lemma 12.5]
) and using the Bruhat decomposition, hence must be an isomorphism as both
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}}_{\operatorname{sc}},C},{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}^{1}
are ind-proper over
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
.

Definition 4.3
.

Let
w\in\tilde{W}
.
The Schubert cell
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}^{\circ}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
is the v-sheaf-theoretic image of the orbit map

(4.16)

L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{I}}\to{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C},\ i\mapsto iw.

The Schubert variety is the v-closure
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}:={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}^{\circ,\mathrm{cl}}
in the sense of
Section

2.1
.

By
Proposition

2.8
, we know that the underlying topological space of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{I,w}
is the weakly generalizing closure of
|{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}^{\circ}|
inside
|{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}|
.
But,
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}^{\circ}
is possibly ill-behaved because
L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{I}}
is not quasicompact.
As we show in
Proposition

4.5
and
Corollary

4.6
for the affine Grassmannian, our definition is equivalent to the pointwise definition in
[
FS21
, Definition VI.2.2]
.
We start with the case of simple reflections:

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{S}
be a simple reflection.
Then there is an isomorphism
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s}\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{C})^{\diamondsuit}
that restricts to
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s}^{\circ}\simeq({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{C})^{\diamondsuit}
.
In particular,
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s}^{\circ}
is a topologically dense open subset of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}_{s}
be the parahoric group scheme over
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
associated to the wall of
{\mathbf{a}}
defining
s
.
The maximal reductive quotient
H
of its special fiber over
C
has semisimple rank
1
.
Using
[
BT84
, Théorème 4.6.33]
, we see that
L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{I}}
is the preimage of
Q^{\diamondsuit}
under
L^{+}{\mathcal{P}}_{s}\rightarrow H^{\diamondsuit}
for some Borel subgroup
Q\subset H
.
Thus, there are isomorphisms
L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{P}_{s}/L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{I}\simeq(H/Q)^{\diamondsuit}\simeq(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C})^{\diamondsuit}
which can be made explicit via the choice of a pinning.
This implies that the monomorphism

(4.17)

L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{P}_{s}/L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{I}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}

is a closed embedding, as
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
is separated and
(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C})^{\diamondsuit}
is proper over
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
.
The isomorphism
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s}^{\circ}\simeq({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{C})^{\diamondsuit}
is now clear, since this is the only non-trivial
Q^{\diamondsuit}
-orbit in
(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C})^{\diamondsuit}
.
∎

In order to treat more general
w=w_{\mathrm{af}}\tau\in\tilde{W}\cong W_{\mathrm{af}}\rtimes\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}
, we invoke Demazure resolutions as follows.
Let
\dot{w}=s_{1}\dots s_{n}
be a reduced word for
w_{\operatorname{af}}=w\tau^{-1}
with
s_{i}\in\mathbb{S}
and consider the Demazure variety

(4.18)

{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}:=L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\times^{L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{I}}}\dots\times^{L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{I}}}L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{P}}_{n}/L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{I}}

which will also be denoted by
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s_{1}}\tilde{\times}\dots\tilde{\times}{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s_{n}}
.
It is connected and cohomologically smooth over
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
(being an iterated
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C}
-fibration), and the twisted product

(4.19)

{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}^{\circ}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s_{1}}\tilde{\times}\dots\tilde{\times}{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s_{n}}

of the open cells is topologically dense by induction on
n
, starting with
Lemma

4.4
and using that
L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{I}}
is pro-(cohomologically smooth) over
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
.
It carries, moreover, a natural morphism (induced by multiplication)

(4.20)

\pi_{\dot{w}}\colon{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}\rightarrow{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}

which necessarily maps onto
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w_{\operatorname{af}}}
, by properness,
L^{+}_{C}\mathcal{I}
-equivariance and the fact that
\dot{w}
maps to
w
.
After translation by
\tau
, we may regard this as a resolution of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}
, which is thus in particular connected.

For the next result, we note that
\tilde{W}
, in analogy to the discussion following (
3.8
), is equipped with a length function and Bruhat partial order induced from the quasi-Coxeter structure on
\tilde{W}\cong W_{\mathrm{af}}\rtimes\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}
.

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
w\in\tilde{W}
.
Then
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}^{\circ}
, respectively
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}
, agrees with the subfunctor of all maps
S\rightarrow{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
such that for all geometric points
S^{\prime}=\operatorname{{Spa}}(K,K^{+})\rightarrow S
, the induced point
S^{\prime}\rightarrow{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
is given by
w
, respectively by
v
for some
v\leq w
.
In particular,
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}^{\circ}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}
is a topologically dense open.

Proof.

Observe that the first assertion cannot be verified at geometric points because
L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{I}}
is not quasicompact.
However, we see from
Lemma

4.4
that the result holds for simple reflections. Indeed, we even have by Bruhat–Tits combinatorics

(4.21)

L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha_{s}}\cdot s={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,s},

where
\alpha_{s}
denotes the positive simple affine root associated to the simple reflection
s
, and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha_{s}}
is the corresponding
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-model of the affine root group.
Pulling across the reflections
s_{i}
appearing in the convolution product of
{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}
, we see that
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}^{\circ}
surjects to the v-sheaf image of
{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}^{\circ}
along
\pi_{\dot{w}}
.
This v-sheaf image identifies with the pointwise description of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}^{\circ}
by quasicompactness of
\pi_{\dot{w}}
and bijectivity at geometric points.

Similarly, the v-sheaf image of
{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}
along
\pi_{\dot{w}}
is a proper closed sub-v-sheaf of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}
. By generalities of Tits system, see
[
BT72
, 1.2.6]
, this v-sheaf image coincides with the desired pointwise description of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}
. Pulling back again via the quotient map
\pi_{\dot{w}}
, we see that
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}^{\circ}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}
is a topologically dense open of the closed v-sheaf image of
\pi_{\dot{w}}
.
∎

As a corollary, we get that the bijection
\lvert{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}\rvert\cong\tilde{W}
from
Lemma

4.1
is a homeomorphism where
\tilde{W}
is endowed with order topology via its Bruhat order, and also that
\pi_{0}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C})=\pi_{0}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C})=\pi_{1}(G)
via
Lemma

4.2
.

Now, we apply our results to the affine Grassmannian
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
.
Note that there is the group isomorphism

(4.22)

X_{\ast}(T)\cong T(B_{\mathrm{dR}})/T(B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}),\ \chi\mapsto\chi(\xi)

which is independent of the choice of uniformizer
\xi\in B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
.
Then the Cartan decomposition induces a bijection

(4.23)

\lvert{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C}\rvert\simeq X_{*}(T)_{+},

where
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C}=L^{+}_{C}G\backslash L_{C}G/L^{+}_{C}G
denotes the Hecke stack.
Therefore, we get a Schubert cell
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}^{\circ}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C}
defined as the v-sheaf-theoretic image of the orbit map and the Schubert cell
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
defined as its closure, for each
\mu\in X_{*}(T)_{+}
, compare with
Definition

4.3
.

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
\mu\in X_{*}(T)_{+}
.
Then
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}^{\circ}
, respectively
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
agrees with the subfunctor of all maps
S\rightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
such that for all geometric points
S^{\prime}=\operatorname{{Spa}}(K,K^{+})\rightarrow S
, the induced point
S^{\prime}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C}
is given by
\mu
, respectively by some
{\lambda}\leq\mu
in the dominance order on
X_{*}(T)_{+}
.
In particular,
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}^{\circ}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
is a topologically dense open.

Proof.

This formally follows from
Proposition

4.5
by using the projection
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C}
from (
4.7
) and noting that the dominance order on
X_{*}(T)_{+}
is induced by the Bruhat order, see
[
Ric13
, Corollaries 1.8, 2.10]
for similar arguments. We leave the details to the reader.
∎

We also have the following fact which says that the
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
-valued points are dense in
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
even for the constructible topology.

Corollary 4.7
.

Let
\mu\in X_{*}(T)_{+}
.
The spatial diamond
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
has enough
C
-facets in the sense of
[
Gle20
, Definition 1.4.38]
.

Proof.

Taking the preimage under the projection
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C}
from (
4.7
), this reduces to the analogous assertion for
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},C,w}
for some
w\in\tilde{W}
.
Since the Demazure resolution is a v-cover, it is enough to prove that
{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}
has enough
C
-facets.
This in turn can be proved inductively on the length of
\dot{w}
.
If
\dot{w}=s\cdot\dot{v}
then
{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}
is a pro-étale
({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{C})^{\diamondsuit}
-bundle over
{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{v}}
.
We may find a pro-étale cover

(4.24)

X\to{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{v}}

with
X\times_{{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{v}}}{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}=X\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}C}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{C})^{\diamondsuit}
.
Following the arguments given in
[
Gle20
, Lemma 2.2.24, Proposition 2.2.30]
, we may even assume that
X
has enough facets over
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
.
By
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.39, 2.]
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{C,\dot{w}}
also has enough facets.
∎

We conclude with some motivation for our later discussion of representability.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
\mu\in X_{*}(T)_{+}
.
The v-sheaf
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
is representable by a projective
C
-scheme
{\mathcal{F}}_{G,C,\mu}
if and only if
\mu
is minuscule.

Proof.

If
\mu
is minuscule, then the
L^{+}_{C}G
-action factors through
G_{C}^{\diamondsuit}
and the Bialynicki-Birula map gives an isomorphism
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}\simeq(G_{C}/P_{\mu})^{\diamondsuit}
, see
[
SW20
, Proposition 19.4.2]
.

Now suppose that
\mu
is not minuscule, that is,
\langle\mu,\theta\rangle\geq 2
for some root
\theta
of
G_{C}
.
Then the
L^{+}_{C}{\mathcal{I}}
-orbit of the point
\mu
is not representable by a rigid space:
This orbit can be filtered by successive smooth rigid spaces, so taking fiber products appropriately, we would eventually arrive at representability of the Banach-Colmez space
{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{C}}(B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}/\xi^{2})
.
However, this is a non-split self-extension of
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}^{\diamondsuit}
which does not even split étale locally, so cannot be representable.
Indeed, if the extension were split étale locally, it would actually split on the nose, as
H^{1}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{C},{\mathcal{O}})
is trivial.
However, if

(4.25)

X:=\operatorname{{Spa}}(C\langle T^{\pm 1}\rangle,O_{C}\langle T^{\pm 1}\rangle)\subset{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{C}

is the affinoid torus, the element
T\in{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{C}(X)
does not admit a lift to
{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{C}}(B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}/\xi^{2})(X)
as we now show.
Let
X^{\prime}=\operatorname{{Spa}}(C\langle T^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle,O_{C}\langle T^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle)
be the usual perfectoid
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
-cover of
X
.
Elements in
{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{C}}(B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}/\xi^{2})(X^{\prime})
can be represented by
[a]+[b]\xi
with
a,b\in(C\langle T^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle)^{\flat}
.
Assume now that

x:=[a]+[b]\xi

maps to
T
, that means,
a^{\sharp}=T
in
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{C}(X^{\prime})
(cf.
[
SW20
, Section 6.2]
for the map
(-)^{\sharp}
), and assume that
x
is invariant under
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
. Let
g\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
.
Then
g
acts on
[a]
via multiplication with
[g]
if we identify
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)\subset C^{\flat}
. Using the
({\operatorname{-}})^{\sharp}
-map, we get

(4.26)

a^{\sharp}\left(\frac{[g]-1}{\xi}\right)^{\sharp}=b^{\sharp}-g(b^{\sharp}).

But, if
g\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
