is a generator, then
a^{\sharp}(\frac{[g]-1}{\xi})^{\sharp}=cT
with
c\in C
non-zero.
Writing
b^{\sharp}
as a powerseries in the
g
-eigenvectors
T^{n}
with
n\in\mathbb{Z}[1/p]
, then shows that (
4.26
) can not hold because
g\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
fixes
T
.
This finishes the argument.
∎

4.2.
Local models

We continue with the notation of
Section

4.1
, and additionally let
{\mathcal{G}}
be a parahoric
O
-model of
G
.

We work with the moduli space
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}
of
{\mathcal{G}}
-torsors over
{\operatorname{Spec}}(B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}})
trivialized over
{\operatorname{Spec}}(B_{\mathrm{dR}})
, see
[
SW20
, Definition 20.3.1]
, which is the Beilinson–Drinfeld Grassmannian over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
.
A crucial result of Scholze–Weinstein concerns its ind-properness, see
[
SW20
, Theorems 19.3.4, 20.3.6, 21.2.1]
.

Theorem 4.9

(Scholze–Weinstein)
.

The structure morphism of the Beilinson–Drinfeld Grassmannian

(4.27)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow\operatorname{{Spd}}O

is ind-proper and ind-representable in spatial diamonds.

In
[
Ans18
, Section 12]
and then later in
[
Gle20
, Section 2.2]
, the first named and second named author respectively constructed and studied the specialization map for
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}
, see also
[
Gle20
, Proposition 2.2.5]
.

Again, we have natural loop groups at hand, namely

(4.28)

L_{O}{\mathcal{G}}\colon(R,R^{+})\mapsto{\mathcal{G}}(B_{\mathrm{dR}}(R^{\sharp}))

and

(4.29)

L^{+}_{O}{\mathcal{G}}\colon(R,R^{+})\mapsto{\mathcal{G}}(B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R^{\sharp})),

where
(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp+})
denotes an untilt of
(R,R^{+})
over
(O,O)
and
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{(+)}(R^{\sharp})
the ring of de Rham periods formed using
O
-Witt vectors.
These define v-sheaves over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
and the base changes to
\operatorname{{Spd}}F
, respectively
\operatorname{{Spd}}k
recover the loop groups
L^{+}G\subset LG
from
Section

4.1
, respectively the v-sheaves
(L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}})^{\diamondsuit}\subset(L_{k}G)^{\diamondsuit}
associated with the loop groups from
Section

3.2
.
Their base changes to
O_{C}
are denoted
L_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}
,
L^{+}_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}
.

Lemma 4.10
.

There is a natural isomorphism

(4.30)

L_{O}{\mathcal{G}}/L^{+}_{O}{\mathcal{G}}\cong{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}},

where the left side is a quotient for the étale topology.
In particular, on geometric fibers

(4.31)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\cong{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O}\operatorname{{Spd}}F,\;\;\;\;\;\;{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\diamondsuit}\cong{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O}\operatorname{{Spd}}k,

where
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
is the affine Grassmannian from
Section

4.1
and
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\diamondsuit}
the v-sheaf attached to the Witt vector partial affine flag variety from
Section

3.2
.

Proof.

For the uniformization (
4.30
), see
[
SW20
, Proposition 20.3.2]
.
The isomorphisms (
4.31
) are given by base change from (
4.30
) by unwinding the definitions.
∎

Let
\mu
be a conjugacy class of cocharacters of
G_{C}
, with field of definition
E\subset C
. We denote by
O_{E}
its ring of integers with residue field
k_{E}
.
We wish to construct a closed sub-v-sheaf

(4.32)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}}

prolonging the Schubert diamonds
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}
which are the descent to
\operatorname{{Spd}}E
of the ones we studied in the previous subsection.

Definition 4.11
.

Let
\mu
be a conjugacy class of cocharacters in
G_{C}
.
The local model
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is the v-closure of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}
inside
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}}
.

A priori
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
does not admit a moduli problem description for general parahoric
{\mathcal{G}}
, so its structure could be harder to parse. Let us give some examples where the local model
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is relatively well understood.

Example 4.12
.

If
{\mathcal{G}}
is reductive, then
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is the integral Schubert variety over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}
and generalizes the objects introduced in
Section

4.1
, see
[
SW20
, Proposition 20.3.6]
and
[
FS21
, VI.1]
.
If
G=T
is a torus, then the explicit description of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
furnishes an identity
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{T}},\mu}=\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}
, see
[
SW20
, Proposition 21.3.1]
.

We need to show permanence of the local model under the
L^{+}_{O}{\mathcal{G}}
-action.

Proposition 4.13
.

The natural action map
L^{+}_{O}{\mathcal{G}}\times{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
restricts, after base change to
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}
, to a group action on the closed sub-v-sheaf
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
.
Moreover, the generic fiber of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is topologically dense.

Proof.

By embedding
{\mathcal{G}}
in
\operatorname{GL}_{n}
, we may always find a quasi-compact closed subsheaf
X\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{E}}
with
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\subset X
, stable under
L^{+}_{O_{E}}{\mathcal{G}}
whose action factors through a congruence quotient
L^{\leq N}_{O_{E}}{\mathcal{G}}
, where
N
is a sufficiently large positive integer (necessarily at least
\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle
as one sees by restricting to
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}
).

The structure map
L^{\leq N}_{O_{E}}{\mathcal{G}}\to\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}
is partially proper, cohomologically smooth and consequently universally open, see
[
Sch17
, Proposition 23.11]
.
By
Corollary

2.9
,

(4.33)

L^{\leq N}_{O_{E}}{\mathcal{G}}\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}=(L^{\leq N}_{E}G\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}E}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu})^{\operatorname{cl}}

as closed sub-v-sheaves of
L^{\leq N}_{O_{E}}{\mathcal{G}}\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}}X
.
Now, we have seen that
L^{+}_{E}G
respects
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}
, so the multiplication map
L^{\leq N}_{E}G\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}E}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}\to X
factors through
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}
.
This also implies that the integral action map

(4.34)

L^{\leq N}_{O_{E}}{\mathcal{G}}\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\to X

factors through the closure
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
, as we desired.

For the last claim, we consider the restricted variant of the Hecke v-stack

(4.35)

{\operatorname{Hk}}^{\leq N}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}:=\big[L^{\leq N}_{O_{E}}{\mathcal{G}}\backslash{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\big].

Its underlying topological space has the extra special property that every subset is weakly generalizing, since for every perfectoid affinoid field
(K,K^{+})
the Bruhat decomposition over
B_{\mathrm{dR}}(K)
is insensitive to variation of
K^{+}
.
Now, the projection map

(4.36)

\operatorname{pr}\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\to{\operatorname{Hk}}^{\leq N}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}

is cohomologically smooth and consequently open.
Therefore, by the same argument of
Corollary

2.9
, we see that both the usual topological and the weakly generalizing closure commute with pullback along
\lvert\operatorname{pr}\rvert
.
It results that
\lvert{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}\rvert
is a dense open of
\lvert{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\rvert
.
∎

We can now prove the following structural properties of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
.

Proposition 4.14
.

With notation as in
Definition

2.35
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\in{\mathcal{K}}
.
More specifically, the local model
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is a flat
p
-adic kimberlite over
O_{E}
with enough facets over
C
and
O_{C}
-formalizable
C
-sections.
Moreover, the special fiber

(4.37)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu,k_{E}}:={\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}(k_{E})}

is of the form
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}^{\diamondsuit}
for a connected perfect Schubert scheme
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}
.
In particular,
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{red}}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}
is perfectly proper and perfectly finitely presented perfect
k
-scheme.

Proof.

Choosing a closed embedding
{\mathcal{G}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{GL}_{n}
, we may find a cocharacter
\nu
of
\operatorname{GL}_{n}
giving rise to a closed immersion

(4.38)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n},\nu},

so that local models are proper v-sheaves and, in particular, quasi-compact.
By
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.4.30.(3), Proposition 2.2.5]
to prove
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is a kimberlite, it suffices to prove it is
p
-adic.
By
[
Gle20
, Proposition 1.3.35]
, we may reduce to proving that the special fiber of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is represented by a v-sheaf of the form
X^{\diamond}
for
X
a perfect scheme.

By
Proposition

4.13
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu,\bar{k}}
, is of the form
16
16

16

A priori this could be an infinite union.

\cup_{i\in I}{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},W_{i}}^{\diamond}
with finite subsets
W_{i}\subset\tilde{W}
, where we have used the fact that
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}
is perfectly proper, in order to deduce
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}^{\diamondsuit}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}^{\diamond}
.
By quasi-compactness, we get
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu,k_{E}}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}^{\diamond}
for some finite subset
W\subset\tilde{W}
, which finishes the proof that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is a
p
-adic kimberlite.

That
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
has
O_{C}
-formalizable
C
-sections is proved in
[
Gle20
, Proposition 2.2.4]
, see also
[
Ans18
, Section 12]
.
We explained in
Corollary

4.7
that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
has enough
C
-facets. Together with
Proposition

4.13
and
Lemma

2.37
, flatness follows.
By
Proposition

2.34
,
\lvert{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}\rvert=\operatorname{sp}(\lvert{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}\rvert)
and since
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}
is connected
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},W}
is also connected.
∎

Remark 4.15
.

It follows that the base change
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}}
is still topologically flat, and hence agrees with the v-closure
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}
of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
inside
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}
.
Indeed, repeating the argument of
Proposition

4.13
over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}
, we see that the special fiber of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}
is represented by a Schubert perfect scheme.
But a
\operatorname{{Spd}}k
-valued point of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is a specialization of some
\operatorname{{Spd}}C
-valued point by
Proposition

4.14
, hence equality of both closures is clear.

Next, we analyse some functoriality behavior of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
in the pair
({\mathcal{G}},\mu)
.
Here, by definition, a map
({\mathcal{G}}_{1},\mu_{1})\to({\mathcal{G}}_{2},\mu_{2})
is a morphism of
O
-group schemes
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
such that the image of
\mu_{1}
in
G_{2,C}
lies in the same conjugacy class as
\mu_{2}
.

Proposition 4.16
.

The association
({\mathcal{G}},\mu)\mapsto{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}
, see
Remark

4.15
, is functorial, preserves closed embeddings and direct products, and induces isomorphisms
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}\cong{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad}},O_{C},\mu_{\operatorname{ad}}}
, where
\mu_{\mathrm{ad}}
denotes the composite of
\mu
with
G_{C}\to G_{\mathrm{ad},C}
.

Proof.

Functoriality follows from that of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
and the definition using v-closures, see
Remark

4.15
.
For the claim regarding closed embeddings and central extensions, we refer to
[
Lou20
, IV, Proposition 4.16, Corollary 4.17]
: one checks injectivity at geometric points, using
Lemma

4.17
.
As for direct products, it suffices to check equality at the level of
\operatorname{{Spd}}k
-valued points by
Proposition

4.14
, and this is easy because the generic fiber was already a product.
∎

Lemma 4.17
.

The specialization map induces a bijection

(4.39)

\pi_{0}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\breve{O}})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\pi_{0}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}})\cong\pi_{1}(G)_{I},

where
I
is the absolute Galois group of
\breve{F}
.

Proof.

For the final bijection, see
[
Zhu17a
, Proposition 1.21]
.
The first is a consequence of proper base change
[
Sch17
, Theorem 19.2, Remark 19.3]
applied to
f\colon{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\breve{O}}\rightarrow\operatorname{{Spd}}\breve{O}
and the base change
i\colon\operatorname{{Spd}}\bar{k}\to\operatorname{{Spd}}\breve{O}
, using that the
0
-th cohomology group computes connected components:
for some coefficient ring, say,
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
with
\ell\neq p
, we apply the proper base change
i^{*}R^{0}f_{*}\Lambda_{X}\cong R^{0}(f^{\prime})_{*}(i^{\prime})^{*}\Lambda_{X}
where
\Lambda_{X}
is the constant sheaf supported on increasing closed
\breve{O}
-proper sub-v-sheaves
X\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\breve{O}}
.
Passing to global sections, the second computes
\Lambda^{\pi_{0}(X_{\bar{k}})}
by definition whereas the first computes
\Lambda^{\pi_{0}(X)}
by using the v-cover
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}\to\operatorname{{Spd}}\breve{O}
.
Finally, we use
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
to pass between
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\diamondsuit}
and
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
.
∎

5.
Geometry of multiplicative group actions

Our approach to the Scholze–Weinstein conjecture requires determining the special fiber of local models in terms of admissible loci.
We follow the general strategy of Haines and the fourth named author
[
HR21
]
of calculating the support of nearby cycles using hyperbolic localization.
This requires translating the results from
[
HR21
, Section 5]
to the v-sheaf Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian.
For basic facts pertaining to
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}
-actions on small v-stacks, the reader is referred to
[
FS21
, Chapter IV.6]
.

5.1.
Over
O

As in
Section

4.1
, we continue to fix a complete discretely valued field
F/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with ring of integers
O
and perfect residue field
k
, a complete algebraic closure
C/F
and a connected reductive
F
-group
G
with parahoric model
{\mathcal{G}}
over
O
containing the connected Néron model
{\mathcal{S}}
of the maximally
F
-split maximal
\breve{F}
-split
F
-torus
S
.

Fix a cocharacter
{\lambda}\colon{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}\subset{\mathcal{G}}
defined over
O
.
After base change to
F
, this induces a Levi
M=M_{\lambda}
with Lie algebra
{\operatorname{Lie}}\,M=({\operatorname{Lie}}\,G)_{{\lambda}=0}
, a parabolic subgroup
P=P_{\lambda}^{+}
with Lie algebra
{\operatorname{Lie}}\,P=({\operatorname{Lie}}\,G)_{{\lambda}\geq 0}
and an unipotent subgroup
U=U^{+}_{\lambda}
with Lie algebra
{\operatorname{Lie}}\,U=({\operatorname{Lie}}\,G)_{{\lambda}>0}
fitting in a semi-direct product decomposition
P=M\ltimes U
.
Since
{\lambda}
is defined over
O
, the decomposition
P=M\ltimes U
extends to
O
-models
{\mathcal{P}}={\mathcal{M}}\ltimes{\mathcal{U}}
, admitting analogous descriptions for their Lie algebras and being equipped with homomorphisms

(5.1)

{\mathcal{M}}\longleftarrow{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}.

The
O
-group schemes
{\mathcal{P}}
,
{\mathcal{M}}
,
{\mathcal{U}}
are smooth affine with connected fibers, and
{\mathcal{M}}
is a parahoric
O
-model of the Levi subgroup
M
, see
[
HR21
, Lemma 4.5]
and also
[
CGP15
, Section 2.1]
,
[
KP21
, Section 6.2]
for proofs of these claims.

By functoriality, (
5.1
) induces maps of ind-(spatial
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
-diamonds)

(5.2)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{M}}\longleftarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}},

where
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow\operatorname{{Spd}}O
and
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow\operatorname{{Spd}}O
are ind-proper by
Theorem

4.9
.
On the other hand, the cocharacter
{\lambda}
induces a cocharacter

(5.3)

{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}\overset{[{\operatorname{-}}]}{\longrightarrow}L^{+}_{O}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\overset{L^{+}_{O}{\lambda}}{\longrightarrow}L^{+}_{O}{\mathcal{G}},

where
[{\operatorname{-}}]
denotes the Teichmüller lift.
Thus, we obtain a left action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}
on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

The
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}
-action on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfies
[
FS21
, Hypothesis IV.6.1]
.

Proof.

Choosing a closed immersion
{\mathcal{G}}\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}
of group schemes, we reduce to the case
{\mathcal{G}}={\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}
, using
Theorem

4.9
to see that the induced map
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}}
is a closed immersion.
Then the lemma is a special case of
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.3.1]
.
∎

Consequently, we obtain a
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}
-equivariant diagram

(5.4)

({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0}\longleftarrow({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{+}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}},

where
({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0}=({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}}
denotes the fixed points and
({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{+}
the attractor classifying
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}
-equivariant maps
({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{{\diamondsuit},+}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}
over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
, see also (
6.3
) below.
The map
({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{+}\rightarrow({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0}
is the Bialynicki-Birula map given by evaluating at the zero section.
Our aim is to understand the relation of (
5.2
) with (
5.4
).
The following result is the analogue of
[
HR21
, Theorem 5.6, Theorem 5.19]
in the context of ind-schemes:

Theorem 5.2
.

The maps (
5.2
) and (
5.4
) fit into a commutative diagram of ind-(spatial
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
-diamonds)

(5.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{P}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}})^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}})^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}},}
\iota^{0}
\iota^{+}
{\operatorname{id}}

with the following properties.

(1)

The maps
\iota^{0}
,
\iota^{+}
are open and closed immersions.

(2)

Their base changes
\iota^{0}_{F}
,
\iota^{+}_{F}
are isomorphisms.

(3)

If
{\mathcal{G}}
is special parahoric (for example, reductive), then
\iota^{0}
,
\iota^{+}
are isomorphisms.

In particular, the complements
({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0}\setminus\iota^{0}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{M}})
,
({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{+}\setminus\iota^{+}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{P}})
are concentrated over
\operatorname{{Spd}}k
, that is, their base change to
\operatorname{{Spd}}F
is empty.

If
{\mathcal{G}}
is reductive, then
Theorem

5.2
is proved in
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.3.1]
.
Indeed, in this case
\iota^{0}
,
\iota^{+}
are isomorphisms.
In general, we follow the strategy of
[
HR21
]
:

Proof of
Theorem

5.2
.

First, we construct the maps
\iota^{0},\iota^{+}
.
The closed subgroup
{\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}
induces a closed immersion
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}
(using
Theorem

4.9
) that is
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}
-equivariant for the trivial action on the source.
Thus, the map factors though the fixed points, defining the necessarily closed immersion
\iota^{0}\colon{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0}
.
The construction of
\iota^{+}
is more delicate and proceeds as follows.
Pick a closed immersion
{\mathcal{G}}\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}
of
O
-group schemes such that the fppf quotient
{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}/{\mathcal{G}}
is quasi-affine, see
[
PR08
, Proposition 1.3]
.
The cocharacter
{\lambda}^{\prime}\colon{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\rightarrow^{\lambda}{\mathcal{G}}\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}
induces parabolic subgroup
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
with Lie algebra
{\operatorname{Lie}}\,{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}=({\operatorname{Lie}}\,{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O})_{{\lambda}^{\prime}\geq 0}
.
The induced map on fppf quotients
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}/{\mathcal{P}}\rightarrow{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}/{\mathcal{G}}
is a monomorphism between finite type
O
-schemes, thus quasi-affine by Zariski’s main theorem.
By functoriality of the construction
{\mathcal{G}}\mapsto{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}
, we obtain a commutative diagram of ind-(spatial
\operatorname{{Spd}}O
-diamonds):

(5.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{P}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}})^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}})^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}}}
\iota^{+}
\cong

Using
Theorem

4.9
, the map
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}}
is a closed immersion so that the square is Cartesian.
This proves the existence of
\iota^{+}
.
Furthermore, the displayed map
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}\to({\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\operatorname{GL}}_{n,O}})^{+}
is an isomorphism by
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.3.1]
.
As
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}/{\mathcal{P}}
is quasi-affine, so
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{P}}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
is a locally closed immersion (compare with the proof of
[
Zhu17b
, Proposition 1.2.6]
and
[
SW20
, Lemma 19.1.5]
), the map
\iota^{+}
is necessarily a locally closed immersion as well.

Now, part (
2
) is immediate from our construction and
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.3.1]
applied over
\operatorname{{Spd}}F
.
For part (
3
), we observe that
\iota^{0},\iota^{+}
are bijective on geometric points if
{\mathcal{G}}
is special parahoric: by (
2
) for geometric points lying over
\operatorname{{Spd}}F
and by the Iwasawa decomposition
[
KP21
, Section 3.3]
for geometric points lying over
\operatorname{{Spd}}k
.
As
\iota^{0}
,
\iota^{+}
are locally closed immersions, they must be isomorphisms, so (
3
) follows.

For (
1
), it remains to prove that
\iota^{0}
,
\iota^{+}
are open immersions for general parahoric group schemes
{\mathcal{G}}
.
For this, we may and do assume that
k
is algebraically closed and observe that there are bijections of connected components

(5.7)

\pi_{0}(({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{+})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\pi_{0}(({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\pi_{0}(({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0}),

where the first holds by general properties of Bialynicki-Birula maps (see the proof of
[
Ric19
, Corollary 1.12]
) and the second by proper base change as in the proof of
Lemma

4.17
.
The fixed points
({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0}
in the Witt vector partial affine flag variety can be analyzed in analogy to
[
HR21
, Section 4]
:
concretely, if
{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{sc}}={\mathcal{M}}_{\operatorname{sc}}\ltimes{\mathcal{U}}
for
{\mathcal{M}}_{\operatorname{sc}}
being the corresponding parahoric model of
M_{\operatorname{sc}}
, then there is a disjoint union (on points) into connected locally closed sub-ind-schemes

(5.8)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}=\bigcup_{[w]}{\mathcal{S}}_{w},\ {\mathcal{S}}_{w}=L_{k}{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{sc}}\cdot w

where
[w]
runs through the double coset
W_{M,{\operatorname{af}}}\backslash\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
and
w
denotes the image of a representative under the embedding
\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}\hookrightarrow{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}
.
The image of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{P}}\hookrightarrow{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}
consists of those
{\mathcal{S}}_{w}
for
[w]
lying in
W_{M,{\operatorname{af}}}\backslash\tilde{W}_{M}/W_{\mathcal{M}}
.
Passing to fixed points, the image of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0}
is the union of the
L_{k}{\mathcal{M}}_{\operatorname{sc}}
-orbits for these
[w]
.
So the map
\pi_{0}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{M}})\rightarrow\pi_{0}(({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0})
identifies with the injection

(5.9)

W_{M,{\operatorname{af}}}\backslash\tilde{W}_{M}/W_{\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow W_{M,{\operatorname{af}}}\backslash\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}.

We let
{\mathcal{C}}^{0}_{\mathcal{G}}
, respectively
{\mathcal{C}}^{+}_{\mathcal{G}}
, be the open and closed sub-v-sheaf of
({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{0}
, respectively of
({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}})^{+}
, consisting of those components belonging to
\operatorname{im}(\pi_{0}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{M}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{0}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}))
under (
5.7
).
Then the maps
\iota^{0}
,
\iota^{+}
factor through
{\mathcal{C}}^{0}_{\mathcal{G}}
, respectively
{\mathcal{C}}^{+}_{\mathcal{G}}
inducing locally closed immersions

(5.10)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{0}_{\mathcal{G}},\ {\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{P}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{+}_{\mathcal{G}},

that are bijective on geometric points, hence isomorphisms.
The theorem is thus proven.
∎

5.2.
Semi-infinite orbits

We end this section with a study of the stratification (
5.8
). Throughout, we assume that
k=\bar{k}
is algebraically closed so that
F=\breve{F}
and
O=\breve{O}
.
Note that the torus
S
is then maximal
F
-split.
The following lemma simplifies some arguments of
[
HR21
, Theorem 6.12]
and is used in the proof of
Theorem

6.16
given in
Section

6.5
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For every
w\in\tilde{W}/W_{{\mathcal{G}}}
, there is an
O
-cocharacter
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}\subset{\mathcal{G}}
such that for the induced strata
{\mathcal{S}}_{w}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}=\{w\}
.

Proof.

Up to changing the Iwahori
L_{k}^{+}{\mathcal{I}}\subset L_{k}^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
, we may and do assume that the Iwahori–Schubert variety
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),w}^{\circ}
is a dense open of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}
. Notice that the closed complement of that dense open is stable under the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-action, so it follows that the connected component of the fixed point
w
in the attractor
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{+}
cuts
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},w}
inside
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),w}^{\circ}
.

The reduced word
\dot{w}=s_{1}\dots s_{n}
determines a minimal gallery
\Gamma=({\mathbf{a}}_{0},{\mathbf{a}}_{1},\dots,{\mathbf{a}}_{n})
, where
{\mathbf{a}}_{i}=s_{1}\dots s_{i}({\mathbf{a}})
, going from the alcove
{\mathbf{a}}
fixed by
{\mathcal{I}}(O)
to its
\dot{w}
-conjugate.
Let
\alpha_{i}
be the unique positive affine root such that
\partial\alpha_{i}
is the wall separating
{\mathbf{a}}_{i-1}
and
{\mathbf{a}}_{i}
. We claim that

(5.11)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),w}^{\circ}=L_{k}^{+}{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\ldots\cdot L_{k}^{+}{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha_{n}}w.

This follows by expanding the Demazure twisted product, pulling across the simple reflections to the right, compare with
Proposition

4.5
.
Indeed,
s_{i-1}\dots s_{1}(\alpha_{i})
is by construction the positive simple affine root attached to
s_{i}
. We need to produce an
O
-cocharacter
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathcal{S}}
whose induced
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-action repels every affine root group
L^{+}{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha_{i}}
, because then it would also repel
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),w}^{\circ}
by (
5.11
). This follows now by
[
HN02
, Corollary 5.6]
but we give below a quick proof for the reader’s convenience.

Consider the subset
\Phi_{G,w}\subset\Phi_{G}
of all euclidean roots
a=\nabla\alpha
which are gradients of the prescribed affine root
\alpha_{i}
attached to
\Gamma
. If
b
denotes the barycenter of
{\mathbf{a}}
, then by definition
a(\dot{w}b-b)<0
is stricly negative for
a\in\Phi_{G,w}
.
In particular,
\Phi_{G,w}
consists entirely of negative
B
-roots where
S\subset B\subset G
is a Borel subgroup whose closed Weyl chamber contains the vector
\dot{w}b-b
.
So we may take any
B
-dominant regular coweight
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to S
which uniquely extends to the desired
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathcal{S}}
because
S
is
F
-split.
∎

Now assume that
{\lambda}
is regular.
Then
M_{\lambda}=T
is a maximal torus,
P_{\lambda}=B
a Borel subgroup with unipotent radical
U
defined over
F
.
The stratification (
5.8
) becomes

(5.12)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}=\bigcup_{w\in\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}}{\mathcal{S}}_{w},\ {\mathcal{S}}_{w}=L_{k}U\cdot w

and the strata
{\mathcal{S}}_{w}
are called semi-infinite orbits, compare with
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.3.1]
.
Recall that there is a semi-infinite Bruhat order
\leq^{\frac{\infty}{2}}
on
\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
defined by:

(5.13)

v\leq^{\frac{\infty}{2}}w\;\;\iff\;\;\forall\;\nu_{I}\gg 0\colon\;{\nu_{I}}(\pi)\cdot v\leq{{\nu_{I}}(\pi)}\cdot w

where
X_{\ast}(T)_{I}\subset\tilde{W}
,
\nu_{I}\mapsto\nu_{I}(\pi)
is viewed as a subgroup using the Kottwitz morphism, see (
3.19
), and where
\nu_{I}\gg 0
means that
\nu_{I}
is sufficiently
B
-dominant.
This order was first introduced by Lusztig in
[
Lus80
]
and depends on the
F
-Borel subgroup
B
attracted by
{\lambda}
.

Proposition 5.4
.

The ind-closure of
{\mathcal{S}}_{w}
inside
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
is given by the perfect sub-ind-scheme whose geometric points factor through some
{\mathcal{S}}_{v}
with
v\leq^{\frac{\infty}{2}}w
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{I}}\to{\mathcal{G}}
be an auxiliary Iwahori model, fixed for the remainder of the proof. Suppose there is a curve
{\mathcal{C}}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
containing
v
and whose complement
{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}:={\mathcal{C}}\setminus\{v\}
is contained in
{\mathcal{S}}_{w}=L_{k}U\cdot w
. Now, notice that, for sufficiently dominant
\nu_{I}
, one gets the inclusion

(5.14)

\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}\subset L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{I}}\cdot{\nu_{I}}(\pi)\cdot w,

because conjugation by
\nu_{I}(\pi)
moves any given perfect
k
-subscheme of
L_{k}U
inside the Iwahori loop group
L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{I}}\subset L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
. This implies the inequality
{{\nu_{I}}(\pi)}v\leq{{\nu_{I}}(\pi)}w
, and therefore
v\leq^{\frac{\infty}{2}}w
.

Conversely, assume that the inequality
v\leq^{\frac{\infty}{2}}w
holds.
By definition, for all sufficiently dominant translations
\nu_{I}\in X_{\ast}(T)_{I}
, the inequality
\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w\leq\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot v
holds in the Bruhat order.
By enlarging
\nu_{I}
if necessary, we may assume the
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\circ}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w}
is of the form
\prod_{\alpha\in\Gamma}L_{k}^{+}{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}\cdot\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w
where all of the
\alpha\in\Gamma
have positive gradient.
There is a curve
{\mathcal{C}}
in
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w}
joining
\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w
to
\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot v
since
\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot v\leq\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w
.
By our assumption on
\nu_{I}(\pi)
, we have
{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w}
for
{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}:={\mathcal{C}}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w}^{\circ}
.
Now, the map
t_{\nu_{I}}\colon{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),w}\to{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{({\mathcal{I}},{\mathcal{G}}),\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w}
induced by left translation with
\nu_{I}(\pi)
is an isomorphism and hence induces
{\mathcal{S}}_{w}\cong{\mathcal{S}}_{\nu_{I}(\pi)\cdot w}
.
Then the curve
t_{\nu_{I}}^{-1}({\mathcal{C}})
joins
w
to
v
, and
t_{\nu_{I}}^{-1}({\mathcal{C}}^{\circ})\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{w}
.
∎

Next, we extend the equi-dimensionality of Mirković–Vilonen cycles
[
MV07
, Theorem 3.2]
(see also
[
Zhu17a
, Corollary 2.8]
and
[
FS21
, Corollary VI.3.8]
) from split groups to twisted groups as follows.
We continue to assume that
{\lambda}
is regular and, additionally, that
{\mathcal{G}}
is special parahoric.
Then
X_{\ast}(T)_{I}=\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
and (
5.12
) becomes

(5.15)

{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}=\bigcup_{\nu_{I}\in X_{*}(T)_{I}}{\mathcal{S}}_{\nu_{I}},\ {\mathcal{S}}_{\nu_{I}}=L_{k}U\cdot{\nu_{I}}(\pi).

If
{\mathcal{G}}
is reductive, then
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}
is the Witt vector affine Grassmannian studied in
[
Zhu17a
]
.
So, in general,
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}
can be regarded as a twisted version when
{\mathcal{G}}
is special parahoric.
Indeed,
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{\mathcal{G}}=\operatorname{colim}{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}}
where
\mu_{I}
runs through
X_{*}(T)_{I,+}
, the image of the
B
-dominant cocharacters under the projection
X_{*}(T)\rightarrow X_{*}(T)_{I}
equipped with the induced dominance order and
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}}:={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}(\pi)}
, as is usual notation for (twisted) affine Grassmannians.
Also, the semi-infinite Bruhat order on
X_{\ast}(T)_{I}
specializes to the dominance relation, that is,
\nu_{I}^{\prime}\leq^{\frac{\infty}{2}}\nu_{I}
if and only if
\nu_{I}-\nu_{I}^{\prime}
is a sum of coinvariants of positive roots with non-negative coefficients.

Lemma 5.5
.

For any
\nu_{I}\in X_{*}(T)_{I}
, the intersection
{\mathcal{S}}_{\nu_{I}}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}}
is non-empty if and only if
\nu_{I}
lies in the
W_{\mathcal{G}}
-orbit of some
\mu_{I}^{\prime}\in X_{*}(T)_{I,+}
with
\mu^{\prime}_{I}\leq\mu_{I}
.
In this case, it is affine and equidimensional of dimension
\langle\rho_{G},\nu+\mu\rangle
.

Here
\rho_{G}\in X^{*}(T)
denotes the half sum of the
B
-positive roots.
We note that the pairing
\langle\rho_{G},\nu+\mu\rangle
is well-defined independently of the choice of lifts
\nu,\mu\in X_{*}(T)
of
\nu_{I},\mu_{I}
because
\rho_{G}
is
I
-invariant.

Proof of
Lemma

5.5
.

The map
({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}})^{+}\rightarrow{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}}
induces an isomorphism (see
Theorem

5.2
)

(5.16)

({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}})^{+}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\bigsqcup_{\nu_{I}\in X_{*}(T)_{I}}{\mathcal{S}}_{\nu_{I}}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}}.

Under
({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}})^{+}\rightarrow({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}})^{0}
, the component
{\mathcal{S}}_{\nu_{I}}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}}
contracts to
\underline{\{\nu_{I}\}}={\operatorname{Spec}}(k)
.
Thus, it is affine because Bialynicki-Birula maps for schemes are affine by
[
Ric19
, Corollary 1.12]
.
Also,
({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}})^{0}
identifies with the constant scheme associated with the subset of
\nu_{I}\in X_{*}(T)_{I}
lying in the
W_{\mathcal{G}}
-orbit of some
\mu_{I}^{\prime}\in X_{*}(T)_{I,+}
with
\mu^{\prime}_{I}\leq\mu_{I}
.
So only such
\nu_{I}
contribute to (
5.16
), and as the Bialynicki-Birula map has a section, the non-emptiness criterion for
{\mathcal{S}}_{\nu_{I}}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}}
holds true.
Furthermore, the union over all
{\mathcal{S}}_{\nu^{\prime}_{I}}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}}
with
\nu_{I}^{\prime}\leq^{\frac{\infty}{2}}\nu_{I}
is a closed perfect subscheme by
Proposition

5.4
.

As noted in
[
FS21
, Corollary VI.3.8]
, the affineness implies the dimension formula once we show that
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mu_{I}^{\operatorname{anti}}}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I}}
is a point where
\mu_{I}^{\operatorname{anti}}
is the antidominant element in the
W_{\mathcal{G}}
-orbit of
\mu_{I}
.
This follows from the proof of
Lemma

5.3
.
∎

6.
Nearby cycles of étale sheaves

6.1.
Recollections

In
[
Sch17
]
, Scholze constructs a category of étale sheaves

(6.1)

{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda):={\operatorname{D}}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)

for all small v-stacks
X
.
As coefficients
\Lambda
, we allow prime-to-
p
torsion rings or, by the adic formalism of
[
Sch17
, Section 26]
, an
\ell
-torsion free, complete
\ell
-adic ring for
\ell\neq p
, or a ring of the form
\Lambda=\Lambda_{0}[\ell^{-1}]
where
\Lambda_{0}
is as in the previous case.
In the final case, as this is not covered in
[
Sch17
]
, we define the triangulated category

(6.2)

{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda):={\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda_{0})\otimes_{\Lambda_{0}}\Lambda,

in analogy to the classical definition for schemes, for example, see
[
KW01
, Appendix A]
.
The adic formalism of
[
Sch17
, Section 26]
carries over to the categories (
6.2
).
Finally, we also allow
\Lambda
to be a filtered colimit of the aforementioned rings, with the obvious definition for the categories.
This includes algebraic field extensions
L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
and their rings of integers
O_{L}
.

The categories of étale sheaves are equipped with the usual six functors formalism: the endofunctors
\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}
,
\RHom
and functors
Rf_{*}
,
f^{*}
for a morphism
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
of small v-stacks.
If
f
is compactifiable and representable in locally spatial diamonds with
\operatorname{dim.\!tr}f<\infty
, we dispose of the functors
Rf_{!}
,
Rf^{!}
, completing the six functor formalism.

In general, the categories
{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda)
and the six functors are rather inexplicit, constructed through v-descent using Lurie’s
\infty
-categorical machinery.
Nevertheless, whenever
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
is a morphism between locally spatial diamonds, then
X
and
Y
admit a well-defined étale site and Scholze’s operations are very closely related to the operations that one can construct site-theoretically, see
[
Sch17
, Proposition 14.15, Section 17]
.

When
X
and
Y
are locally spatial diamonds we say that an object
A\in{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda)
is ULA (=universally locally acyclic) with respect to
f
if, for all locally spatial diamonds
Y^{\prime}\to Y
, the pullback
A^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{D}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)
is overconvergent along the fibers of
f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}=X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
and
R(f^{\prime}\circ j^{\prime})_{!}j^{\prime}{}^{*}A
is perfect-constructible for all separated étale neighborhoods
j^{\prime}\colon U^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
for which
f^{\prime}\circ j^{\prime}
is quasi-compact, see
[
FS21
, Definition IV.2.1]
.
If
\Lambda
is
\ell
-adic as above, then a complex
A\in{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda)
is called perfect-constructible if
A\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\Lambda}\Lambda/\ell
is étale locally perfect-constant after passing to a constructible stratification, equivalently
A\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\Lambda}\Lambda/\ell^{n}
are so for all
n\geq 1
.
Finally, if
\Lambda=\Lambda_{0}[\ell^{-1}]
is as in (
6.2
), then an object in
{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda)
is called perfect-constructible if it admits a
\Lambda_{0}
-lattice which is so.
For
X
and
Y
more general v-stacks (and
f\colon X\to Y
representable in locally spatial diamonds), we call
A
ULA if it is ULA after any base change
S\to Y
with
S
a locally spatial diamond.

Suppose
X
is a small v-stack proper and representable in spatial diamonds over a base
S
, and that
X
is equipped with an action by
{\mathbb{G}}_{m,S}^{\diamondsuit}
satisfying the conditions
[
FS21
, Hypothesis IV.6.1.]
.
One can consider the v-stacks

(6.3)

X^{\pm}={\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}}\big(({\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{\pm,\diamondsuit},X\big)

which (by hypothesis) are represented by a finite partition of
X
into locally closed subsets.
This also induces a partition of the fixed-point v-stack
X^{0}=X^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}}
into closed and open subsets.
We have inclusion maps
q^{\pm}\colon X^{\pm}\to X
and projection maps
p^{\pm}\colon X^{\pm}\to X^{0}
, from that we obtain the hyperbolic localization functor

(6.4)

L_{X/S}\colon{\operatorname{D}}(X/{\mathbb{G}}_{m,S}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)\rightarrow{\operatorname{D}}(X^{0},\Lambda),

which can be expressed as
R(p^{+})_{!}(q^{+})^{*}
or equivalently as
R(p^{-})_{*}R(q^{-})^{!}
by
[
FS21
, Theorem IV.6.5]
.
This functor enjoys plenty of compatibilities, in analogy to
[
Ric19
]
, which we will exploit to compute nearby cycles, see
[
FS21
, Propositions IV.6.12, IV.6.13, IV.6.14]
.

6.2.
Over
C

We continue with the notation and denote by
F/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
a complete discretely valued field with ring of integers
O
and perfect residue field
k
of characteristic
p>0
.
Also, we fix a complete algebraic closure
C/F
, and a connected reductive
F
-group
G
.

In this section, we recall the structure of the categories of monodromic sheaves with bounded support
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
and
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{mon},\operatorname{bd}}
studied in
[
FS21
, Section VI]
.
As in
Section

5
, for any cocharacter
\lambda\colon{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to G_{C}
, we have the induced
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}
-action on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C}
, whose attractors only depend on the attracting parabolic
P\subset G_{C}
.

In particular, hyperbolic localization gives a constant terms functor

(6.5)

\operatorname{CT}_{P}\colon{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}\rightarrow{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{mon},\operatorname{bd}}\xrightarrow{L_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}/_{C}}}{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{M,C},\Lambda)

providing the main tool to effectively study the category of derived étale sheaves on
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C}
as in
[
FS21
, Corollary VI.3.5]
.
One of the crucial techniques is the following conservativity lemma
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.4.2]
whose proof we sketch for convenience.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
T\subset B\subset G_{C}
be an arbitrary maximal torus and a Borel containing it.
Then
A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
vanishes if and only if
\operatorname{CT}_{B}(A)\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,C},\Lambda)
does.

Proof.

The proof is done by considering a maximal strata where
A
is concentrated.
This strata is of the form
[\operatorname{{Spd}}C/(L^{+}_{C}G)_{\mu}]
for the stabilizer of
\mu\in X_{*}(T)_{+}
.
The attractor of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,C,\mu}
at the anti-dominant coweight
-\mu
with respect to
B
is an isolated point.
Using the
R(p^{+})_{!}(q^{+})^{*}
-version of hyperbolic localization, we see that the fiber of
\operatorname{CT}_{B}
over
\mu\in{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T}
agrees with pullback to this point.
∎

This allows us to localize several properties of derived objects in
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
.
For instance,
A
is ULA if and only if
\operatorname{CT}_{B}(A)
which, in turn, is equivalent to
[\mu]^{*}A
being a perfect object for all maps
[\mu]\colon\operatorname{{Spd}}C\to{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G}
with
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
, see
[
FS21
, Propositions VI.6.4, VI.6.5]
.

Next, we move to the natural perverse t-structure on
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C,\mu},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
, see
[
FS21
, Definition/Proposition VI.7.1]
.
This is given in terms of the following subcategory

(6.6)

{}^{p}{\operatorname{D}}^{\leq 0}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}=\{A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}\colon j_{\mu}^{*}A\in{\operatorname{D}}^{\leq-\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle}\},

which determines
{}^{p}{\operatorname{D}}^{\geq 0}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)
uniquely.
Intersecting these two, we get the category of perverse sheaves
{\operatorname{Perv}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)
.

Thanks to
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.7.4]
, the t-structure is preserved under and detected by
\operatorname{CT}_{B}[\deg_{B}]
.
Here, for any
C
-parabolic
B\subset P
with Levi quotient
M
, the degree is the locally constant function on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{P,C}
induced by

(6.7)

\deg_{P}(\lambda)=\langle 2\rho_{G}-2\rho_{M},\lambda\rangle,

where
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)
is a coweight and
\rho_{M}
is the half-sum of all
B
-positive
M
-roots.
The main geometric fact used in the proof of
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.7.4]
is the equidimensionality of semi-infinite orbits.

When working with torsion coefficients, it is convenient to single out flat perverse sheaves, which are those objects
A
such that for every
\Lambda
-module
M
the complex
A\otimes_{\Lambda}^{\mathbb{L}}M
is perverse.

Definition 6.2
.

The Satake category
{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)
is the full subcategory of flat ULA objects in
{\operatorname{Perv}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)
.

The Satake category is endowed with a monoidal product

(6.8)

\star\colon{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)\times{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)\rightarrow{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)

arising from the convolution Hecke stack
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C}\widetilde{\times}{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C}
, see
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.8.1]
.
Due to the fusion interpretation
[
FS21
, Definition/Proposition VI.9.4]
, the monoidal structure is naturally symmetric monoidal.

Taking cohomology of the affine Grassmannian furnishes a fiber functor

(6.9)

F\colon{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)\rightarrow{\operatorname{Rep}}(\Lambda)

to the category of
\Lambda
-finite locally free modules and the Tannakian formalism gives us an interpretation of these categories in terms of a group of automorphisms.

Theorem 6.3

(Fargues–Scholze)
.

The automorphism group of
F
is naturally isomorphic to the Langlands dual group
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
formed over
\Lambda
.

One may regard the dual group
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
as combinatorially defined in terms of root data (a priori), or as its correct natural definition (a posteriori). The cyclotomic twist in
[
FS21
, Theorem VI.11.1]
is not needed, because we are working over the algebraically closed field
C
.

6.3.
Over
\bar{k}

Let
{\mathcal{G}}
be a parahoric
O
-model of
G
.
In this section, we look at what happens with the geometric special fiber Hecke v-stack
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}=L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}^{\diamondsuit}\backslash L_{\bar{k}}G^{\diamondsuit}/L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}^{\diamondsuit}
.
We note that the categories of étale sheaves compare well to their scheme-theoretic companions, see
Proposition

A.5
.

We continue with the notation and, in addition, fix a maximal
\breve{F}
-split
F
-torus
S\subset G
containing a maximal
F
-split torus (see
[
BT84
, Proposition 5.1.10]
) with centralizer
T
, which is a maximal
F
-torus inside
G
, such that their connected Néron
O
-models
{\mathcal{S}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
embed into
{\mathcal{G}}
.
Each parabolic subgroup
P\subset G_{\breve{F}}
with Levi
M
containing
S_{\breve{F}}
extends to a diagram of
O
-group schemes
{\mathcal{M}}\leftarrow{\mathcal{P}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{G}}_{\breve{O}}
by taking flat closures.
Again, choosing a cocharacter
{\lambda}\colon{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{\breve{O}}\subset{\mathcal{G}}_{\breve{O}}
with
M=M_{\lambda}
and
P=P^{+}_{\lambda}
, the formalism of
Section

5
applies to define
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\diamondsuit}
-actions on the pro-smooth cover
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}={{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\diamondsuit}
.
This gives rise to constant term functors

(6.10)

\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}\colon{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}\rightarrow{\operatorname{D}}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{0,\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}},

not depending on the choice of
{\lambda}
such that
M=M_{\lambda}
and
P=P^{+}_{\lambda}
.
Here, the fixed points
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{0}
contain
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{M}},\bar{k}}
as an open and closed sub-ind-scheme by
Theorem

5.2
, but are strictly bigger unless
{\mathcal{G}}_{\breve{O}}
is special parahoric.

As in the previous section, we analyse the key properties ULA, flatness and perversity.
The crucial step is the following conservativity result.

Proposition 6.4
.

An object
A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
vanishes if and only if
\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{B}}(A)
does for every
\breve{F}
-Borel
S_{\breve{F}}\subset B\subset G_{\breve{F}}
.

Proof.

Just like in
Lemma

6.1
, we argue on a maximal strata of
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
where
A
does not vanish, say one indexed by some
w
.
In this case, by
Lemma

5.3
there is a choice of
\breve{F}
-Borel
B
for which the associated attractor intersects
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w}
in an isolated point.
In this case,
\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{B}}
agrees with pullback to this point.
∎

Definition 6.5
.

An object
A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
is ULA whenever its pullback to
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w}^{\diamondsuit}
is ULA over
\operatorname{{Spd}}\bar{k}
.

A priori, this notion depends on the choice of left or right trivialization, but it follows a posteriori from
Proposition

6.7
that it does not, see
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.6.2]
.
The ULA property interacts very well with constant terms:

Proposition 6.6
.

If
A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
is ULA, then so is
\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}(A)
.
Conversely, if
\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{B}}(A)
is ULA for all Borel subgroups
S_{\breve{F}}\subset B\subset G_{\breve{F}}
, then so is
A
.

Proof.

Abusing notation, we also call
A
the pullback of this object to
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\diamondsuit}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
.
By
[
FS21
, Theorem IV.2.23]
, to prove that for
B=A
or
B=\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}(A)
, the object
B
is ULA it is enough to show that

(6.11)

p_{1}^{*}{\mathbb{D}}(B)\otimes p_{2}^{*}B\rightarrow\RHom(p_{1}^{*}B,Rp_{2}^{!}B)

is an isomorphism.
Now, for any pair of flat closures of parabolics
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}
a direct computation (using properties of hyperbolic localization, cf. the proof of
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.6.4]
) shows that

(6.12)

\operatorname{CT}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{P}}_{2}}(p_{1}^{*}{\mathbb{D}}(A)\otimes p_{2}^{*}A)=p_{1}^{*}{\mathbb{D}}(\operatorname{CT}_{{{\mathcal{P}}^{-}_{1}}}(A))\otimes p_{2}^{*}\operatorname{CT}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2}}(A)

and that

(6.13)

\operatorname{CT}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{P}}_{2}}(\RHom(p_{1}^{*}A,Rp_{2}^{!}A))=\RHom(p_{1}^{*}(\operatorname{CT}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-}_{1}}(A),Rp_{2}^{!}\operatorname{CT}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2}}(A)))

where
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{-}
is opposite to
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}
. In the forward direction, it is enough to use this for
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}={\mathcal{P}}^{-}
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}={\mathcal{P}}
.
For the converse, we let
K
denote the cone of
Equation

6.11
.
By the conservativity of
Proposition

6.4
, it is enough to prove
\operatorname{CT}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{1},{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}(K)=0
for all
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{B}}_{2}
since these exhaust the Borel subgroups of of
G_{\breve{F}}\times G_{\breve{F}}
.
But this follows from the computation above,
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.19]
and the hypothesis that
\operatorname{CT}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}(A)
is ULA.
∎

We prove that ULA sheaves admit an easy description in terms of restrictions to Schubert strata:

Proposition 6.7
.

The following are equivalent for an object
A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
:

(1)

A
is ULA.

(2)

For all strata of
A
pullback along

[w]\colon\operatorname{{Spd}}\bar{k}\rightarrow{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}

is a perfect complex
17
17

17

If
\Lambda=\Lambda_{0}[\ell^{-1}]
as in (
6.2
), then we require the pullback to arise as the
\ell
localization of a perfect complex over
\Lambda_{0}
.
in

(6.14)

{\operatorname{D}}(\operatorname{{Spd}}\bar{k},\Lambda)={\operatorname{D}}(\Lambda).

(3)

The pullback to
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\diamondsuit}
lies in

(6.15)

{\operatorname{D}}_{\operatorname{cons}}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}\subset{\operatorname{D}}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{ula},\operatorname{bd}},

where
{\operatorname{D}}_{\operatorname{cons}}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
is the category of perfect-constructible
\Lambda
-sheaves with bounded support on the ind-scheme
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
and the inclusion is the one constructed in
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
.

Proof.

The equivalence of (2) and (3) follows from
Proposition

A.5
, and the fact that the equivalence in
Proposition

A.5
is compatible with pullback to
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
, respectively to
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{\diamondsuit}
or to strata.

Let
j_{w}
denote the inclusion of the strata in
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
corresponding to
w
.
For proving that (2) implies (1) one can, as in
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.6.5]
, reduce to showing that
R(j_{w})_{!}\Lambda
is ULA (for each
w
).
But their pullback to
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
are clearly algebraic and by
Proposition

A.1
they are also ULA, see also
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.30]
.
Alternatively one can use the Demazure resolution, compare with
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.5.7]
.

For the converse implication, we induct on the number of strata where
A
does not vanish and consider the cone of
R(j_{w})_{!}j^{\ast}_{w}A\to A
for a maximal strata
w
.
Indeed, pullback by open immersion preserves being ULA by
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.2.13.(i)]
.
Then we apply
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.4.1]
to see that
j^{\ast}_{w}A\in{\operatorname{D}}(\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\mu},\Lambda)
has a perfect stalk, and use that
R(j_{w})_{!}(j_{w}^{\ast}A)
is ULA, by the proven (2) implies (1).
Then we can conclude by induction.

∎

Arguing as in
[
FS21
, Definition/Proposition VI.7.1]
, we can define a perverse t-structure.

Definition 6.8
.

The perverse t-structure on
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
is the only such that

(6.16)

{}^{p}{\operatorname{D}}^{\leq 0}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)=\{A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)\colon j_{w}^{*}A\in{\operatorname{D}}^{\leq-l(w)}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)\},

respectively

(6.17)

{}^{p}{\operatorname{D}}^{\geq 0}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)=\{A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)\colon Rj_{w}^{!}A\in{\operatorname{D}}^{\geq-l(w)}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)\}.

Perverse sheaves

(6.18)

{\operatorname{Perv}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)=\,^{p}{\operatorname{D}}^{\leq 0}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)\cap\,^{p}{\operatorname{D}}^{\geq 0}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)

are the heart of the t-structure.
Such an
A
is flat perverse if in addition
A\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\Lambda}M
is in
{\operatorname{Perv}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)
for all
\Lambda
-modules
M
.

We note that, in general, there cannot be any degree shifts such that
\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}[\deg_{\mathcal{P}}]
preserves the perverse t-structure, due to lack of parity.
But, we define

(6.19)

\deg_{\mathcal{P}}({\lambda}_{I})=\langle 2\rho_{G}-2\rho_{M},{\lambda}\rangle

for translation elements
{\lambda}_{I}\in X_{*}(T)_{I}
.
This is useful for the following result:

Proposition 6.9
.

Assume that
{\mathcal{G}}_{\breve{O}}
is special parahoric, and let
A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)
.
Then
A
is perverse if and only if
\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{B}}(A)[\deg_{\mathcal{B}}]\in{\operatorname{Perv}}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{T}},\bar{k}}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)
for all Borel subgroups
S_{\breve{F}}\subset B\subset G_{\breve{F}}
.
The same applies to the flat objects.

Proof.

It suffices to follow the proof of
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.7.4]
.
For preserving the t-structure, we use the fact that the non-empty intersections
{\mathcal{S}}_{\bar{k},{\lambda}_{I}}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\nu_{I}}

are equidimensional of dimension
\langle 2\rho_{G},\lambda+\nu\rangle
, see
Lemma

5.5
.
The converse then follows from
Proposition

6.4
.
∎

In particular, it is now permitted to introduce the Satake category at special level.
Notice that there is no hope of such a well-behaved class of objects to exist at arbitrary level, because the quotient
\tilde{W}/W_{\mathcal{G}}
carries no natural abelian structure.

Definition 6.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\breve{O}}
be special parahoric.
Then the Satake category
{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)
is the full subcategory of
{\operatorname{Perv}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)
comprised of flat ULA objects.

This category lies within the category of perverse sheaves
{\operatorname{Perv}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{sch}},\Lambda)
on the schematic Hecke stack
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{sch}}=L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}\backslash L_{\bar{k}}G/L_{\bar{k}}^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
by
Proposition

6.7
and
Appendix

A
for the comparison with sheaves on schematic v-stacks.
It carries moreover a monoidal structure given by convolution
\star
.

6.4.
Over
O_{C}

Let
f\colon X\to{\operatorname{Spec}}(O_{C})
be a scheme of finite presentation over
O_{C}
and denote by
j
the inclusion
X_{\eta}\hookrightarrow X
of the generic fiber.
In
[
HS21
, Theorem 1.7]
, Hansen and Scholze prove that the pullback functor

(6.20)

j^{*}\colon{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda)\to{\operatorname{D}}(X_{\eta},\Lambda)

restricts to an equivalence between
f
-ULA and
f_{\eta}
-ULA objects.
In the setup of diamonds, the argument for full faithfulness is the same as was explained to us by Scholze, and it consists of proving the adjunction map
A\to Rj_{*}j^{*}A
is an isomorphism.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
X
be a small v-sheaf over
\operatorname{{Spd}}(O_{C})
representable in locally spatial diamonds, compactifiable and of finite trascendence degree.
Let
A\in{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda)
be ULA for the structure map to
\operatorname{{Spd}}(O_{C})
.
Then
A\to Rj^{\prime}_{\ast}j^{\prime\ast}A
is a isomorphism, where
j^{\prime}:X_{\eta}\to X
and
j:\operatorname{{Spd}}(C)\to\operatorname{{Spd}}(O_{C})
denote the inclusion of generic fibers.

Proof.

By hypothesis
j^{\prime\ast}A
is ULA with respect to
\operatorname{{Spd}}(C)
.
In particular, by
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.19]
the map

(6.21)

j^{\prime\ast}A\otimes_{\Lambda}^{\mathbb{L}}\Lambda\cong\RHom(\mathbb{D}_{X_{\eta}/\operatorname{{Spd}}(C)}(j^{\prime\ast}A),Rf_{\eta}^{!}\Lambda)

is an isomorphism. Since
j^{\prime}
is an open immersion
j^{\prime\ast}=Rj^{\prime!}
and
\mathbb{D}_{X_{\eta}/\operatorname{{Spd}}(C)}(j^{\prime\ast}A)=j^{\prime\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/\operatorname{{Spd}}(O_{C})}(A)
as follows from
[
Sch17
, Theorem 1.8.(v)]
. We get

(6.22)

\displaystyle Rj^{\prime}_{\ast}j^{\prime\ast}A

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle Rj^{\prime}_{\ast}\RHom(j^{\prime\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/\operatorname{{Spd}}(O_{C})}(A),Rf_{\eta}^{!}\Lambda)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\RHom(\mathbb{D}_{X/\operatorname{{Spd}}(O_{C})}(A),Rj^{\prime}_{\ast}Rf_{\eta}^{!}\Lambda)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\RHom(\mathbb{D}_{X/\operatorname{{Spd}}(O_{C})}(A),Rf^{!}Rj_{\ast}\Lambda)

the result now follows from the identity
\Lambda=Rj_{\ast}\Lambda
and double duality for ULA sheaves.
∎

In particular, a
f_{\eta}
-ULA object
A
comes from a
f
-ULA object if and only if
Rj_{*}j^{\ast}A
is
f
-ULA.

Below, we prove essential surjectivity for
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}
, the Hecke stack over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}
.
For hyperspecial parahoric
{\mathcal{G}}
, this is
[
FS21
, Corollary VI.6.7]
. Before doing this, recall that hyperbolic localization allows us to define again a constant term functors

(6.23)

\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}\colon{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}\rightarrow{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}^{0},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}.

By
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.6.12]
, there is a natural equivalence

(6.24)

\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ Rj_{{\mathcal{G}},*}\cong Rj_{{\mathcal{M}},*}\circ\operatorname{CT}_{P},

with
j_{\mathcal{G}},j_{\mathcal{M}}
denoting the inclusion of the respective generic fibers.
Now, we can probe integral ULA objects.

Proposition 6.12
.

Consider the inclusion of Hecke stacks
j\colon{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C}\to{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}
.
There is an equivalence

(6.25)

j^{*}\colon{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd},\operatorname{ula}}\to{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd},\operatorname{ula}},

whose inverse functor is
Rj_{*}
.

Proof.

Suppose
A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd},\operatorname{ula}}
, it suffices to prove
Rj_{*}A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd},\operatorname{ula}}
.
Let
B
denote the pullback of
A
to
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}
, which by definition is ULA.
By smooth base change,
Rj_{*}A
pulls back to
Rj_{*}B
(here we implicitly use
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.4.1]
).
By
[
FS21
, Theorem IV.2.23]
, we must show

(6.26)

p_{1}^{*}{\mathbb{D}}(Rj_{*}B)\otimes p_{2}^{*}Rj_{*}B\rightarrow\RHom(p_{1}^{*}Rj_{*}B,Rp_{2}^{!}Rj_{*}B)

is an isomorphism.
Let
K
denote the cone of this map. By assumption, and since
j^{*}=Rj^{!}
, this map is an isomorphism on the generic fiber.
Consequently,
K=i_{*}L
for some
L\in D({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
and the inclusion
i\colon{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}\to{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}
.
We may use the conservativity result
Proposition

6.4
to prove
L=0
.
This reduces us to proving that
\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{B}}(Rj_{*}A)=Rj_{*}\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{B}}(A)
is ULA for all Borel subgroups
S_{\breve{F}}\subset B\subset G_{\breve{F}}
.
Now, the fixed-point locus
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}^{0}
of the action induced by
{\mathcal{B}}
is ind-representable by a locally finite type scheme over
O_{C}
of relative dimension
0
.
We call this scheme
X
and let
h\colon X_{\eta}\to X
the inclusion of generic fibers.
By inspection,
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}^{0},\Lambda)\cong{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda)
and
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},C}^{0},\Lambda)\cong{\operatorname{D}}(X_{\eta},\Lambda)
.
In particular
Rj_{*}\cong c_{X}^{*}Rh_{*}Rc_{X_{\eta},*}
with notation as in
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
.
By
[
HS21
, Theorem 1.7]
,
Rh_{*}
preserves ULA objects, which allows us to conclude the same holds for
Rj_{*}
.
∎

6.5.
Nearby cycles

We can now look at the nearby cycles functor

(6.27)

\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}}}:=i^{*}Rj_{*}\colon{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}\rightarrow{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}},

Arising from the diagram

(6.28)

{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C}\xrightarrow{j}{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}\xleftarrow{i}{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}

of geometric fibers inclusions of the integral Hecke stack.

Proposition 6.13
.

The functor of nearby cycles lies in a natural equivalence

(6.29)

\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}[\deg_{\mathcal{P}}]\circ\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}}}\cong\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}}\circ\operatorname{CT}_{P}[\deg_{P}],

that is, it commutes with shifted constant term functors.

Proof.

Without the shift, this is a direct consequence of
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.6.12]
.
Using
Theorem

5.2
, this also shows that
\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}}}
is supported on the open and closed sub-v-sheaf
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{M}},\bar{k}}\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{0}
.
So the shifts agree by definition.
∎

Surprisingly, this commutativity property delivers us a lot of control on the values assumed by
\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}
on the Satake category.

Corollary 6.14
.

Nearby cycles
\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}
restrict to a functor

(6.30)

{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd},\operatorname{ula}}\rightarrow{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd},\operatorname{ula}}

and, if
{\mathcal{G}}
is furthermore special parahoric, then it even restricts to

(6.31)

{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)\rightarrow{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)

between the Satake categories.

Proof.

This follows from
Proposition

6.6
and
Proposition

6.9
.
∎

Let us examine the nearby cycles
\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}({\operatorname{Sat}}(V))
applied to a Satake object
{\operatorname{Sat}}(V)\in{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)
corresponding to a
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
-representation
V
with
\mu
as its highest weight.
Given an
F
-Borel
B\subset G
, the commutativity of
Proposition

6.13
yields

(6.32)

\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{B}}[\deg_{\mathcal{B}}]\big(\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}({\operatorname{Sat}}(V))\big)=\bigoplus_{\lambda_{I}}V(\lambda_{I})\cdot{\lambda}_{I},

where now the
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
-representation is regarded as a
\widehat{T}^{I}_{\Lambda}
-representation by restriction. Here, we use that (by construction) the constant term functor corresponds via geometric Satake to restriction of representations, see
[
FS21
, Section VI.11]
.
In particular, we get:

Corollary 6.15
.

For a
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
-representation
V
with highest weight
\mu
, the compactly supported cohomology groups

(6.33)

\operatorname{H}^{l}_{c}\!\big({\mathcal{S}}_{\bar{k},w}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w},\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}({\operatorname{Sat}}(V))\big)

vanish for all
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
unless
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\mu}
.

Proof.

This follows from
Lemma

3.12
.
∎

We are now finally ready to compute the special fiber of the local model.

Theorem 6.16
.

There is an equality
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\diamondsuit}={\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu,k_{E}}
as sub-v-sheaves of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},k_{E}}^{\diamondsuit}
.

Proof.

By specializing the orbit of
\mu
under the finite Weyl group, it is easy to see that
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\diamondsuit}
is contained in the special fiber of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
.
By
Corollary

6.15
, it is thus enough to prove that for a maximal stratum in
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\mu}
enumerated by
w
, we have
\operatorname{H}^{l}_{c}({\mathcal{S}}_{\bar{k},w}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w},A)\neq 0
for some
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and for
A:=\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}({\operatorname{Sat}}(V))
for some
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
-representation
V
with highest weight
\mu
.
Using induction on
\mu
, we may and do assume that
w
lies in the open complement of the closed union of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},{\lambda}}
for all
{\lambda}<\mu
. Indeed, if
{\lambda}<\mu
, then
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\lambda}}^{\diamondsuit}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\diamondsuit}
. By
Lemma

5.3
, our Borel subgroup
S_{\breve{F}}\subset B\subset G_{\breve{F}}
can always be chosen such that
w
is an isolated point of the attractor
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w}^{+}
so that
\operatorname{H}^{*}_{c}({\mathcal{S}}_{\bar{k},w}\cap{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},w},A)=\operatorname{H}^{*}(\{w\},A)=:A_{w}
is the stalk of
A
at
w
.

Consider
X={\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}}U
where
U
denotes the analytic locus of the open unit ball
\mathbb{D}_{O_{C}}^{\diamondsuit}
.
Let
g\colon X_{C}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of the generic fiber.
Let
K
denote a completed algebraic closure of
k(\hskip-1.99168pt({t})\hskip-1.99168pt)
.
We may choose a
\operatorname{{Spd}}(K)
-valued point
\overline{w}
of
X
that lies over
w
.
It suffices to prove
A_{\overline{w}}
is not identically
0
.
Since
U
is smooth over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}
, the smooth base-change theorem and our inductive assumption on
w
allows us to compute

(6.34)

A_{\overline{w}}=(Rg_{*}\Lambda[\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle])_{\overline{w}},

where
V
is chosen to have weight multiplicity
1
at
\mu
.
Since
X_{C}
,
X
and
K^{\diamondsuit}
are locally spatial diamonds, we may compute the right-side term site-theoretically.
Letting
l:=-\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle
, we have

(6.35)

\operatorname{H}^{l}(A_{\overline{w}})=\operatorname{lim}_{W}\operatorname{H}^{0}(W_{C},\Lambda)

where
W
ranges over étale neighborhoods of
\overline{w}
in
X
.
By
Remark

4.15
and openness of
X\to{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}
, the generic fiber
X_{C}
is dense in
X
which proves that the above expression does not vanish.
∎

6.6.
Centrality of nearby cycles

In the classical theory, say, over function fields, it is known that nearby cycles on Hecke stacks give central perverse sheaves on partial affine flag varieties, see
[
Gai01
]
.
Centrality holds true in our context as well:

Proposition 6.17
.

For every
A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)
and
B\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)
with bounded support, there is a canonical isomorphism

(6.36)

\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}(A)\star B\cong B\star\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}(A)

in
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)
.

Proof.

We can repeat the proof of
[
Zhu14
, Proposition 7.4]
in our context:

Similar to
[
FS21
, Definition/Proposition VI.9.4]
, we work with the convolution integral Hecke stack

(6.37)

{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{I;I_{1},\ldots,I_{k}}\to(\operatorname{{Spd}}O)^{I},

where
I=I_{1}\sqcup\ldots\sqcup I_{k}
is a finite partitioned index set.
It parametrizes
{\mathcal{G}}
-bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
over
B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
together with isomorphisms of
{\mathcal{E}}_{j-1}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{j}
outside the reunion of the divisors
\xi_{i}
for all
i\in I_{j}
.
We fix
I:=\{1,2\}
and drop it from the notation.
There are three ordered partitions
\{1\}\sqcup\{2\}
,
\{2\}\sqcup\{1\}
and
\{1,2\}
, leading to the diagram of v-sheaves:

(6.38)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{\{1\},\{2\}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{\{1,2\}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}^{\{2\},\{1\}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}\times{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}}
m
n
p

q

The diagram arises by base change along the map
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}\to(\operatorname{{Spd}}O)^{2}
induced by the divisor
\pi=0
in the second coordinate.
The maps
m,n
are the natural projections given by remembering
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
, and are ind-proper, as one sees by pulling back to the
convolution affine Grassmannian, combine
Theorem

4.9
with the proof of
[
SW20
, Proposition 20.4.1]
.
The maps
p,q
are given by sending
({\mathcal{E}}_{0},{\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{2})
to the ordered pair
(({\mathcal{E}}_{0},{\mathcal{E}}_{1}),({\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{2}))
, respectively
(({\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{2}),({\mathcal{E}}_{0},{\mathcal{E}}_{1}))
, and are pro-(cohomologically smooth) because
L^{+}_{O}{\mathcal{G}}\to\operatorname{{Spd}}O
is so.
More precisely, one passes to a bounded part and factors the action of
L^{+}_{O}{\mathcal{G}}
through a congruence quotient.

Furthermore, the maps
m,n
are convolution maps in the special fiber and induce isomorphisms over the generic fiber such that (
6.38
) commutes.
The commutativity yields a canonical isomorphism by using adjunctions

(6.39)

Rm_{\eta,*}p^{*}_{\eta}(A\boxtimes B)\cong Rn_{\eta,*}q_{\eta}^{*}(A\boxtimes B),

which will induce the desired isomorphism (
6.36
) upon applying the nearby cycles for the family
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\{1,2\}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}}
.
Indeed, since
(Rj_{*}A)\boxtimes B
is ULA by
Proposition

6.12
and
[
FS21
, Corollary IV.2.25]
for outer tensor products, it is still ULA after cohomologically smooth pullback along
p,q
and proper pushforward along
m,n
(here, we use that the support of
A,B
is bounded).
Thus, (
6.39
) canonically extends integrally yielding (
6.36
) after restriction to the special fiber. ∎

The following would be a natural reinforcement of the previous proposition to also preserving perversity.
For schemes, nearby cycles always preserve perversity
[
HS21
, Lemma 6.3]
.
In our setting, this is not immediate and would transport Gaitsgory’s central functor
[
Gai01
]
to the
p
-adic context.

Conjecture 6.18
.

For every
A\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,C},\Lambda)
, the
\Lambda
-flat central sheaf
\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}(A)\in{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd},\operatorname{ula}}
is perverse.

By a combination of
Corollary

6.14
and
Proposition

6.17
, we know that
6.18
holds true whenever
{\mathcal{G}}_{\breve{O}}
is special parahoric.
Also, using the representability in
Theorem

1.1
and a comparison with schematic nearby cycles (see
[
Sch17
, Proposition 27.6.]
), the conjecture holds true whenever
\mu
is minuscule.
In general, we lack tools to verify
6.18
– shifted constant terms appear to be insufficient – but one still expects some form of Artin vanishing to hold in this very particular context of the Hecke stack.

7.
Minuscule means representable

Our goal in this section is to prove the Scholze--Weinstein conjecture on minuscule
18
18

18

Recall that this is sharp, due to the theory of Banach-Colmez spaces, see
Proposition

4.8
.
local models
[
SW20
, Conjecture 21.4.1]
as stated in
Theorem

1.1
.
Its main feature is the representability part, see
[
Lou20
, Conjecture IV.4.18]
, which we verify without any assumption on the prime
p
or the pair
({\mathcal{G}},\mu)
, thereby showing the existence of weakly normal projective
O_{E}
-schemes
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
with natural, equivariant isomorphisms
({\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}})^{\diamondsuit}\cong{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
in all cases.

As for their geometry, we show under
1.9
and
1.13
that the special fiber is given by
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}}
, in particular reduced and even weakly normal. This implies the geometry part of the Scholze–Weinstein conjecture under those assumptions, see
[
Lou20
, Conjecture IV.4.19]
.

Recall that our strategy for representability involves specializations triples.
Since explicitly calculating the specialization map seems very hard, we need to consider convolutions of local models, so as to partially resolve
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
and understand their integral sections better.

7.1.
Convolution

We continue to denote by
F/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
a complete discretely valued field with ring of integers
O
and perfect residue field
k
of characteristic
p>0
.
Fix a completed algebraic closure
C/F
, and a connected reductive
F
-group
G
with parahoric
O
-model
{\mathcal{G}}
.
Also, we fix an auxiliary maximal
\breve{F}
-split
F
-torus
S\subset G
whose connected Néron model
{\mathcal{S}}
embeds in
{\mathcal{G}}
, see
[
BT84
, Proposition 5.10]
, and denote by
T
its centralizer with connected Néron model
{\mathcal{T}}\subset{\mathcal{G}}
. Additionally, we fix an auxiliary
\breve{F}
-Borel
T_{\breve{F}}\subset B\subset G_{\breve{F}}
.

When proving the representability of the v-sheaf local models, it is not difficult to reduce to the case that
G
is the Weil restriction of a split group, see proof of
Theorem

7.21
.
In this case, it will be helpful to partially resolve the local model via convolution.
We recall that the Beilinson–Drinfeld Grassmannian admits the following convolution variant

(7.1)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}\widetilde{\times}\dots\widetilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}:=L_{O}{\mathcal{G}}\times^{L^{+}_{O}{\mathcal{G}}}\cdots\times^{L^{+}_{O}{\mathcal{G}}}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}\to\operatorname{{Spd}}O,

which, in terms of torsors, parametrizes successive modifications of
{\mathcal{G}}
-torsors together with a generic trivialization of the last.
It admits natural closed sub-v-sheaves

(7.2)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}:={\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\times}\dots\widetilde{\times}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{n}},

for any sequence
\mu_{\bullet}=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})
of
B_{C}
-dominant coweights
\mu_{i}
of
T_{C}
, after base change to
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}
, where
E
is the reflex field of
\mu_{\bullet}
. We will still call them (convolution) local models for simplicity.
More precisely, denote by
\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}}
the preimage in
L_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}
of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}
, which is an
L_{O_{C}}^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
-torsor over
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
. Then

(7.3)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{1}}}\times^{L^{+}_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}}\cdots\times^{L^{+}_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}}\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{2}}}\times^{L^{+}_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{n}}.

This presentation is not “minimal” in the following sense:
Namely, given a contracted product
X\times^{H}Y
in any topos and a normal subgroup
N\subset H
acting trivially on
Y
, then the natural map

X\times^{H}Y\to X/N\times^{H/N}Y

is an isomorphism. Hence, in (
7.3
) we may replace
L^{+}_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}
by some sufficiently large congruence quotient, and accordingly the torsors
\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}}
by their pushforwards to these congruence quotients.
Let us note that the multiplication

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C}}

has image
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},|\mu_{\bullet}|}
with
|\mu_{\bullet}|:=\mu_{1}+\ldots+\mu_{n}
, and can therefore be regarded as a (partial) resolution of the latter. Regarding the structure of the convoluted local models, we can record the following.

Lemma 7.1
.

The convoluted local model
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}
is a proper, flat
p
-adic kimberlite over
