\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}
with topologically dense generic fiber.

Proof.

In order to prove that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}
is a proper flat
p
-adic kimberlite, we must first show that this proper v-sheaf is v-formalizing. Replace the universal
L^{+}_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}
-torsors by the corresponding
W^{+}_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}
-torsors, see
[
Gle20
, Definition 2.2.14]
and denote by
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\operatorname{for}}
the corresponding convolution. The natural map

(7.4)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\operatorname{for}}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}

is an isomorphism because both v-sheaves are qcqs and have the same geometric points, compare with
[
Gle20
, Proposition 2.2.27]
. It is now easy to show that it is formally separated and formally adic, with representable special fiber, see
Proposition

7.2
.
Via projection to the first factor we have a map

(7.5)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{1}},

which splits after pullback to
\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{1}}}
into the projection of the product
\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{1}}}\times_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},(\mu_{2},\ldots,\mu_{n})}
.
Hence, after replacing
L^{+}_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}
(and hence
\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{1}}}
) by a sufficiently large congruence quotient, we can deduce that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}
has dense generic fiber by induction on
n
,
Proposition

4.14
and preservation of closures under open maps.
∎

From now on, we will always consider sequences of minuscule dominant coweights
\mu_{\bullet}=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})
whose sum

(7.6)

\lvert\mu_{\bullet}\rvert=\mu_{1}+\dots+\mu_{n}

is still minuscule.
Basically, this means that the support of each
\mu_{i}
lies in disjoint irreducible components of the Dynkin diagram.
We say that a coweight is
tiny
if it is minuscule and its support is contained in at most one irreducible component.

Proposition 7.2
.

Both fibers of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}\to\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}
are representable. More precisely, we have isomorphisms

(7.7)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}C}\cong{\mathcal{F}}_{G,C,\mu_{\bullet}}^{\diamondsuit}\cong{\mathcal{F}}_{G,C,\mu_{1}}^{\diamondsuit}\times\dots\times{\mathcal{F}}_{G,C,\mu_{n}}^{\diamondsuit},

and also

(7.8)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}\bar{k}}\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\diamondsuit},

where on the right we mean the convolution
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{1}}\widetilde{\times}\dots\widetilde{\times}{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{n}}
.

Proof.

The description of the generic fiber via convolution is formal, and it is formal (using
Theorem

6.16
) that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}k}
is the convolution of the
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{i}}^{\diamondsuit}
. Using
Lemma

A.3
this convolution identifies with
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\diamondsuit}
.
∎

We are aiming to carefully write down certain “minimal”
{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>i,\diamondsuit}
-torsors

for some associated smooth connected groups

, such that

(7.9)

{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{1}}\times^{{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>1,\diamondsuit}}\dots\times^{{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>i-1,\diamondsuit}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}\times^{{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>i,\diamondsuit}}\dots\times^{{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>n-1,\diamondsuit}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{n}}

recovers the convolution local model
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}
.
We begin by introducing the group schemes
{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>i}
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\mu_{>i}=\mu_{i+1}+\dots+\mu_{n}
and let

be the quotient of
G_{C}
by the intersection of all conjugates of

. Then,

acts faithfully on

.

Furthermore, if we let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>i}
be the unique fppf quotient
19
19

19

Beware that the morphism of parahoric group schemes
{\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad}}
is not always an fppf surjection.
of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}
with generic fiber
G^{>i}_{\mathrm{ad},C}
, then
{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>i}
acts on
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{>i}}
, and its fibers are smooth, affine, connected with trivial center.

Proof.

The claims on
G_{\operatorname{ad},C}^{>i}
follow from
Lemma

7.1
.
The smooth group scheme quotient with the asserted properties obviously exist, due to
[
BT84
, Proposition 1.7.6]
, and it clearly inherits connected fibers. The generic fiber is clearly adjoint: apply semi-simplicity of
G_{\operatorname{ad}}
. However, it is more delicate to show that the special fiber is adjoint.

Assume without loss of generality (
Proposition

4.16
) that
G={\operatorname{Res}}_{F^{\prime}/F}G^{\prime}
is an adjoint
F
-simple group with
G^{\prime}
absolutely simple, and

a finite field extension of

. Let

be that parahoric over

associated with

. Then, a simple calculation reveals that

(7.10)

{\mathcal{G}}^{>i}={\operatorname{Res}}_{A_{i}/O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}_{A_{i}}^{\prime},

where

is the finite

-algebra obtained as the image of

in the product of those copies of

indexed by the support of

. Indeed, this smooth connected group scheme has the desired universal property and its special fiber is adjoint by
[

. (Notice that the reducedness hypothesis is superfluous for calculating the center.)
∎

Now, we come to the definition of the torsors.

Definition 7.4
.

The v-sheaf
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
is defined as the pushforward to
({\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>i})^{\diamondsuit}
of the natural
L^{+}_{O_{C}}{\mathcal{G}}
-torsor
\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}}
over
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
.

One might expect, just like in

, that these torsors have natural algebraic models over the ring of integers of some reflex field and this will be an integral part of our strategy.
Since the fibers are relatively easy to understand by the Demazure resolution, we now focus on the behavior over a certain
{\mathcal{G}}_{O_{C}}
-semi-orbit.

Recall that for any
{\lambda}\in W_{0}\cdot\mu
, where

is the Weyl group of
(G,S)

uniquely extends to a point

by properness of v-sheaf local models.

Definition 7.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\circ}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
be the unique sub-v-sheaf whose base change
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}^{\circ}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}
to

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}^{\circ}=\bigcup_{\lambda\in W_{0}\cdot\mu}{\mathcal{G}}_{O_{C}}^{\diamondsuit}\cdot[{\lambda}].

We recall that the elements

are the rational conjugates of

in

and correspond to the open Schubert orbits in the

-admissible locus, see the discussion after

.
Also, it is easy to see and left to the reader that the definition of

-split

whose connected Néron model

embeds in

.
The stabilizer of rational conjugates

is actually representable and well behaved.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}_{\lambda}^{-}
be the flat closure in
{\mathcal{G}}_{O_{C}}
of the repeller parabolic
P_{\lambda}^{-}\subset G_{C}
defined by
{\lambda}

(1)

-fixer of
{\lambda}
inside

.

(2)

{\mathcal{P}}_{\lambda}^{-}\to{\operatorname{Spec}}(O_{C})
is smooth affine with connected fibers.

Proof.

By topological flatness, it is clear that
{\mathcal{P}}_{\lambda}^{-,\diamondsuit}
fixes
{\lambda}
.
For dimension reasons, the special fiber of

is equal to the fixer in
{\mathcal{G}}_{O_{C}}
of
{\lambda}_{I}
in the affine flag variety

, which in particular shows that the special fiber of

is connected.
Having described the special fiber of

, we see that all

-valued points of the

-fixer of

inside

actually belong to the closed subgroup

, so it necessarily lies in that closed subgroup.

It suffices now to verify smoothness

20

20

Observe that the conjugation action of
{\lambda}
on

of

By

We want to uniquely characterize the left
{\mathcal{G}}^{\diamondsuit}_{O_{C}}
-equivariant right
{\mathcal{G}}^{>i,\diamondsuit}_{O_{C}}
-torsor

(7.13)

{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor},\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}:={\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}\times_{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}.

For this, we use the following abstract statement.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be any topos, and let
J,A\in\mathfrak{X}
be group objects.
Let
Z:=J/P
be an orbit for
J
.

(1)

The groupoid of left
J
-equivariant right
A
-torsors
{\mathcal{T}}
over
Z
is equivalent to the groupoid of right
A
-torsors
{\mathcal{S}}
over a terminal object equipped with a morphism of groups
\varphi_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon P\to\mathrm{Aut}_{A}({\mathcal{S}})
, with equivalence given by sending a
{\mathcal{T}}
to the fiber
{\mathcal{S}}:={\mathcal{T}}_{1\cdot P}
of
1\cdot P\in Z
with its action by
P
.

(2)

If
P
is self-normalizing, then for each left
J
-equivariant right
A
-torsor
f\colon{\mathcal{T}}{\to}Z
each morphism
\sigma\colon{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
, which is equivariant for
J
and
A
, is automatically a morphism of left
J
-equivariant right
A
-torsors, that is,
f\circ\sigma=f
.
If furthermore
\varphi_{{\mathcal{T}}}
has trivial centralizer, then
\sigma=\mathrm{id}
.

Note that if
{\mathcal{S}}
is trivial, then
\mathrm{Aut}_{A}({\mathcal{S}})\cong A
.

Proof.

For (1), it suffices to note that an inverse is given by sending a pair
({\mathcal{S}},P\to\mathrm{Aut}_{A}({\mathcal{S}}))
to the contracted product
J\times^{P}{\mathcal{S}}
.
For (2), one notes that by
A
-equivariance
\sigma
descents to an
J
-equivariant morphism of
Z
.
As
P
is self-normalizing, this morphism must be the identity.
∎

We can therefore conclude that
the left
{\mathcal{G}}^{\diamondsuit}_{O_{C}}
-equivariant
{\mathcal{G}}^{>i,\diamondsuit}_{O_{C}}
-right torsor
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor},\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
is governed by certain morphisms of groups

(7.14)

{\mathcal{P}}_{{\lambda}_{i}}^{\diamondsuit}\rightarrow({\mathcal{G}}^{>i}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}})^{\diamondsuit}

for all rational conjugates
\lambda_{i}
of
\mu_{i}
by applying
Lemma

7.7
.
In our situation, the generic fiber of
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor},\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
is quite well-understood, and thus we will apply the following general result describing extensions of a given left equivariant right torsor.

Lemma 7.8
.

We use the notation of
Lemma

7.7
.
Furthermore, let
Y\in\mathfrak{X}
be any object, and denote by a subscript
({\operatorname{-}})_{Y}
the base change to
Y
.
Assume that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}\to Z_{Y}
is a left
J_{Y}
-equivariant right
A_{Y}
-torsor with associated tuple
(\widetilde{{\mathcal{S}}},\varphi_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}\colon P_{Y}\to\mathrm{Aut}_{A_{Y}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{S}}}))
.
Then the groupoid of pairs of a left
J
-equivariant right
A
-torsors
{\mathcal{T}}
over
Z
with an isomorphism
{\mathcal{T}}_{Y}\cong\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
identifies with the groupoid of following data:
{\mathcal{S}}
an
A
-torsor over a terminal object of
\mathfrak{X}
, an identification
\gamma\colon{\mathcal{S}}_{Y}\cong\widetilde{{\mathcal{S}}}
, and a morphism of groups
\varphi\colon P\to\mathrm{Aut}_{A}({\mathcal{S}})
such that
\varphi_{Y}
agrees with
\varphi_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}
under the identification
\mathrm{Aut}_{A_{Y}}({\mathcal{S}}_{Y})\cong\mathrm{Aut}_{A_{Y}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{S}}})
induced by
\gamma
.

Proof.

This follows from
Lemma

7.7
.
∎

In our case, the
A
-torsors
\widetilde{{\mathcal{S}}}\cong A_{Y\times Z},{\mathcal{S}}\cong A_{Z}
we are interested in are trivial, and the morphism
A(\mathfrak{X})\to A(Y)
is injective.
Then
\varphi
is determined by
\gamma
(and
\varphi_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}
), and after fixing a section
z\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{S}}}(Y)\subset\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}(Y)
we get thus an injection from isomorphism classes of pairs
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{T}}_{Y}\cong\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}})
to
