by sending
({\mathcal{T}},{\mathcal{T}}_{Y}\cong\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}})
to the class of
z^{-1}y_{Y}\in A(Y)/A(\mathfrak{X})
, where
y\in{\mathcal{S}}(\mathfrak{X})\subset{\mathcal{T}}(Y)
is any section.
If
\varphi_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}
has trivial centralizer, the groupoid of such pairs is equivalent to the groupoid of left
J
-equivariant right
A
-torsors
{\mathcal{T}}
over
Z
, which are isomorphic to
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
over
Y
(but we do not fix such an isomorphism).

Applying these considerations to the orbits in
{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
with its left
{\mathcal{G}}_{O_{C}}^{\diamondsuit}
-equivariant right
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ad},O_{C}}^{>i}
-torsor
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor},\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{i}}
having generic fiber
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{G,\mu_{i},C}
, we need therefore to

•

fix base points over
O_{C}
in the orbits in
{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
,

•

fix
C
-points
\tilde{\lambda}_{j}\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{tor},{\diamondsuit}}_{G,C,\mu_{i}}
lying over the generic fibers of the chosen base points in
{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
,

•

find sections
y_{j}\in{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ,\mathrm{tor}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}(O_{C})
lying over the chosen base points in
{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
,

•

calculate the difference of
y_{j}^{-1}\tilde{\lambda}_{j}\in G_{\mathrm{ad},C}^{>i}(C)
, which yields the desired
n
classes modulo
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ad},O_{C}}^{>i}(O_{C})
.

The first point is easy as we can take the
[\lambda]\colon\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}^{\circ}
with
\lambda
running through the rational Weyl group conjugates of
\mu_{i}
. For the second point, we can fix a (suitable) uniformizer
\xi\in B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}^{+}(C)
and consider the images of the
\lambda(\xi)\in LG(C)
in
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{i},C}^{\mathrm{tor},\diamondsuit}
.
To state the outcome, we have to make the following definition.

Definition 7.9
.

Let
\nu\in X_{*}(T)
.
The different
\delta_{G}(\nu)
is the class in
T(C)/{\mathcal{T}}(O_{C})
of

(7.15)

\prod_{\sigma\neq 1}\nu^{\sigma}(\pi_{E}^{\sigma}-\pi_{E}),

where
F\subset E\subset C
is the reflex field of
\nu
,
\pi_{E}\in O_{E}
some uniformizer and
\sigma
varies over the non-trivial cosets in the quotient
{\operatorname{Gal}}_{F}/{\operatorname{Gal}}_{E}
of the absolute Galois groups.

For a uniformizer
\pi_{E}\in O_{E}
for a finite field extension
E/F
, contained in
C
, we denote by
\pi_{E}^{\flat}\in O_{C}^{\flat}
a chosen sequence of compatible
p^{n}
-roots of
\pi_{E}
.
Recall that
\xi_{E}:=\pi_{E}-[\pi_{E}^{\flat}]\in W_{O_{E}}(O_{C}^{\flat})
maps to a uniformizer of
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(C)
.
We can now define the
\tilde{\lambda}
as the images of
\lambda(\xi_{F})\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{tor,\diamondsuit}}_{{\mathcal{G}},C,\mu_{i}}(C)
for any
\lambda\in W_{0}\cdot\mu_{i}
.

Proposition 7.10
.

The v-sheaf
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor},\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},,O_{C},\mu_{i}}
is the unique left
{\mathcal{G}}_{O_{C}}
-equivariant right
{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>i}
-torsor over
{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}
with generic fiber isomorphic to
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{tor},\diamondsuit}_{{\mathcal{G}},C,\mu_{i}}
determined by the images of
\delta_{G}(\lambda)
in
G_{\operatorname{ad},C}^{>i}(C)/{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad},O_{C}}^{>i}(O_{C})
for
\lambda\in W_{0}\cdot\mu_{i}
(and the above choices for the
\tilde{\lambda}
’s).

Proof.

Let us fix some
\lambda\in W_{0}\cdot\mu_{i}
.
Consider the morphism

(7.16)

T^{\prime}:={\operatorname{Res}}_{E/F}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\rightarrow G

of algebraic groups induced by
\lambda
as follows: compose its Weil restriction
T^{\prime}:={\operatorname{Res}}_{E/F}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\operatorname{Res}}_{E/F}T_{E}
with the norm map
{\operatorname{Res}}_{E/F}T_{E}\to T
.
Note that
\lambda\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,E}\to G_{E}
can be reconstructed from this composition by restricting its base change to
E
to the first factor.
Set
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Res}_{O_{E}/O}\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
We will now construct a section
y\in L_{O}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(O_{C})
, whose image in
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
is the section
[\lambda]
, and then calculate
y^{-1}\tilde{\lambda}\in LT(C)
as necessary.

For this we claim that the element
\xi_{E}=\pi_{E}-[\pi_{E}^{\flat}]
becomes a unit after inverting
\xi_{F}=\pi_{F}-[\pi_{F}^{\flat}]
, thus giving rise to an element
y\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(W_{O_{E}}(O_{C}^{\flat}))\subset L_{O}{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}(O_{C})
. Indeed, let
P(X)=X^{d}+a_{1}X^{d-1}+\ldots+a_{d}
be the minimal polynomial of
\pi_{E}
over
F
, which is Eisenstein as
E/F
is totally ramified.
Then the norm of
\xi_{E}
in
W_{O_{F}}(O_{C}^{\flat})
equals

(7.17)

P([\pi_{E}^{\flat}])=[\pi_{E}^{\flat}]^{d}+a_{1}[\pi_{E}^{\flat}]^{d-1}+\dots+a_{d}.

Reducing modulo
\xi_{F}
, this element certainly vanishes because
[\pi_{E}^{\flat}]\equiv[\pi_{E}^{\flat}]^{\sharp}=\pi_{E}
modulo
\xi_{F}
.
On the other hand,
P([\pi_{E}^{\flat}])
is clearly a primitive element of degree
1
inside
W_{O}(O_{C}^{\flat})
, as
a_{d}\in\pi_{F}O^{\times}
.
Hence,
P([\pi_{E}^{\flat}])
and
\xi_{F}
generate the same principal ideal, see
[
BS19
, Lemma 2.24]
.

Let us now consider the generic fiber of the point
y
. For this we must pass to

(7.18)

B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(C)\otimes_{F}E=\prod_{\sigma}B_{{\mathrm{dR}}}^{\sigma}(C).

Here, notice that we are conjugating the natural
E
-structure on the corresponding factor of the right side by
\sigma
. Then the coordinate of
\xi_{E}
for
\sigma\neq 1
is a unit in
B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(C)
which reduces modulo the uniformizer
\xi_{F}
to
\pi_{E}^{\sigma}-\pi_{E}
. As for its coordinate on the
\sigma=1
factor, it must be a prime element, because so is the norm in
B_{\mathrm{dR}}(C)
, as seen in the previous calculation.
We can conclude that the generic fiber of the section
y
maps to
\lambda
, and that
\tilde{\lambda}^{-1}y
is
\delta_{G}(\lambda)
.
∎

The remark below will not be needed in the continuation.

Remark 7.11
.

Inspecting
Proposition

7.10
, one sees that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}^{\operatorname{tor},\circ}
is representable by a smooth
O_{E}
-scheme.
Indeed, the defining maps
\varphi_{\lambda}\colon{\mathcal{P}}^{-,\diamondsuit}_{\lambda}\to{\mathcal{G}}^{>i,\diamondsuit}_{\mathrm{ad},O_{C}}
are naturally algebraic over a finite unramified extension of
E
and so are their integral models by
[
BT84
, Proposition 1.7.6]
.
The considerations after
Lemma

7.8
then furnishes an algebraic space, and it is a scheme because it is a torsor over a scheme under an affine group scheme.

7.2.
Specialization maps

The aim of this subsection is to characterize the specialization map.
We are going to see that it is already determined by the semi-orbit.
In the following, we consider all pairs
({\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I})
where
{\mathcal{G}}
is a parahoric
O
-model of some reductive
F
-group
G
,
I
some finite index set and
\mu_{I}=(\mu_{i})_{i\in I}
is a sequence of minuscule coweights in
G_{C}
such that
\sum_{i\in I}\mu_{i}
is still minuscule (and so are all subsums).
Morphisms of such pairs
({\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I})\to({\mathcal{G}}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}_{J})
are given by morphisms of
O
-group schemes
{\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}
, surjections of sets
\operatorname{pr}\colon I\twoheadrightarrow J
such that, for all
j\in J
, the image of
\sum_{i\in\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(j)}\mu_{i}
in
G^{\prime}_{C}
lies in the conjugacy class of
\mu_{j}^{\prime}
.
This generalizes the functoriality considered in
Proposition

4.16
.
We denote
({\mathcal{G}},\mu_{I})
also by
({\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet})
if the index set
I
is understood.

Theorem 7.12
.

The specialization morphisms for all pairs
({\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet})
as above

(7.19)

\operatorname{sp}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}\colon|{\mathcal{F}}_{G,C,\mu_{\bullet}}|\rightarrow|{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k},\mu_{\bullet}}|

are the only functorial collection of continuous and spectral maps, whose restrictions to
{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}(O_{C})
agree with the natural maps.

Proof.

We are going to uniquely determine the values taken by
\operatorname{sp}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}
on the subset
{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu_{\bullet}}(K)
for a cofinal set of finite extensions
K/F
given by those Galois extensions that split
G
. This characterizes the map
\operatorname{sp}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}
by continuity with respect to the constructible topology. Indeed,
{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu_{\bullet}}
is a smooth rigid space defined over
E
, see
[
Gle20
, Theorem 1.4.35.]
.
21
21

21

If
\mu
is not minuscule it is not true that
\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
-points of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}
are dense for the constructible topology. Indeed, Bialynicki-Birula maps give a bijection between
\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
-points.

Having fixed a Galois extension
K/F
splitting
G
, we are however allowed to enlarge the parahoric group
{\mathcal{G}}
in order to compute these values. In particular, we may and do assume that
G={\operatorname{Res}}_{K/F}H
, where
H=G_{K}
is a split reductive group. Refining
\mu_{\bullet}
so that every element in the sequence is tiny allows us to conclude that every
K
-valued point of
{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu_{\bullet}}
extends to an
O_{K}
-valued point of the semi-homogeneous
{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}
by
Lemma

7.13
below.
∎

Lemma 7.13
.

Suppose
K/F
is Galois,
G={\operatorname{Res}}_{K/F}H
and all
\mu_{i}\in\mu_{\bullet}
are tiny. Then, we have an equality

(7.20)

{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{\bullet}}(O_{K})={\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu_{\bullet}}(K)

of sets induced by the natural morphism.

Proof.

Let us assume first that
K=F
. Then
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{H}}
is a parahoric model of a split reductive
F
-group and the right side can be given by the Iwasawa decomposition

(7.21)

{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu}(K)=\bigcup_{\lambda}{\mathcal{H}}(O_{K})\cdot{\lambda},

see
[
BT72
, Proposition 4.4.3]
.
Now, obviously the points of the form
\lambda
extend to integral points of
{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{{\mathcal{H}},\mu}
, due to the splitness assumption, and thus the same holds for its
{\mathcal{H}}(O_{K})
-orbits.

Now, consider an arbitrary finite Galois extension
K/F
. We get the result immediately for tiny coweights, as the
{\mathcal{G}}(O_{K})
-action on
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is via the parahoric subgroup
{\mathcal{H}}(O_{K})\subset H(K)
. In general, we use this to show the claim by an inductive procedure. Suppose one is given an element
(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\in{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu_{\bullet}}(K)
, such that each of the representatives
x_{j}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu_{j}}^{\operatorname{tor}}(K)
lies in
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{j}}^{\operatorname{tor},\circ}(O_{K})
for
j<i
. Now note that
x_{i}
is in the
{\mathcal{G}}^{>i}(O_{K})
-orbit of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{i}}^{\operatorname{tor},\circ}(O_{K})
, due to the
n=1
case and the fact that the
O_{C}
-section of
Proposition

7.10
descends to
O_{K}
, see also
Remark

7.11
. So we may replace it in the expression, and now the assumption holds for all
j<i+1
. After finitely many steps of this iteration, we get the claim.
∎

Remark 7.14
.

It follows by inspecting the proof of
Theorem

7.12
that in order to compute the specialization mapping, it is enough to consider Weil restrictions of split groups and their Iwahori models, see
7.16
.

Remark 7.15
.

He–Pappas–Rapoport
[
HPR20
, Conjecture 2.12]
conjecture that, for any fixed pair
({\mathcal{G}},\mu)
, there is at most one flat projective
O_{E}
-scheme equipped with an
{\mathcal{G}}_{O_{E}}
-action having the correct fibers, identified
{\mathcal{G}}
-equivariantly.
This is much stronger than
Theorem

7.12
above, since it makes no reference to convolution or functoriality.
Our approach is inspired by their conjecture in applying equivariant methods to pin down the specialization map.

7.3.
Comparison isomorphisms

In this subsection, we use our work from the previous ones to establish certain comparison isomorphisms between (at least some of) our local models and those that have appeared elsewhere, see
[
PZ13
,
Lev16
,
Lou19
,
FHLR22
]
. During this subsection, we shall work under the following:

Assumption 7.16
.

Given a pinned split simple adjoint group
(H,T_{H},B_{H},e_{H})
, let
G={\operatorname{Res}}_{K/F}H
with
K/F
an arbitrary finite extension, with
K_{0}/F
being the maximal unramified subextension. Also let
{\mathcal{I}}
be the standard Iwahori model with respect to the chosen pinning.

In order to prove
Theorem

7.21
, we need to compare
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}
to certain candidates
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
constructed in
[
FHLR22
, Definition 4.7]
, which are variations on the work of Levin
[
Lev16
]
.
Here,
\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}
is a
O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
-lift of
{\mathcal{I}}
along
t\mapsto\pi
, obtained by taking restriction of scalars along an ad hoc lift
O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]\to O_{0}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{u}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
of
O\to O_{K}
of the dilatation of
H\otimes O_{0}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{u}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
along
B_{H}\otimes O_{0}
concentrated in the
u
-divisor, see
[
PZ13
, Theorem 4.1]
and
[
MRR20
, Definition 2.1, Example 3.3]
. The various lifts
O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]\to O_{0}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{u}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
defined in
[
FHLR22
, Subsection 2.2]
are given by choosing uniformizers and lifting Eisenstein polynomials over
O_{0}
in such a way that they remain separable Eisenstein over both
k_{0}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
and
K_{0}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
.

One has a schematic Beilinson–Drinfeld Grassmannian
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}}^{\mathrm{sch}}
defined in terms of power series rings, classifying
\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}
-torsors over
R[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t-\pi}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
trivialized over
R(\hskip-1.99168pt({t-\pi})\hskip-1.99168pt)
, and admitting uniformization via loop groups
L_{O}^{\operatorname{sch}}\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}/L^{\operatorname{sch},+}_{O}\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}
.
The generic fiber is equivariantly isomorphic to the schematic affine Grassmannian
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}^{\operatorname{sch}}
over
F
, see
[
FHLR22
]
.
So we get an embedding
{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}^{\operatorname{sch}}|_{{\operatorname{Spec}}\,E}
for a minuscule coweight
\mu
.

Definition 7.17
.

The
O_{E}
-scheme
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
is defined as the absolute weak normalization of the flat closure of
{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu}
inside
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},O_{E}}^{\mathrm{sch}}
.
For a minuscule sequence
\mu_{\bullet}
of dominant coweights, we set
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},O_{E},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\operatorname{sch}}
as the convolution product of the
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},O_{E},\mu_{i}}^{\operatorname{sch}}
.
We define the
{\mathcal{I}}^{>i}_{O_{C}}
-torsor
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}^{\operatorname{sch,tor}}
by pushing forward the universal
L^{\operatorname{sch},+}_{O_{C}}\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}
-torsor under the natural projection.

The
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
are normal
O_{E}
-schemes by
[
FHLR22
, Theorem 4.10]
, so their formation commutes with base change to
O_{C}
. They also come with transition morphisms

(7.22)

{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},O_{C},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{N}}_{\tilde{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}},O_{C},\tilde{\mu}}^{\operatorname{sch}},

which are closed immersions, where
\tilde{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}}
is a further
O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
-lift defined with respect to the extension
\tilde{K}/K/F
, see
[
FHLR22
, Corollary 2.10]
, and
\tilde{\mu}
is the image of
\mu
in
\tilde{G}
. In what follows, we shall simply say they are functorial in
(\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu)
.

Our next goal is to compare the v-sheaves associated with
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\operatorname{sch}}
to
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}
. We start by recording what happens in the generic fiber.

Lemma 7.18
.

There are unique equivariant isomorphisms

(7.23)

{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch,tor}}|_{{\operatorname{Spec}}\,C}\cong{\mathcal{F}}_{G,C,\mu_{i}}^{\operatorname{tor}}

for each term
\mu_{i}
of the sequence
\mu_{\bullet}
.
They yield canonical equivariant isomorphisms

(7.24)

{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\operatorname{sch}}|_{{\operatorname{Spec}}\,C}\cong{\mathcal{F}}_{G,C,\mu_{\bullet}}

functorially in
(\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu_{\bullet})
.

Proof.

This follows by definition and uniqueness is ensured by
Lemma

7.7
.
∎

Next, we need to take care of the special fiber:

Proposition 7.19
.

There are unique equivariant isomorphisms

(7.25)

\left({\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu_{i}}^{\operatorname{sch,tor}}|_{{\operatorname{Spec}}\,\bar{k}}\right)^{\operatorname{perf}}\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu_{i},\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{tor}}

for each term
\mu_{i}
of the sequence
\mu_{\bullet}
.
They yield canonical equivariant isomorphisms

(7.26)

\left({\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\operatorname{sch}}|_{{\operatorname{Spec}}\,\bar{k}}\right)^{\operatorname{perf}}\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu_{\bullet},\bar{k}}

functorially in
(\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu_{\bullet})
.

Proof.

Set
{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime}=\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}\otimes k[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
, a standard Iwahori model of the connected reductive group
G^{\prime}={\operatorname{Res}}_{k_{0}(\hskip-1.99168pt({u})\hskip-1.99168pt)/k(\hskip-1.99168pt({t})\hskip-1.99168pt)}H
.
By
[
FHLR22
, Theorem 4.10]
, we have
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu,\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{sch,perf}}={\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\bar{k}}
.
Hence, the statement above is just a generalization of
Lemma

3.15
to convolution products.

Let
w\in\tilde{W}
be an element such that
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},w}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu_{i},\bar{k}}
and choose a Demazure resolution
\pi_{\dot{w}}\colon{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}\to{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},w}
.
We have to compare the following pullback square

(7.27)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}^{\operatorname{tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},w}^{\operatorname{tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},w}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\dot{w}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{{\mathcal{D}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\dot{w}}}

with its equicharacteristic counterpart. The bottow arrow was dealt with in
Lemma

3.15
and the second paragraph there applies verbatim to comparing the left arrow. We claim that these suffice to recover the remainder of the diagram.

Note that the vertical arrows affine morphisms, so they can be written via relative spectra, i.e. we have
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},\dot{w}}^{\operatorname{tor}}={\operatorname{Spec}}(p_{{\mathcal{D}},*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\operatorname{tor}}})
and
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},\bar{k},w}^{\operatorname{tor}}={\operatorname{Spec}}(p_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell},*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\operatorname{tor}}})
. On the other hand, we know that
\pi_{\dot{w},*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}={\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}}
, so the same equality holds for
\pi_{\dot{w}}^{\operatorname{tor}}
by flat base change, as the vertical arrows are perfectly smooth. But this means
p_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell},*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}^{\operatorname{tor}}}=\pi_{\dot{w},*}p_{{\mathcal{D}},*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\operatorname{tor}}}
, just as asserted.

Next, we show that the isomorphisms constructed above are unique. Without torsors, this has been verified in
Proposition

3.10
, so any automorphism respects the orbit part
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{I}},w}^{\circ,\operatorname{tor}}
. So we only have to verify the conditions of
Lemma

7.7
on centralizers of the transfer homomorphism
\varphi_{w}
. In this case, it is given by

(7.28)

\operatorname{int}(w^{-1})\colon L^{+}{\mathcal{I}}\cap wL^{+}{\mathcal{I}}w^{-1}\mapsto w^{-1}L^{+}{\mathcal{I}}w\cap L^{+}{\mathcal{I}}.

We see that the image of the right side in
({\mathcal{I}}_{\bar{k}}^{>i})^{\operatorname{perf}}
contains the image of
{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{perf}}
, where
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathcal{G}}
is the flat closure of some Borel
B\subset G
. However, inspecting the description of
{\mathcal{I}}_{\bar{k}}^{>i}
given in
Lemma

7.3
, we see that the centralizer must be trivial: indeed, it is contained in
{\mathcal{T}}_{\bar{k}}^{>i}
, which itself decomposes as a product of groups indexed by positive simple
{\mathcal{S}}_{\bar{k}}^{>i}
-roots
a
, acting faithfully on the corresponding
a
-root groups.

Finally, we must show that the isomorphisms just constructed are functorial with respect to
(\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu_{\bullet})
. This is easy for
\underline{{\mathcal{I}}}
, by uniqueness of equivariant automorphisms. As for
\mu_{\bullet}
, we appeal to
Proposition

3.2
and the calculation of Picard groups in
Theorem

3.8
and
Remark

3.9
to recover the Stein factorization of the proper surjection

(7.29)

{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu_{\bullet}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}.

In equicharecteristic, the Stein factorization is already
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}}
due to Zariski’s connectedness theorem applied to
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},O_{C},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
. Therefore, we get a new equivariant surjection
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}
, which becomes the identity after composing with the isomorphism
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}}
of
Lemma

3.15
, by the uniqueness proved in
Proposition

3.10
.
∎

The last comparison involves the semi-orbits.

Proposition 7.20
.

There are unique equivariant isomorphisms

(7.30)

({\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathcal{I}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}^{\operatorname{sch,\circ,tor}})^{\diamondsuit}\simeq{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},O_{C},\mu_{i}}^{\circ,\operatorname{tor}}

for each term
\mu_{i}
of the sequence
\mu_{\bullet}
. They yield canonical equivariant isomorphisms

(7.31)

({\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathcal{I}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\operatorname{sch},\circ})^{\diamondsuit}\simeq{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},O_{C},\mu_{\bullet}}^{\circ},

compatibly with those of
Lemma

7.18
and
Proposition

7.19
in the obvious sense.

Proof.

We have already identified the generic fibers of these v-sheaves, see
Lemma

7.18
. By
Lemma

7.8
, we reduce to calculating
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{C}
-valued points of the left side torsor and compare their residue to that of
Proposition

7.10
. The resulting isomorphism will then reduce to the expected isomorphisms over
\operatorname{{Spd}}k
obtained in
Proposition

7.19
, by uniqueness of equivariant automorphisms.

Now, we repeat the same calculation of
Proposition

7.10
, that already went back to Zhu, see
[
Zhu14
]
and
[
Lev16
, Proposition 4.2.8]
. Here, we work with the power series loop group
L_{O_{C}}^{\operatorname{sch}}\underline{{\mathcal{T}}}
in the setting of
[
FHLR22
, Subsection 2.2]
.
After refining
\mu_{\bullet}
, we may and do assume that each term
\mu_{i}\in\mu_{\bullet}
is concentrated in a single component of the Dynkin diagram of
G
.
There is a natural map

(7.32)

{\operatorname{Res}}_{O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{u}]\hskip-1.42262pt]/O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to\underline{{\mathcal{T}}}

induced by
{\lambda}_{i}
via taking the norm of restriction of scalars. Hence, we may and do assume that
\underline{{\mathcal{T}}}={\operatorname{Res}}_{O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{u}]\hskip-1.42262pt]/O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
. Note that here
O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{u}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
is a finite
O[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
-algebra, where
u
satisfies here an Eisenstein–Teichmüller type polynomial

(7.33)

u^{n}+a_{1}(t)u^{n-1}+\dots+a_{n}(t)=0

in
t
based on some fixed choices of uniformizers
\pi_{K}
for
K
and
\pi
for
F
, see
[
FHLR22
, Subsection 2.2]
. Now, we claim for any
\sigma\in{\operatorname{Gal}}_{F}
, the element
{\sigma}z_{u}=u-{\sigma}\pi_{K}
is a unit in
O_{C}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{u}]\hskip-1.42262pt][z_{t}^{-1}]
with
z_{t}=t-\pi
. Notice that its norm in
O_{C}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
equals

(7.34)

P(t)={\sigma}\pi_{K}^{n}+a_{1}(t){\sigma}\pi_{K}^{n-1}+\dots+a_{n}(t)

which is the product of
z_{t}
with a unit of
O_{C}[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
. Indeed, we calculate the value
P(\pi)=0
, also of the first derivative
P^{\prime}(\pi)\in O_{C}^{\times}
and apply the Taylor series inside
C[\hskip-1.42262pt[{t}]\hskip-1.42262pt]
. Finally, we notice that
{\sigma}z_{u}
reduces to the unit
\tau\pi_{K}-{\sigma}\pi_{K}
for all
\tau\neq{\sigma}
of
{\operatorname{Gal}}_{F}/{\operatorname{Gal}}_{K}
of the semi-field
C[\hskip-1.42262pt[{u}]\hskip-1.42262pt][z_{t}^{-1}]
, and to a prime element in the factor indexed by
\sigma
due to norm considerations. The desired claim has been shown.
∎

7.4.
The Scholze–Weinstein conjecture

In this subsection, we finally prove the Scholze–Weinstein conjecture, see
Theorem

7.21
and
Theorem

7.23
below.

We start by adressing the representability problem as in
[
Lou20
, Conjecture IV.4.18]
, which is one half of
[
SW20
, Conjecture 21.4.1]
. Recall that
F/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is a complete non-archimedean field with perfect residue field
k
,
G
is an arbitrary (connected) reductive
F
-group,
\mu
is a dominant coweight of
G_{C}
and
{\mathcal{G}}
an arbitrary parahoric
O
-model of
G
.

Theorem 7.21
.

Let
\mu
be minuscule. Then, there is a unique (up to unique isomorphism) flat, projective and weakly normal
O_{E}
-model
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
of the
E
-scheme
{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu}
endowed with a
{\mathcal{G}}_{O_{E}}
-action for which

(7.35)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch},\diamondsuit}\cong{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu},

prolonging
{\mathcal{F}}^{\diamondsuit}_{G,\mu}\cong{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}
equivariantly under
{\mathcal{G}}^{\diamondsuit}_{O_{E}}
.

Proof.

First of all, let us work under
7.16
.
We know that the geometric fibers of
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},O_{C},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch},\diamondsuit}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},O_{C},\mu}
are uniquely equivariantly isomorphic by
Lemma

7.18
,
Proposition

7.19
. By uniqueness, this commutes with the Galois action, so it descends to the fibers over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}
.

Furthermore, thanks also to
Proposition

7.20
,
Theorem

7.12
, and
Remark

7.14
, we know that the specialization maps

(7.36)

\operatorname{sp}\colon{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu}(C)\rightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}(\bar{k}),

arising respectively from the
p
-adic kimberlite
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},O_{C},\mu}
and
{\mathcal{N}}^{\operatorname{sch},\diamondsuit}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},O_{C},\mu}
must coincide. By continuity for the constructible topology, we obtain an equivariant isomorphism of specialization triples:

(7.37)

\big({\mathcal{N}}^{\operatorname{sch},\diamondsuit}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},E,\mu},{\mathcal{N}}^{\operatorname{sch},\diamondsuit}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu,k_{E}},\operatorname{sp}_{{\mathcal{N}}^{\operatorname{sch}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu}}\big)\cong\big({\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},E,\mu},{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu,k_{E}},\operatorname{sp}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}}\big)

associated with v-sheaves over
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}
. Observing that both v-sheaves satisfy the hypothesis of
Theorem

2.36
, we may directly appeal to it in order to get a necessarily equivariant isomorphism

(7.38)

{\mathcal{N}}^{\operatorname{sch},\diamondsuit}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu}\cong{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}.

Now maintain the part of
7.16
that refers to
G
, but suppose
{\mathcal{G}}
is now an arbitrary parahoric model such that
{\mathcal{I}}\to{\mathcal{G}}
.
We get a v-cover

(7.39)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}

and, paralelly, a scheme-theoretic projective cover

(7.40)

{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{I}}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}\to{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{G}}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}

by virtue of
[
FHLR22
, Section 4.2]
.
Therefore, it is enough to verify that the v-sheaf-theoretic equivalence relations coincide along the left side identification.

By construction, this reduces to
{\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{\operatorname{perf}}
-equivariantly compare the surjection

(7.41)

{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{I}},\mu}\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}

to the one obtained in equicharacteristic. This is entirely similar to what was done in
Lemma

3.15
and
Proposition

7.19
, so we omit it.

Finally, suppose that
G
is arbitrary. Thanks to
Proposition

4.16
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\operatorname{ad}},\mu_{\operatorname{ad}}}
after base change to
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}
, and decomposes into products, hence we may assume
G
is simple and adjoint. We can find a locally closed immersion

(7.42)

{\mathcal{G}}\to\tilde{{\mathcal{G}}},

where
\tilde{{\mathcal{G}}}
is a parahoric model of a Weil-restricted split form of
G
, which was treated in the previous paragraph.
Since we have an inclusion
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{\tilde{{\mathcal{G}}},\tilde{\mu}}
, it now suffices to take the absolute weak normalization of the flat closure of
{\mathcal{F}}_{G,\mu}
inside the scheme-theoretic local model attached to
(\tilde{{\mathcal{G}}},\tilde{\mu})
.
∎

Remark 7.22
.

Let us explain how representability can be proved for classical groups without resorting to the characterization of the specialization map found in
Theorem

7.12
. Indeed, for those groups we can directly understand the v-sheaves
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tor}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu_{i}}
by embedding them in a similar torsor attached to Weil-restricted
\operatorname{PGL}_{n}
. Those had been studied already by Pappas–Rapoport, see
[
PR05
, Proposition 5.2]
, and a careful analysis of the map in
[
SW20
, Proposition 21.6.9]
reveals that all proposed definitions coincide. The result follows by v-descent.

We have found certain finite type
O_{E}
-schemes
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\mathrm{sch}}
representing
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
, but we still do not know a lot about the geometry of its special fiber, which is the second part of
[
SW20
, Conjecture 21.4.1]
, see also
[
Lou20
, Conjecture IV.4.19]
.
We recall the canonical deperfection
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}}
of the
\mu
-admissible locus introduced in
Definition

3.11
and
Definition

3.14
.

Theorem 7.23
.

Under
1.9
and
1.13
, the special fiber of the
O_{E}
-scheme
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
of
Theorem

7.21
is uniquely
{\mathcal{G}}_{k_{E}}
-equivariantly isomorphic to the canonical deperfection of the
\mu
-admissible locus:

(7.43)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}|_{{\operatorname{Spec}}\,k_{E}}\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}}

In particular,
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
is normal, Cohen–Macaulay and has a reduced, weakly normal, Frobenius split special fiber.

Proof.

During the proof of
Theorem

7.21
, we already saw that the algebraic local models
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
are actually the
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{G}}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
constructed in
[
FHLR22
, Definition 4.7]
by a variation on the techniques of Pappas–Zhu
[
PZ13
]
, Levin
[
Lev16
]
and also the third author
[
Lou19
,
Lou20
]
. For this, we may pass to a finite unramified extension of
F
, so
G
is quasi-split and residually split, so that
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{G}}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
is defined (under
1.9
). Then, it embeds in a local model associated with a Weil-restricted split group, confer
[
FHLR22
, Corollary 2.10]
(this is where the
1.13
comes from). We conclude under the given hypothesis that the
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
are indeed normal, Cohen–Macaulay and have a Frobenius split special fiber by
[
FHLR22
, Theorem 4.10]
. Indeed, the special fiber of
{\mathcal{N}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{G}}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
is reduced equal to an equicharacteristic admissible locus
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{can}}
, which equivariantly identifies with
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}}
by
Lemma

3.15
.
∎

Remark 7.24
.

More generally, we may incorporate in
Theorem

7.23
the additional cases found in
[
Lou19
,
Lou20
]
in order to get the statement of
Theorem

7.23
in slightly bigger generality, see
Theorem

1.1
.
We leave the details to the reader.

To conclude, let us only use
Theorem

7.21
–and not resort to the construction of local models in
[
FHLR22
]
– in order to study the geometry of the special fiber of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
.

First of all, we know that the perfection of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu,k_{E}}^{\operatorname{sch}}
equals
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
by
Theorem

6.16
and fully faithfulness of
\diamondsuit
on perfect schemes, see
[
SW20
, Proposition 18.3.1]
.
By the weak normality property and
Lemma

7.6
, we conclude that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch}}
admits a smooth open subscheme
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch},\circ}
descending

(7.44)

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{\breve{E}},\mu}^{\operatorname{sch},\circ}=\bigcup_{\lambda}{\mathcal{G}}_{O_{\breve{E}}}/{\mathcal{P}}_{\lambda}^{-},

compare with the argument in
[
Ric16
, Corollary 2.14]
.
It follows that we have a natural morphism

(7.45)

{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu,k_{E}}^{\operatorname{sch}}.

The following conjecture is then the full
p
-adic coherence conjecture:

Conjecture 7.25
.

The map (
7.45
) is always an isomorphism.

In order to prove this in the very few cases left, see
1.9
and
1.13
and
Remark

7.24
, we envision the following steps.
First, one should remove
1.9
from
Theorem

3.16
.
Then, it would suffice to show that (
7.45
) is a closed immersion, removing also
1.13
.
This property can be verified after mapping to some suitable larger
{\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{{\mathcal{G}}},\tilde{\mu}}^{\mathrm{can}}
.
In particular, we see that both steps take place entirely in the special fiber.

Alternatively, we can give a purely v-sheaf-theoretic criterion for
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{sch}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu,k_{E}}
being reduced:

Lemma 7.26
.

Suppose
(\widehat{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{C},\mu}}_{/\bar{x}})_{\eta}
is connected for every
\bar{k}
-valued point
\bar{x}
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
. Then
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{sch}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu,k_{E}}
is geometrically reduced. Under
1.9
,
7.25
holds.

Proof.

By
Proposition

2.38
, we know that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\mathrm{sch}}
is normal.
In particular,
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{sch}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu,k_{E}}
is S1, but it must also be R0, as it contains a smooth dense open
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{can},\circ}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
.
So Serre’s criterion for reducedness furnishes the claim.
As for identifying the special fiber with
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}^{\operatorname{can}}
as per
7.25
, we appeal to
Theorem

3.16
, which computes the dimension of the vector spaces of global sections of ample line bundles.
∎

Finally, let us also mention the following conjecture, arising from
[
FHLR22
]
, on the singularities of
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{sch}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
:

Conjecture 7.27
.

The local model
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{sch}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
has pseudo-rational singularities.

8.
The test function conjecture

Throughout this section, we let
F/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be a finite field extension with ring of integers
O
and finite residue field
k
of cardinality
q
.
We fix an algebraic closure
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, an embedding
F\hookrightarrow\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and denote by
\Gamma={\operatorname{Gal}}(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/F)
the absolute Galois group of
F
with inertia subgroup
I
.
Let
G
be a reductive
F
-group with parahoric
O
-model
{\mathcal{G}}
.

Furthermore, fix a square root
\sqrt{q}
, an auxiliary prime
\ell\nmid q
and put
\Lambda={\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}(\sqrt{q})
.
We let
{}^{L}G=\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}\rtimes\Gamma
be the Langlands dual group viewed as a pro-algebraic
\Lambda
-group scheme. Each algebraic representation
V
of
{}^{L}G
furnishes, by choosing a quasi-inverse to the geometric Satake equivalence, a semi-simple perverse
\Lambda
-sheaf
{\operatorname{Sat}}(V)
of “weight zero” on the
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-affine Grassmannian
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\to\operatorname{{Spd}}F
.
Here
\sqrt{q}
is needed to define a square root of the
\ell
-adic cyclotomic character used when Tate twisting irreducible perverse sheaves supported on components of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
of odd parity to be of “weight zero”.
More precisely, for a dominant coweight
\mu
defined over
F
, we have

(8.1)

{\operatorname{Sat}}(V_{\mu})=i_{\mu,*}j_{\mu,!*}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mu}^{\circ}}\big(\textstyle{{\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle}\over 2}\big),

where
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}^{\circ}\overset{j_{\mu}\,}{\to}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\mu}\overset{i_{\mu}\,}{\to}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
and
V_{\mu}
is the irreducible representation of
{}^{L}G
of highest weight
\mu
.
Every simple object is of this form, up to taking a finite Galois orbit of
\mu
’s and tensoring with simple
\Lambda
-local systems on
\operatorname{{Spd}}F
of weight zero (corresponding to irreducible representations of
\Gamma
factoring through a finite quotient).

As in
Section

6.5
, we consider the functor of nearby cycles

(8.2)

\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}}}:=i^{*}Rj_{*}({\operatorname{-}})|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}\colon{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G},\Lambda)\rightarrow{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Lambda),

where
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}\xrightarrow{j}{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}}\xleftarrow{i}{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
are the inclusions of the geometric fibers.

Lemma 8.1
.

For every finite dimensional algebraic
{}^{L}G
-representation
V
, the sheaf of nearby cycles
\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}({\operatorname{Sat}}(V))
naturally defines an object in the category

(8.3)

{\operatorname{D}}_{\operatorname{cons}}\big([\underline{\Gamma}\backslash{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{sch}}],\Lambda\big)^{\operatorname{bd}}

of constructible
\Lambda
-sheaves with bounded support on the v-stack
[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{sch}}]
where
\underline{\Gamma}
denotes the associated group v-sheaf and the action on the schematic Hecke stack
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\operatorname{sch}}
is induced by the quotient map
\Gamma\to{\operatorname{Gal}}(\bar{k}/k)
.
In particular, the cohomology sheaves
\operatorname{R}^{n}\!\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}({\operatorname{Sat}}(V))
,
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
define
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
-equivariant, constructible
\Lambda
-sheaves with bounded support on
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}
equipped with an equivariant continuous
\Gamma
-action as defined in
[
SGA73
, Exposé XIII]
compatibly with the
L^{+}_{\bar{k}}{\mathcal{G}}
-action.

Proof.

The group
\Gamma
is identified with the group of continuous automorphisms of
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
over
F
.
Since the geometric fiber inclusions
i
and
j
are
\underline{\Gamma}
-equivariant, we obtain maps of v-stacks

(8.4)

{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G}=[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash{\operatorname{Hk}}_{G,{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}]\xrightarrow{[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash j]}[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}}]\xleftarrow{[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash i]}[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}],

and define the Galois equivariant nearby cycles functor
[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}}}]:=[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash i]^{*}R[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash j]_{*}({\operatorname{-}})
in analogy to (
8.2
).
Consider the quotient map
v\colon{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}\to[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash{\operatorname{Hk}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}]
.
We claim that the map
v^{*}[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}}}](\mathrm{Sat}(V))\to\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}}}(\mathrm{Sat}(V))
induced by base change is an isomorphism.

Note that we can not apply the base change theorem directly because
j
is not quasi-compact and
\Gamma
is only profinite.
Instead, we apply constant term functors, which commute with arbitrary base change:
By finite étale descent, we may and do assume that
G
is quasi-split and residually split, so that every
\breve{F}
-Borel descends to
F
.
Using the conservativity of constant term functors, see
Proposition

6.4
, we see again as in
Proposition

6.12
that the equivariant integral extension
R[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash j]_{*}\operatorname{Sat}(V)
is ULA over
[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}]
.
In particular, so is its pullback to
\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}
, which implies by
Proposition

6.12
that it equals
Rj_{*}(\operatorname{Sat}(V)|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}{\mathbb{C}}_{p}})
.
Restricting to geometric special fibers implies the claim.

It formally follows from
Proposition

A.5
and the construction of derived categories of
\Lambda
-sheaves that the comparison functor (
A.3
) induces an equivalence

(8.5)

{\operatorname{D}}([\underline{\Gamma}\backslash\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}^{\mathrm{sch}}],\Lambda)^{\mathrm{bd}}\cong{\operatorname{D}}([\underline{\Gamma}\backslash\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}],\Lambda)^{\mathrm{bd}}

under which constructible sheaves correspond to ULA sheaves, see
Proposition

6.7
.
Note that both properties are preserved and detected under the functor
v^{*}
, respectively its schematic counterpart.
Therefore,
[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}}}](\mathrm{Sat}(V))
naturally defines an object in the category (
8.3
) and its underlying sheaf is
\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}}}(\mathrm{Sat}(V))
.

For the final statement on the comparison with
[
SGA73
, Exposé XIII]
, we reduce to the case where
\Lambda
is a finite ring by the construction of categories of
\ell
-adic sheaves, see also (
6.2
).
Then, for any qcqs
k
-scheme
X
, the category of abelian
\Lambda
-sheaves on
(X_{\bar{k}})_{\textup{\'{e}t}}
equipped with a continuous
\Gamma
-action as in
[
SGA73
, Exposé XIII, Définition 1.1.2]
embeds fully faithfully into the category of abelian
\Lambda
-sheaves on
[\underline{\Gamma}\backslash X_{\bar{k}}]
inducing an equivalence on full subcategories of constructible sheaves.
Applying this to closed subschemes
X\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
implies the lemma.
∎

For every
\Phi\in\Gamma
, we define a function
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\operatorname{sch}}(k)\to\Lambda
by the formula

(8.6)

\tau_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}(x):=(-1)^{d_{V}}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{n}\operatorname{trace}\big(\Phi\;|\;\operatorname{R}^{n}\!\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}{\operatorname{Sat}}(V)_{\bar{x}}\big)

whenever
V
is irreducible and extend the definition to general
V
by linearity.
Here,
d_{V}=\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle
with
\mu
being the highest weight of
V
.
So the sign
(-1)^{d_{V}}
in (
8.6
) only depends on the parity of the connected component of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
that supports
{\operatorname{Sat}}(V)
.

Lemma 8.2
.

For every finite dimensional algebraic
{}^{L}G
-representation
V
and every
\Phi\in\Gamma
, the function
\tau_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}
naturally lies in the center of the parahoric Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}(G(F),{\mathcal{G}}(O))_{\Lambda}
.

Proof.

Lang’s lemma together with an approximation argument
[
RS20
, Lemma A.3]
implies that
\operatorname{H}^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(k,L^{+}{\mathcal{G}})
vanishes, so
{\operatorname{Hk}}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\operatorname{sch}}(k)={\mathcal{G}}(O)\backslash G(F)/{\mathcal{G}}(O)
.
As the function
\tau_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}
is supported on finitely many double cosets, it lies in
{\mathcal{H}}(G(F),{\mathcal{G}}(O))_{\Lambda}
.
Centrality follows from
Proposition

6.17
and the usual sheaf function dictionary.
∎

On the other hand, the theory of Bernstein centers defines another function:
Namely, for every choice of lift
\Phi\in\Gamma
of geometric Frobenius, we let
z_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}
be the unique function in the center of
{\mathcal{H}}(G(F),{\mathcal{G}}(O))_{\Lambda}
that acts on every smooth irreducible
{\mathcal{G}}(O)
-spherical representation
\pi
over
\Lambda
by the scalar

(8.7)

\operatorname{trace}\Bigl(s^{\Phi}(\pi)\;\big|\;V\Bigr),

where
s^{\Phi}(\pi)\in[\widehat{G}^{I}\rtimes\Phi]_{\operatorname{ss}}/\widehat{G}^{I}
is the Satake parameter for
\pi
with respect to
\Phi
constructed in
[
Hai15
]
.

Theorem 8.3
.

For every finite dimensional algebraic
{}^{L}G
-representation
V
and every choice of lift
\Phi
of geometric Frobenius, there is an equality

(8.8)

\tau_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}=z_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}

of functions in the parahoric Hecke algebra.

Proof.

As both sides of (
8.8
) are additive in
V
, we may freely assume that
V
is irreducible, and even further that
V|_{\widehat{G}\rtimes I}
is irreducible: otherwise both sides in (
8.8
) are zero (hence, equal) by elementary considerations, see
[
HR21
, Lemma 7.7]
.

Fix a maximal
F
-split torus
A\subset G
whose Néron model embeds in
{\mathcal{G}}
and a regular cocharacter
{\lambda}\colon{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to A
.
Then
{\lambda}
induces a minimal
F
-Levi
M
, respectively
F
-parabolic
P
in
G
.
Denote by
{\mathcal{M}}\subset{\mathcal{P}}
their flat closures in
{\mathcal{G}}
.
Then the constant terms morphism
[
Hai14
, Section 11.11]
induces an injective morphism on the centers of the parahoric Hecke algebras

(8.9)

\operatorname{ct}_{\mathcal{P}}\colon{\mathcal{Z}}(G(F),{\mathcal{G}}(O))_{\Lambda}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}(M(F),{\mathcal{M}}(O))_{\Lambda}.

As in
[
HR21
, Lemma 7.8, Equation (7.15)]
, one checks the formulas

(8.10)

\operatorname{ct}_{\mathcal{P}}\big(\tau_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}\big)=\tau_{{\mathcal{M}},V|_{{}^{L}M}}^{\Phi},\;\;\;\operatorname{ct}_{\mathcal{P}}\big(z_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}\big)=z_{{\mathcal{M}},V|_{{}^{L}M}}^{\Phi},

where
{}^{L}M=\widehat{M}\rtimes\Gamma
is viewed as a closed subgroup of
{}^{L}G
.
The second formula in (
8.10
) is straight forward.
The first formula in (
8.10
) is based on the isomorphism

(8.11)

\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}[\deg_{\mathcal{P}}]\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}\cong\Psi_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\operatorname{CT}_{P}[\deg_{P}]\colon{\operatorname{Sat}}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G},\Lambda)\to{\operatorname{D}}_{\operatorname{cons}}([\underline{\Gamma}\backslash{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{M}},\bar{k}}],\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}},

see
Proposition

6.13
, using that
\operatorname{CT}_{P}[\deg_{P}]
corresponds to the restriction of representations
V\mapsto V|_{{}^{L}M}
under the geometric Satake equivalence
[
FS21
, Section VI]
.
(We note that the sign
(-1)^{d_{V}}
in (
8.6
) appears when comparing
\operatorname{CT}_{\mathcal{P}}[\deg_{\mathcal{P}}]
and
\operatorname{ct}_{\mathcal{P}}
under the sheaf function dictionary, see also
[
HR21
, Lemma 7.2]
.)

Hence, we reduce to the case where
G=M
is a minimal
F
-Levi, so anisotropic modulo center, and
V|_{\widehat{G}\rtimes I}
is irreducible.
Let
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},V}
be the v-sheaf theoretic closure of the support of
{\operatorname{Sat}}(V)
in
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}
, a finite reunion of
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mu}
for
\mu
ranging over the highest weights of
V
.
The proof of
[
HR21
, Lemma 7.13]
is based on Iwahori-Weyl group combinatorics, hence applies to show that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}},V}
has only a single
\operatorname{{Spd}}k
-valued point
x_{V}
.
As
\Phi
lifts the geometric Frobenius, we can apply the Grothendieck-Lefschetz trace formula to
\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}{\operatorname{Sat}}(V)
viewed as an object in
{\operatorname{D}}_{\operatorname{cons}}([\underline{\Gamma}\backslash{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}}],\Lambda)^{\operatorname{bd}}
to compute

(8.12)

\operatorname{trace}\big(\Phi\;|\;\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}{\operatorname{Sat}}(V)_{\overline{x_{V}}}\big)=\operatorname{trace}\big(\Phi\;|\;\operatorname{H}^{*}({{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}},\bar{k}},\Psi_{\mathcal{G}}{\operatorname{Sat}}(V))\big)

Since
Rj_{*}({\operatorname{Sat}}(V)|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}\,{\mathbb{C}}_{p}})
is ULA by
Proposition

6.12
, the latter cohomology group is
\Gamma
-equivariantly isomorphic to

(8.13)

\operatorname{H}^{*}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\mathbb{C}}_{p}},{\operatorname{Sat}}(V))=\operatorname{H}^{*}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{*},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}},{\operatorname{Sat}}(V)),

where
G^{*}
is the unique quasi-split inner form of
G
.
We note that there is a canonical identification
{}^{L}G={{}^{L}}G^{*}
so that on Satake categories
\operatorname{Sat}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G},\Lambda)\cong\operatorname{Sat}({\operatorname{Hk}}_{G^{*}},\Lambda)
by
[
FS21
, Section VI]
. Let
{\mathcal{G}}^{*}
denote the parahoric corresponding to
{\mathcal{G}}
(necessarily, an Iwahori) and
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}}^{*},V}
the associated v-sheaf local model.
On the other hand, we know
[
Hai14
, Proposition 11.12.6]
that
z_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}
is supported at
x_{V}
with value

(8.14)

z_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}(x_{V})=\sum_{x\in{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{G}}^{*},V}(\operatorname{{Spd}}\,k)}z_{{\mathcal{G}}^{*},V}^{\Phi}(x).

Now assuming the test function conjecture for the pair
({\mathcal{G}}^{*},V)
, that is, assuming
z_{{\mathcal{G}}^{*},V}^{\Phi}=\tau_{{\mathcal{G}}^{*},V}^{\Phi}
, we can apply the Grothendieck-Lefschetz trace formula again to see that (
8.14
) equals the trace of
\Phi
on (
8.13
), up to the sign
(-1)^{d_{V}}
.
So
z_{{\mathcal{G}}^{*},V}^{\Phi}=\tau_{{\mathcal{G}}^{*},V}^{\Phi}
implies
z_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}=\tau_{{\mathcal{G}},V}^{\Phi}
.

Hence, we reduce to the case where
G=G^{*}
is quasi-split.
Now, the minimal Levi
M=T
is a maximal torus, so
(
8.10
)
reduces us to the case where
G=T
is a torus and
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{T}}
its connected locally finite type Néron model.
Without loss of generality, we assume that
V|_{\widehat{T}\rtimes I}
is irreducible.
Evidently,
T
is anisotropic modulo center so that both functions
\tau_{{\mathcal{T}},V}^{\Phi},z_{{\mathcal{T}},V}^{\Phi}
are supported at
x_{V}
.
Using (
8.12
), the ULA property of
Rj_{*}({\operatorname{Sat}}(V)|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}\,{\mathbb{C}}_{p}})
and
\operatorname{H}^{0}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,{\mathbb{C}}_{p}},{\operatorname{Sat}}(V))=V
, we see

(8.15)

\tau_{{\mathcal{T}},V}^{\Phi}(x_{V})=(-1)^{d_{V}}\operatorname{trace}\big(\Phi\;|\;V\big)

which equals
z_{{\mathcal{T}},V}^{\Phi}(x_{V})
because
d_{V}=0
.
This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

Theorem

8.3
implies
Theorem

1.2
.

Proof.

Let
\mu
be a conjugacy class of geometric cocharacters in
G
.
Denote by
E\subset\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
its reflex field with maximal unramified subextension
E_{0}/F
.
Their rings of integers are denoted by
O_{E}\supset O_{E_{0}}
with residue fields
k_{E}=k_{E_{0}}
and absolute Galois groups
\Gamma_{E}\subset\Gamma_{E_{0}}
.
For every
\Phi\in\Gamma_{E}
and
x\in{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}(k_{E})
, there is an equality

(8.16)

\operatorname{trace}\big(\Phi\;|\;\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{E}}{\operatorname{Sat}}(V_{\mu})_{\bar{x}}\big)=\operatorname{trace}\big(\Phi\;|\;\Psi_{{\mathcal{G}},O_{E_{0}}}{\operatorname{Sat}}(I_{E}^{E_{0}}(V_{\mu}))_{\bar{x}}\big),

where
I_{E}^{E_{0}}(V_{\mu})
is the induction to
\widehat{G}\rtimes\Gamma_{E_{0}}
of the
\widehat{G}\rtimes\Gamma_{E}
-representation
V_{\mu}
and
{\operatorname{Sat}}(V_{\mu})
,
{\operatorname{Sat}}(I_{E}^{E_{0}}(V_{\mu}))
the corresponding Satake sheaves on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}|_{{\operatorname{Spec}}\,E}
, respectively
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}|_{{\operatorname{Spec}}\,E_{0}}
.
Indeed, (
8.16
) follows from the commutation of nearby cycles with proper pushforward applied to the finite morphism
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}}|_{\operatorname{{Spd}}O_{E_{0}}}
, noting that it induces the induction of representations on Satake categories.

Now, we apply (
8.16
) to the pair
{\mathcal{G}}_{0}:={\mathcal{G}}_{O_{E_{0}}},V_{\mu,0}:=I_{E}^{E_{0}}(V_{\mu})
and any choice of lift
\Phi\in\Gamma_{E}\subset\Gamma_{E_{0}}
of geometric Frobenius to obtain

(8.17)

\tau_{{\mathcal{G}}_{0},V_{\mu,0}}^{\Phi}=z_{{\mathcal{G}}_{0},V_{\mu,0}}^{\Phi}.

The left hand side of (
8.17
) is equal to the function from
Theorem

1.2
by (
8.16
) and so is the right hand side of (
8.17
) by a similar equality
[
Hai18
, Lemma 8.1]
for inductions of representations along the totally ramified extension
E/E_{0}
.
That the function (
8.17
) takes, after multiplying by
(\sqrt{q_{E}})^{\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle}
, values in
{\mathbb{Z}}
independently of the choice of
\ell\neq p
,
\sqrt{q_{E}}
and
E\hookrightarrow\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
follows from
[
HR21
, Theorem 7.15]
where the statement is verified for the semi-simplified version of the right hand side of (
8.17
) without any assumptions on
({\mathcal{G}},\mu)
.
The same arguments apply here.
∎

Appendix A
Étale cohomology for v-stacks on schemes

In this section, we extend some parts of
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
to v-stacks on perfect schemes, see also
[
Wu21
, Appendix A]
.
Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
.
Let
X
be a perfect scheme over
k
, and let
X^{\diamondsuit}
be the associated v-sheaf from
Section

3.1
(under the tilting equivalence), that is, if
\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+})
is an affinoid perfectoid space over
\operatorname{{Spa}}(k,k)
and
X
affine, then

(A.1)

X^{\diamondsuit}(\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+}))=X({\operatorname{Spec}}(R)),

while for
X
general
X^{\diamondsuit}
is the analytic sheafification of the presheaf
\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+})\mapsto X({\operatorname{Spec}}(R))
.
We note that the functor
({\operatorname{-}})^{\diamondsuit}
does depend on
k
.

Fix a torsion ring
\Lambda
with
p\in\Lambda^{\times}
.
We let
{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda):=\widehat{{\operatorname{D}}}(X_{\textup{\'{e}t}},\Lambda)
be the left-completed étale derived
\infty
-category of
X
, see
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
.
By
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
there is a morphism

(A.2)

c_{X}\colon(X^{\diamondsuit})_{v}\to X_{\textup{\'{e}t}}

of sites (even to the proétale site of
X
), and the induced functor of
\infty
-categories

(A.3)

c_{X}^{\ast}\colon{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda)\to{\operatorname{D}}(X^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)

is fully faithful,
[
Sch17
, Proposition 27.2.]
.
In general the functor
c_{X}^{\ast}
is not essentially surjective, for example, on topological spaces
|X^{\diamondsuit}|\to|X|
is surjective, but very often not injective.

The functor
c_{X}^{\ast}
enjoys many compatibilities.
If
f\colon Y\to X
is a map of schemes, then
c_{X}^{\ast}\circ f^{*}\cong(f^{\diamondsuit})^{*}\circ c_{X}^{\ast}
and
c_{X}^{\ast}(A)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\Lambda}c_{X}^{\ast}(B)\cong c_{X}^{\ast}(A\otimes_{\Lambda}^{\mathbb{L}}B)
, see
[
Sch17
, Proposition 27.1.]
.
If
f\colon Y\to X
is separated perfectly of finite type, then
c_{X}^{\ast}\circ Rf_{!}\cong Rf^{\diamondsuit}_{!}\circ c_{X}^{\ast}
, see
[
Sch17
, Proposition 27.4.]
.
As we now justify
c_{X}^{\ast}
also preserves ULA-objects.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
S
be a qcqs perfect scheme in characteristic
p
, and let
f\colon X\to S
be a separated perfect scheme perfectly of finite presentation over
S
.
Let
A\in{\operatorname{D}}(X,\Lambda)
such that
A
is ULA with respect to
f
.
Then
c_{X}^{\ast}A
is ULA with respect to
f^{\diamondsuit}
, and
c_{X}^{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/S}(A)\cong\mathbb{D}_{X^{\diamondsuit}/S^{\diamondsuit}}(c_{X}^{\ast}A)
for the Verdier duals.

Proof.

As in
[
HS21
, Section 3]
, we let
\mathcal{C}_{S}
denote the
2
-category whose objects are schemes over
S
as in the hypothesis, and where morphisms from
X
to
Y
are given by objects in
{\operatorname{D}}(X\times_{S}Y,\Lambda)
.
Given two maps
A\in{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}_{S}}(X_{1},X_{2})
and
B\in{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}_{S}}(X_{2},X_{3})
, we define their composition
A\ast B\in{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}_{S}}(X_{1},X_{3})
by the formula

(A.4)

A\ast B:=R\pi_{1,3}{}_{!}(\pi_{1,2}^{*}A\otimes_{\Lambda}^{{\mathbb{L}}}\pi_{2,3}^{*}B).

By
[
HS21
, Proposition 3.4, Definition 3.2]
, the object
A\in{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}_{S}}(X,S)
is ULA with respect to
f
if and only if
A
is a left adjoint in
\mathcal{C}_{S}
.
Analogously, let
\mathcal{C}_{S^{\diamondsuit}}
denote the category considered in
[
FS21
, Section IV.2.3.3.]
.
By
[
FS21
, Theorem IV.2.23.]
, the object
c_{X}^{\ast}A\in{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}_{S}}(X^{\diamondsuit},S^{\diamondsuit})
is ULA with respect to
f^{\diamondsuit}
if it is a left adjoint in
\mathcal{C}_{S^{\diamondsuit}}
.
Now, we observe that the functors
c_{X}^{\ast}
can be promoted to a functor of
2
-categories
c^{*}\colon{\mathcal{C}_{S}}\to{\mathcal{C}_{S^{\diamondsuit}}}
by the rule
c^{*}X=X^{\diamondsuit}
and
c^{*}(A)=c_{X\times_{S}Y}^{*}(A)
for
A\in{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}_{S}}(X,Y)
.
Here, we use that
c^{*}
commutes with the required operations by
[
Sch17
, Propositions 27.1, 27.4]
.
But functors between 2-categories preserve the adjunctions between 1-morphisms which finishes the proof as the right adjoints are given by Verdier duals.
∎

We move on to study stacks.
Let
\mathrm{Ani}
be the category of anima (also called spaces,
\infty
-groups or Kan complexes).
By left Kan extension along the Yoneda embedding

(A.5)

\mathrm{SchPerf}_{k}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{SchPerf}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Ani}),

we can extend
22
22

22

We take care of the set-theoretic issues by fixing a suitable cardinal, large enough to allow all the examples that we are interested in.
the functors
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{-}},\Lambda),{\operatorname{D}}(({\operatorname{-}})^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)
using
\ast
-pullbacks and the natural transformation
c_{({\operatorname{-}})}^{\ast}
to contravariant functors
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{-}},\Lambda),{\operatorname{D}}(({\operatorname{-}})^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Kan}}
on
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{SchPerf}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Ani})
with values in symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-categories, sending colimits to limits.
More concretely, if a functor (also known as, higher prestack)

(A.6)

\mathfrak{X}\cong\underset{i}{{\operatorname{colim}}}X_{i}\in\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{SchPerf}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Ani})

is written as a colimit of representables, then

(A.7)

{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X},\Lambda)\cong\lim_{i}{\operatorname{D}}(X_{i},\Lambda)

and similarly for
{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Kan}}
.
By
[
Sch17
, Proposition 27.2]
and
[
BN19
, Lemma B.6]
(more precisely, its proof of
1.
), the natural transformation

(A.8)

c_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\ast}\colon{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X},\Lambda)\to{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Kan}}

is fully faithful.
Note that at this moment the right hand side is not the left completed derived étale category of some (higher) v-stack “
\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit}
” on
\mathrm{Perf}_{k}
, but depends on
\mathfrak{X}
and is defined abstractly (therefore, we have added the superscript
\mathrm{Kan}
).

Assume now that
\mathfrak{X}
is a stack (in
1
-groupoids) with representable diagonal such that there exists a v-cover by a perfect scheme
X\to\mathfrak{X}
. Then

(A.9)

\mathfrak{X}\cong{\operatorname{colim}}_{\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}X^{\bullet/\mathfrak{X}}

with the colimit of the Čech nerve of
X\to\mathfrak{X}
taken in
\mathrm{Ani}
-valued v-sheaves on
\mathrm{SchPerf}_{k}
.
Using
[
HS21
, Theorem 5.7]
, we get

(A.10)

{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X},\Lambda)\cong\lim_{\Delta}{\operatorname{D}}(X^{\bullet/\mathfrak{X}},\Lambda).

Indeed, by definition

(A.11)

{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X},\Lambda)\cong\lim_{U\to\mathfrak{X}}{\operatorname{D}}(U,\Lambda)

where the limit is taken over all perfect schemes with a morphism to
\mathfrak{X}
, and thus

(A.12)

\begin{matrix}&{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X},\Lambda)\\
=&\lim_{U\to\mathfrak{X}}{\operatorname{D}}(U,\Lambda)\\
\cong&\lim_{U\to\mathfrak{X}}\lim_{\Delta}{\operatorname{D}}(U\times_{\mathfrak{X}}X^{\bullet/\mathfrak{X}},\Lambda)\\
\cong&\lim_{\Delta}\lim_{U\to\mathfrak{X}}{\operatorname{D}}(U\times_{\mathfrak{X}}X^{\bullet/\mathfrak{X}},\Lambda)\\
\cong&\lim_{\Delta}{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}\times_{\mathfrak{X}}X^{\bullet/\mathfrak{X}},\Lambda)\\
\cong&\lim_{\Delta}{\operatorname{D}}(X^{\bullet/\mathfrak{X}},\Lambda),\end{matrix}

where the first isomorphism comes from
[
HS21
, Theorem 5.7]
applied to the covering
X\times_{\mathfrak{X}}U\to U
, the second isomorphism just commutes two inverse limits, and the last two isomorphisms use that
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{-}},\Lambda)
sends (by definition) colimits to limits and that
{\operatorname{colim}}_{U\to\mathfrak{X}}U\times_{\mathfrak{X}}X^{n/\mathfrak{X}}\cong X^{n/\mathfrak{X}}
.

Let
X^{\diamondsuit,\bullet/\mathfrak{X}}
be the simplicial v-sheaf obtained by applying the functor
({\operatorname{-}})^{\diamondsuit}
to
X^{\bullet/\mathfrak{X}}
.
Now assume additionally that the projection maps in
X^{\diamondsuit,\bullet/\mathfrak{X}}
are v-covers, and let
\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit}
be the colimit of
X^{\diamondsuit,\bullet/\mathfrak{X}}
in v-stacks on
\mathrm{Perf}_{k}
.
Then
\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit}
is a small v-stack with well-defined
{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)
, and actually the v-stackification of
\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+})\mapsto\mathfrak{X}({\operatorname{Spec}}(R))
.
By
[
Sch17
, Proposition 17.3]
, we can conclude that

(A.13)

{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)\cong\lim_{\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\operatorname{D}}(X^{\diamondsuit,\bullet/\mathfrak{X}},\Lambda).

Moreover, note that there exists a canonical morphism

(A.14)

{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Kan}}\to{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda),

which probably need not be an equivalence in general, but whose composite with
c^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is still fully faithful.

In general, it need however not be true that for a v-cover
Y\to X
of (perfect) schemes the map
Y^{\diamondsuit}\to X^{\diamondsuit}
is a v-cover of small v-sheaves.

Example A.2
.

Let
C
be a perfect, non-archimedean field over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and fix a pseudo-uniformizer
\pi_{C}\in C
.
Let

(A.15)

Z:=\{\textstyle{\frac{1}{n}}\ |\ n\in\mathbb{N}\}\cup\{0\}\subset[0,1]

with its subspace topology, which is profinite.
We consider the space of continuous functions

(A.16)

X:={\operatorname{Spec}}(C^{0}(Z,C_{\mathrm{disc}})),

where
C_{\mathrm{disc}}
is the field
C
equipped with the discrete topology.
Note that
|X|\cong Z
.
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
g_{n},h_{n}\colon Z\to C_{\mathrm{disc}}
be the locally constant functions with

(A.17)

g_{n}(1/m)=1/\pi_{C}^{m},h_{n}(1/m)=1

for
m\leq n
and
g_{n}(z)=h_{n}(z)=0
otherwise.
Let

(A.18)

Y_{n}\subset\mathbb{A}^{1}_{X}={\operatorname{Spec}}(C^{0}(Z,C_{\mathrm{disc}})[T])

be the vanishing locus of
h_{n}T-g_{n}
.
Then
Y_{n+1}\subset Y_{n}
, and we can set
Y:=\lim_{n}Y_{n}
which is a v-cover of
X
. Indeed, each
Y_{n}
is a v-cover, and inverse limits of v-covers between affine schemes are v-covers.
More concretely, each map
{\operatorname{Spec}}(V)\to X
with
V
a valuation ring must factor through a (closed) point of
X
, and then it suffices to see that
Y\to X
is surjective (it is bijective over
X\setminus\{0\}
, and
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{C_{\mathrm{disc}}}
over
0
).

We claim that
Y^{\diamondsuit}\to X^{\diamondsuit}
is not a v-cover. Set
R:=C^{0}(Z,C)
(now
C
given its valuation topology), and
R^{+}=C^{0}(Z,\mathcal{O}_{C})
.
The canonical map
C^{0}(Z,C_{\mathrm{disc}})\to R
defines a map

(A.19)

S:=\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+})\to X^{\diamondsuit},

which does not v-locally factor through
Y^{\diamondsuit}\subset(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{X})^{\diamondsuit}
.
Indeed, assume that
S^{\prime}\to S
is a v-cover with
S^{\prime}
affinoid and
S^{\prime}\to Y^{\diamondsuit}
a lift of
S\to X^{\diamondsuit}
.
Then the image of
S^{\prime}\to Y^{\diamondsuit}\times_{X^{\diamondsuit}}S\subset\mathbb{A}^{1,\mathrm{ad}}_{S}
must factor through some quasi-compact subset.
But over each point
z_{n}:=1/n\in Z\cong|S|
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that
Y^{\diamondsuit}\times_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\{z_{n}\}
is the point
1/\pi_{C}^{n}\in\mathbb{A}^{1,\mathrm{ad}}_{z_{n}}
, and in
\mathbb{A}^{1,\mathrm{ad}}_{S}
this set of points does not lie in a quasi-compact open.

Adding (perfect) finite presentation such examples cannot occur.

Lemma A.3
.

If
f\colon Y\to X
is a (perfectly) finitely presented map of perfect schemes and v-cover, then
f^{\diamondsuit}\colon Y^{\diamondsuit}\to X^{\diamondsuit}
is a v-cover.

Proof.

The functor
({\operatorname{-}})^{\diamondsuit}
preserves open covers, so we may assume that
Y\to X
are affine.
In this case, a (perfectly) finite presented v-cover is a cofiltered limit of v-covers between (perfect) affine schemes of (perfect) finite presentation over
{\operatorname{Spec}}(k)
, see
[
BS17
, Lemma 2.12]
.
As
f
is of (perfectly) finite presentation, we may assume that
f
is the base change of a v-cover between (perfect) affine schemes of (perfect) finite presentation over
k
.
The functor
({\operatorname{-}})^{\diamondsuit}
preserves fiber products, and base changes of v-cover of v-sheaves on
{\mathrm{Perf}}_{k}
are again v-covers.
Thus, we may reduce to the case that
Y,X
are of (perfect) finite presentation over
k
. Then the statement follows from
[
Gle20
, Proposition 2.2.9]
.

∎

We get the following consequence.

Lemma A.4
.

Assume
\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{Y}
are v-stacks on
\mathrm{SchPerf}_{k}
with representable diagonal of perfectly finite presentation, and that
\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{Y}
admit a (perfectly) finitely presented v-covers by a perfect schemes, which are of (perfect) finite presentation over
{\operatorname{Spec}}(k)
. Let
\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit}
be the v-stackification of the functor
\operatorname{{Spa}}(R,R^{+})\mapsto\mathfrak{X}({\operatorname{Spec}}(R))
on
\mathrm{Perf}_{k}
, and similarly for
\mathfrak{Y}
. Let
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
be a morphism of v-stacks.

(1)

Then the canonical morphism

(A.20)

{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Kan}}\to{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)

is an equivalence, and the composite (still denoted
c_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\ast}
)

(A.21)

{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X},\Lambda)\overset{c_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\ast}}{\to}{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Kan}}\cong{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)

is fully faithful.

(2)

The functors
c^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ f^{\ast},(f^{\diamondsuit})^{\ast}\circ c^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{Y}}
are naturally isomorphic.

(3)

If
\mathfrak{X}\cong[{\operatorname{Spec}}(k)/H]
for some perfectly finitely presented group scheme
H
over
k
, then the functor

(A.22)

{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X},\Lambda)\to{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We prove the first point.
From the assumptions on
\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit}
we can conclude that the morphisms
U\to\mathfrak{X}
of perfectly finite presentation such that
U
is of perfectly finite presentation over
k
are cofinal among all maps
V\to\mathfrak{X}
with
V
a perfect scheme.
In particular, in the definition of
{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Kan}}
one can replace the limit over all
V^{\prime}s
with a morphism to
\mathfrak{X}
by the limit over all
U^{\prime}s
with morphism to
\mathfrak{X}
. Using
Lemma

A.3
and the same argument as for
{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X},\Lambda)
, we can then conclude that
{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Kan}}\cong{\operatorname{D}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)
.
Fully faithfulness follows from fully faithfulness of
c_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\ast}
.
The second point follows by expressing the categories as limits over
\Delta
by choosing v-covers
X\to\mathfrak{X},Y\to\mathfrak{Y}
of perfectly finite presentation with
X,Y
of perfectly finite presentation over
k
, such that
X\to\mathfrak{X}\overset{f}{\to}\mathfrak{Y}
factors over
Y\to\mathfrak{Y}
.
For the last point, we note that
{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Spec}}(k),\Lambda)\cong{\operatorname{D}}(\operatorname{{Spa}}(k,k)^{\diamond},\Lambda)
as both identify naturally with the derived category of
\Lambda
-modules, see
[
FS21
, Theorem V.1.1]
.
Computing both sides via the Čech nerve of the covering
{\operatorname{Spec}}(k)\to[{\operatorname{Spec}}(k)/H]
, the statement follows from
[
BN19
, Lemma B.6]
.
∎

We want to apply the results of this appendix to the schematic Hecke stack.
Thus, assume that
O
is a complete discrete valuation ring with residue field
k
,
{\mathcal{G}}
a parahoric group scheme over
O
with generic fiber
G
and
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}=L_{k}G/L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
the partial affine flag variety for
{\mathcal{G}}
as in
Section

3.2
.
Let

(A.23)

\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},k}^{\mathrm{sch}}:=[L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}\backslash L_{k}G/L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}]

be the (schematic) Hecke stack where the quotient is taken in v-stacks on
\mathrm{SchPerf}_{k}
.
Then
\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},k}:=(\mathrm{Hk}^{\mathrm{sch}}_{{\mathcal{G}},k})^{\diamondsuit}
is the small v-stack on
{\mathrm{Perf}}_{k}
considered in
Section

6
.
We denote by

(A.24)

{\operatorname{D}}(\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},k}^{\mathrm{sch}},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{bd}},\ {\operatorname{D}}(\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},k},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{bd}}

the categories of objects with bounded support.

Proposition A.5
.

The categories
{\operatorname{D}}(\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},k}^{\mathrm{sch}},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{bd}},\ {\operatorname{D}}(\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},k},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{bd}}
are equivalent.

Proof.

Consider a closed substack

(A.25)

[L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}\backslash X]\subset\mathrm{Hk}^{\mathrm{sch}}_{{\mathcal{G}},k}

with
X\subset{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
a closed
L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
-stable subscheme, that is, a union of Schubert varieties.
By the argument of
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.4.1]
, the vanishing of the cohomology of the affine line over
k
implies that

(A.26)

{\operatorname{D}}([L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}\backslash X],\Lambda)\cong{\operatorname{D}}([H\backslash X],\Lambda)

for any perfectly finitely presented quotient
H
of
L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
by some congruence subgroup whose action on
X
is trivial.
By
Lemma

A.4
, we have, abusing notation, a natural fully faithful functor

(A.27)

c^{\ast}_{[H\backslash X]}\colon{\operatorname{D}}([H\backslash X],\Lambda)\to{\operatorname{D}}([H\backslash X]^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)\cong{\operatorname{D}}([H^{\diamondsuit}\backslash X^{\diamondsuit}],\Lambda),

and we claim that this functor is an equivalence.
We prove this by induction on the number of Schubert strata contained in
X
.
Let
i\colon Y\subset X
be a closed
H
-stable (perfect) subscheme with non-empty open complement
j\colon U\to X
, for which
[H\backslash U]
is a disjoint union of classifying stacks for closed subgroups of
H
.
By
Lemma

A.4
, we have

(A.28)

{\operatorname{D}}([H\backslash U],\Lambda)\cong{\operatorname{D}}([H^{\diamondsuit}\backslash U^{\diamondsuit}],\Lambda),

and by induction induction hypothesis

(A.29)

{\operatorname{D}}([H\backslash Y],\Lambda)\cong{\operatorname{D}}([H^{\diamondsuit}\backslash{Y}^{\diamondsuit}],\Lambda).

Let us note that as in
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
the functors
c^{\ast}_{[H\backslash X]},c^{\ast}_{[H\backslash U]},c^{\ast}_{[H\backslash Y]}
admit right adjoints
Rc_{[H\backslash X],\ast}
,
Rc_{[H\backslash U],\ast}
,
Rc_{[H\backslash Y],\ast}
, and it suffices to see that
Rc_{[H\backslash X],\ast}
is conservative.
It is formal that

(A.30)

[H\backslash j]^{\ast}\circ Rc_{[H\backslash X],\ast}\cong Rc_{[H\backslash U],\ast}\circ[H\backslash j]^{\diamondsuit,\ast},

where
[H\backslash j]\colon[H\backslash U]\to[H\backslash X]
denotes the morphism induced by
j
. More precisely, there exists a natural morphism from the left hand side to the right hand side, and it suffices to see that the morphims induced on the left adjoints is an isomorphism. If
T\to[H\backslash X]
is a
v
-cover with
T\to{\operatorname{Spec}}(k)
of morphism of schemes of finite type, then it suffices (by
Lemma

A.3
) to prove the statement on the isomorphism of left adjoints after pullback to
T^{\diamondsuit}
. Here, the functor
c_{T}^{\ast}
on the étale derived categories is induced by a morphism of topoi, and then (
A.30
) follows by general base change to open subtopoi.

Let
A\in{\operatorname{D}}([H\backslash X]^{\diamondsuit},\Lambda)
such that
Rc_{[H\backslash X],\ast}(A)=0
.
Then we deduce
[H\backslash j]^{\diamondsuit,\ast}(A)=0
because
Rc_{[H\backslash U],\ast}
is an equivalence.
In particular,
A\cong[H\backslash i]_{\ast}^{\diamondsuit}[H\backslash i]^{\diamondsuit,\ast}(A)
. Now note that

(A.31)

[H\backslash i]_{\ast}\circ Rc_{[H\backslash Y],\ast}\cong Rc_{[H\backslash X],\ast}\circ[H\backslash i]^{\diamondsuit}

as follows by adjunction from
[H\backslash i]^{\ast}\circ c_{[H\backslash X]}^{\ast}\cong c_{[H\backslash Y]}^{\ast}\circ[H\backslash i]^{\diamondsuit,\ast}
. We can conclude that

(A.32)

[H\backslash i]_{\ast}Rc_{[H\backslash Y],\ast}([H\backslash i]^{\diamondsuit,\ast}A)=0,

which implies
[H\backslash i]^{\diamondsuit,\ast}(A)=0
because
[H\backslash i]_{\ast}
is conservative and
Rc_{[H\backslash Y],\ast}
an equivalence. This implies that
A=0
as desired.

The equivalence
c^{\ast}_{[H\backslash X]}
is natural with respect to inclusions
X\to X^{\prime}
of
H
-stable subsets, and morphism
H^{\prime}\to H
of quotients of
L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
(which act trivially on
X
).
More precisely, from
c^{\ast}_{[H\backslash X]}
we get an equivalence

(A.33)

{\operatorname{D}}([L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}\backslash X],\Lambda)\cong{\operatorname{D}}([(L_{k}^{+}{\mathcal{G}})^{\diamondsuit}\backslash X^{\diamondsuit}],\Lambda)

using
[
FS21
, VI.4.1]
, and then we can pass to the colimits of both sides along closed
L^{+}_{k}{\mathcal{G}}
-stable subschemes of
{{\mathcal{F}}\!\ell}_{{\mathcal{G}}}
.
Then the left hand side is
{\operatorname{D}}(\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},k}^{\mathrm{sch}},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{bd}}
while the right hand side is
{\operatorname{D}}(\mathrm{Hk}_{{\mathcal{G}},k},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{bd}}
.
∎
